Static Bank · Section 1Constitution, Polity and Amendments28 items · 20 ★ must-learn · 8 ☆ good to know

1.1 Constitution at a glance

1/28★ Must-learnBasic facts

  • Adopted 26 Nov 1949 → in force 26 Jan 1950 (Constitution Day = 26 Nov)
  • Drafting Committee chair → Dr B R Ambedkar | Constituent Assembly president → Dr Rajendra Prasad
  • Original: 395 Articles, 22 Parts, 8 Schedules → now 25 Parts (Part VII repealed), 12 Schedules, about 448 numbered Articles
  • Preamble amended once → 42nd Amendment 1976: added Socialist, Secular, Integrity
Exam angle

Which amendment changed the Preamble?

Remember

One Preamble change = 42nd, the "mini-Constitution".

Mnemonic

Parts I–VI: "Union Citizens Rights Direct Duties; Union States" = Union, Citizenship, Rights, Directives, Duties (IVA), Union (V), States (VI).

Mnemonic

Part IX family: 73 → 74 → 97 = Panchayat → Municipality → Cooperative = IX, IXA, IXB.

Mnemonic

Parts XIV–XX: "Services Tribunals Elect Special Language, Emergency Misc. Amend" = XIV Services, XIVA Tribunals, XV Elections, XVI Special, XVII Language, XVIII Emergency, XIX Misc, XX Amendment.

Remember

XVIII = Emergency | XX = Amendment (Art 368) | XII = money.

1.3 All 12 Schedules

3/28★ Must-learnSchedules

No.Content
1States and Union Territories
2Salaries and emoluments: President, Governors, Speakers, judges, CAG

5Administration of Scheduled Areas and Scheduled Tribes
6Tribal Areas in Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram
7Union / State / Concurrent Lists (98 / 59 / 52)

9Land reform and other laws protected from judicial review (1st Amendment)
10Anti-defection (52nd)
11Panchayat powers, 29 subjects (73rd)
12Municipality powers, 18 subjects (74th)
Mnemonic

1–6 "SEO-RAT": States, Emoluments, Oaths, Rajya Sabha, Areas (Scheduled), Tribal areas.

Mnemonic

7–12 "Lazy Lions Leave Dogs; Pandas Mourn": Lists, Languages, Land, Defection, Panchayat, Municipality.

Remember

11th = Panchayat = 29 | 12th = Municipality = 18 (the bigger number goes with the earlier amendment, 73rd).

  • 8th Schedule growth → 14 original; Sindhi (21st, 1967); Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali (71st, 1992); Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santhali (92nd, 2003)

1.4 Part III: Fundamental Rights (Arts 12–35)

4/28★ Must-learnSix rights, Article ladder

Mnemonic

Ladder 5-4-2-4-2-1: Equality 5 Arts (14–18), Freedom 4 (19–22), Exploitation 2 (23–24), Religion 4 (25–28), Cultural-Educational 2 (29–30), Remedies 1 (32).

RightArtWhat
General12, 1312 = "State" defined; 13 = laws violating FRs are void
Equality14Equality before law, equal protection
15No discrimination (religion, race, caste, sex, place of birth)
16Equal opportunity in public employment
17Untouchability abolished
18Titles abolished
Freedom19Six freedoms: speech; assembly; association, unions, cooperative societies; movement; residence; profession
20Protection in conviction (no double jeopardy, no self-incrimination)
21Life and personal liberty
21AEducation, age 6–14 (86th)
22Protection against arrest and detention
Against Exploitation23No trafficking, no forced labour
24No child labour (under 14) in hazardous work
Religion25Freedom of conscience, practise, propagate
26Manage religious affairs
27No tax to promote a religion
28Religious instruction in educational institutions
Cultural and Educational29Protection of minorities' language and culture
30Minorities can set up educational institutions
Remedies32Move Supreme Court directly ("heart and soul", Ambedkar)
Misc33, 34, 35Armed forces; martial law; Parliament's power to legislate on FRs
  • Art 31 (property) repealed by 44th Amendment 1978 → now Art 300A (legal right)
Mnemonic

Six freedoms of 19(1) = "SAAMRP": Speak, Assemble, Associate, Move, Reside, Profess.

5/28★ Must-learnWrits (Art 32 SC, Art 226 High Courts)

WritMeaningUse
Habeas Corpus"produce the body"Illegal detention
Mandamus"we command"Public officer to do duty
ProhibitionstopHigher court tells lower court to stop a proceeding
Certiorari"to be certified"Quash a lower court's order
Quo Warranto"by what authority"Challenge right to hold public office
Mnemonic

"Ha Ma Pro Cer Quo". Prohibition prevents (during), Certiorari cancels (after).

Remember

Art 32 = SC, Fundamental Rights only. Art 226 = HC, FRs and other legal rights, so wider.

1.5 Part IV: DPSP (Arts 36–51) and Part IVA

6/28★ Must-learnDPSP, article by article

  • Art 36 = "State" defined | Art 37 = not enforceable in court, yet fundamental in governance

40Village panchayatsGandhian
41Work, education, public assistanceSocialist
42Just and humane work, maternity reliefSocialist
43Living wage; cottage industriesGandhian
43AWorkers' participation in management (42nd)Socialist
43BCooperative societies (97th)Gandhian

Mnemonic

Gandhian = 40, 43, 43B, 46, 47, 48: "Village, Cottage, Cooperative, Weaker, Prohibition, Cow".

Mnemonic

Liberal = 44, 45, 48A, 49, 50, 51: "UCC, Early, Environment, Monuments, Judiciary, Peace".

Remember

42nd added 39A, 43A, 48A ("A-A-A": legal Aid, workers' participation, environment). Group split follows the standard textbook classification.

7/28★ Must-learnFundamental Duties (Art 51A, Part IVA)

  • 42nd Amendment 1976 (Swaran Singh Committee) → 10 duties
  • 11th added by 86th Amendment 2002 → parent/guardian provides education to child aged 6–14
  • Idea borrowed from the USSR constitution

Mnemonic

President: 52-53-54 = Exists, Executive power, Elected. 61 = impeachment.

Remember

72 = pardon | 74 = aid and advise | 76 = Attorney General | 123 = President's ordinance.

10/28★ Must-learnParliamentary committees and sessions

CommitteeMembersSinceKey points
PAC (Public Accounts Committee)22 (15 LS + 7 RS)1921Examines CAG audit reports and appropriation accounts; chair from the opposition by convention since 1967; a Minister cannot be a member; one-year term
Estimates Committee30 (Lok Sabha only)1950Called the "continuous economy committee"; examines estimates, suggests economies; chair from the ruling party
COPU (Committee on Public Undertakings)22 (15 LS + 7 RS)1964Examines public sector undertakings (PSUs); set up on the Krishna Menon Committee recommendation
DRSCs (Departmentally Related Standing Committees)24 committees, 31 members each (21 LS + 10 RS)1993Scrutinise demands for grants and Bills of each ministry; one-year term; Ministers cannot be members
Government Assurances15 in each House1953Tracks promises made by Ministers on the floor
Subordinate Legislation15 in each House1953Checks rules and regulations framed under Acts
Privileges15 in each House1952Examines breaches of privilege
Business Advisory15 in each House1952Allots time for business; chaired by Speaker / Chairman
EthicsLS: 15; RS: 10LS 2000; RS 1997Examines ethics complaints against members
  • Sessions: summoned by the President (Art 85); gap between two sittings must not exceed 6 months → at least two sessions a year
  • Three usual sessions: Budget (longest), Monsoon, Winter
  • Quorum = one-tenth of total members (Art 100); joint sitting on deadlock → Art 108, presided by the Lok Sabha Speaker (not for Money Bills or Constitution Amendment Bills)
Mnemonic

Numbers: PAC 22, COPU 22, Estimates 30 (LS only); DRSC 31 × 24. "PAC-COPU twins at 22; Estimates alone at 30."

Exam angle

Which financial committee has only Lok Sabha members, and how many?

1.8 Parts VIII–XIII: UTs, local bodies, relations, finance

12/28★ Must-learnUTs, Panchayat, Municipality, Cooperatives

  • Part VIII (239–242): UTs; 239AA = Delhi (69th Amendment, NCT)
  • Part IX: Panchayat (73rd; in force 24 Apr 1993) → Gram Sabha, 3 tiers, 5-year term, State Election Commission (243K), State Finance Commission (243I)
  • Part IXA: Municipality (74th; in force 1 Jun 1993)
  • Part IXB: Cooperatives (97th) → after SC (Rajendra N Shah, 2021) valid only for multi-state cooperative societies
Remember

Panchayati Raj Day = 24 April. Panchayat has its own State EC and State FC.

13/28☆ Good to knowCentre–State relations (Part XI, Arts 245–263)

  • 246 = distribution of legislative power (Seventh Schedule); 248 = residuary power → Union
  • 249 = Rajya Sabha (2/3 present and voting) can empower Parliament to legislate on a State List subject
  • 253 = implementing treaties; 256–257 = executive control
  • 262 = inter-state water disputes; 263 = Inter-State Council

14/28★ Must-learnFinance (Part XII, Arts 264–300A)

ArtSubject

1.12 Citizenship, census, tribal areas, bodies and civilian honours

24/28★ Must-learnCitizenship (Part II, Arts 5–11)

ArtProvision
5Citizen at commencement: domiciled in India and born here, or a parent born here, or ordinarily resident for 5 years
6Migrants from Pakistan: before 19 Jul 1948 (citizen on arrival); after → registration
7Migrants to Pakistan after 1 Mar 1947 who returned on a permit
8Persons of Indian origin living abroad: register via consulate
9Voluntarily acquiring foreign citizenship → loses Indian citizenship
10Continuance of citizenship
11Parliament may regulate citizenship by law → Citizenship Act, 1955
Citizenship Act 1955Detail
Acquisition (5 modes)Birth, Descent, Registration, Naturalisation, Incorporation of territory
Loss (3 ways)Renunciation, Termination (voluntarily taking another citizenship), Deprivation (by Centre: fraud, disloyalty, etc.)
Birth26 Jan 1950 – 30 Jun 1987: born in India = citizen; from 1 Jul 1987 one parent must be a citizen; from 3 Dec 2004 the other parent must not be an illegal migrant
Descent (abroad)Father a citizen (to 10 Dec 1992); either parent from 10 Dec 1992; register at consulate within 1 year
Naturalisation12 months continuous residence + 11 of preceding 14 years; 5 years for CAA 2019 groups
Amendments1986 (birth rules); 2003 (defined "illegal migrant"; barred them); 2005 (OCI category); 2015 (PIO and OCI merged into OCI cardholder); 2019 CAA
CAA 2019Hindus, Sikhs, Buddhists, Jains, Parsis, Christians from Afghanistan, Bangladesh, Pakistan who entered by 31 Dec 2014; residence needed 11 → 5 years
  • Single citizenship (unlike the US federal model); dual citizenship not allowed; illegal migrants cannot become citizens
  • Citizenship is a Union List subject → only Parliament legislates
Mnemonic

Acquire = BDRNI (Birth, Descent, Registration, Naturalisation, Incorporation). Lose = RTD (Renunciation, Termination, Deprivation).

25/28☆ Good to knowPart X: Scheduled and Tribal Areas (Fifth vs Sixth Schedule)

Fifth ScheduleSixth Schedule
Article244(1)244(2) and 275(1)
AreaScheduled Areas in 10 states: Andhra Pradesh, Telangana, Chhattisgarh, Gujarat, Himachal Pradesh, Jharkhand, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Odisha, RajasthanTribal Areas in 4 states: Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram
Who declaresPresident declares Scheduled Areas (after consulting the Governor)Governor organises and reorganises autonomous districts and regions
BodyTribes Advisory Council (TAC): up to 20 members, three-fourths ST MLAs; advisory only10 Autonomous District Councils (plus Regional Councils): up to 30 members (26 elected + 4 nominated), term 5 years
PowersGovernor can modify or stop laws for the area; lower self-ruleCouncils have legislative, executive and judicial powers; higher self-rule
Panchayati RajExtended by PESA 1996 (Panchayats Extension to Scheduled Areas Act; Bhuria Committee, 1995)Part IX does not apply; Councils perform those functions
  • Part X = Arts 244–244A (Scheduled and Tribal Areas); Art 244A allows an autonomous state within Assam (added 1969)
Mnemonic

Fifth → 10 states, President, advisory TAC. Sixth → 4 NE states, Governor, Councils with real power (26 + 4 = 30).

26/28☆ Good to knowConstitutional, statutory and executive bodies

TypeBody (source)
ConstitutionalElection Commission (Art 324); UPSC and State PSCs (Arts 315–323); Finance Commission (Art 280); CAG (Art 148); Attorney General (Art 76); Advocate General (Art 165); GST Council (Art 279A); NCSC (Art 338); NCST (Art 338A); NCBC (Art 338B, 102nd Amendment 2018); Special Officer for linguistic minorities (Art 350B)
Statutory (created by an Act)NHRC (Protection of Human Rights Act 1993; set up 12 Oct 1993); National Commission for Women (Act 1990; set up 1992); CIC (RTI Act 2005); CVC (statutory since 2003); Lokpal (Act 2013); NGT (Act 2010); CCI (Competition Act 2002); TRAI (1997); SEBI; NFRA (Companies Act 2013, s.132)
Executive / non-statutoryNITI Aayog (cabinet resolution, 1 Jan 2015; replaced Planning Commission of 1950)
  • NHRC chairperson: Justice V. Ramasubramanian (took charge 30 Dec 2024)
  • UPSC: first Public Service Commission 1 Oct 1926; named UPSC on 26 Jan 1950; Art 316 appointment, Art 317 removal only after Supreme Court inquiry
Remember

Constitutional = has its own Article. Statutory = has its own Act. NITI Aayog has neither.

27/28★ Must-learnCensus and Census 2027

  • Legal base: Census Act 1948 and Census Rules 1990; run by the Registrar General and Census Commissioner (Ministry of Home Affairs); census is a Union subject (Art 246, Entry 69, Seventh Schedule)
  • First census 1872 (non-synchronous); every 10 years since 1881
  • Census 2027: two phases → houselisting from 1 Apr 2026 (about 5–6 months; buildings and households) → population enumeration in Feb 2027
  • Reference date 1 Mar 2027; 1 Oct 2026 for snow-bound areas (Ladakh, J&K, Himachal Pradesh, Uttarakhand)
  • First digital census; caste data beyond SC/ST to be collected for the first time since Independence (last complete caste count: 1931)
  • Home Secretary asked States not to change administrative boundaries till 31 Mar 2027
  • 16th census in the series and the 8th since Independence (last census 2011); outlay ₹11,718.24 crore; caste enumeration approved by the Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs on 30 Apr 2025; houselisting helpline 1855; app and portal in 16 languages
  • Section 15 of the Census Act keeps individual data confidential (not open to RTI or as evidence in court)
Mnemonic

1872 first → 1881 every decade → 1931 last caste count → 2027 digital + caste.

Exam angle

Under which Act is the Census conducted, and which Entry of the Union List covers it?

28/28☆ Good to knowBharat Ratna, Padma awards and gallantry honours

HonourGiven forNotes
Bharat Ratna"Exceptional service of the highest order" in any fieldInstituted 1954; max 3 a year; PM recommends to the President; no formal nominations
Padma VibhushanExceptional and distinguished servicePadma names took effect 1955
Padma BhushanDistinguished service of a high order
Padma ShriDistinguished service
  • Padma cap: 120 a year (excluding posthumous and foreign / NRI awardees); Padma Awards Committee headed by the Cabinet Secretary
  • Announced on the eve of Republic Day; conferred by the President; no cash prize (certificate and medal)
  • Art 18 abolishes titles; the Supreme Court held (Balaji Raghavan, 1996) that these awards are honours, not titles
  • Gallantry ladder: wartime → Param Vir Chakra, Maha Vir Chakra, Vir Chakra; peacetime → Ashoka Chakra, Kirti Chakra, Shaurya Chakra
Mnemonic

Padma rank = V-B-S: Vibhushan > Bhushan > Shri; all below Bharat Ratna.

Static Bank · Section 2RBI, Monetary Policy and Banking Basics36 items · 28 ★ must-learn · 8 ☆ good to know

2.1 RBI at a glance

1/36★ Must-learnFoundation facts

  • Idea → Hilton Young Commission 1926 (Royal Commission on Indian Currency and Finance) | Ambedkar's The Problem of the Rupee shaped its thinking
  • Act → RBI Act 1934 | began work 1 Apr 1935 | HQ Kolkata → Mumbai (1937)
  • Private shareholders' bank → nationalised 1 Jan 1949
  • Statutory body for central banking: issuer of notes, banker to government, banker's bank, regulator, forex manager
  • First Governor → Sir Osborne Smith | first Indian Governor → C D Deshmukh (1943)
  • Logo → Lion and palm tree (from the East India Company double mohur; lion replaced the tiger)
  • Accounting year → July–June, changed to April–March from 2020-21 (nine-month transition)
Exam angle

Which commission led to RBI? Which Act governs it? When was it nationalised?

Remember

1926 idea → 1934 Act → 1935 start → 1937 Mumbai → 1949 nationalised. Say it as "26-34-35-37-49".

2/36☆ Good to knowGovernors since 1997

GovernorTerm
Bimal Jalan1997–2003

D Subbarao2008–2013
Raghuram Rajan (23rd)2013–2016
Urjit Patel (24th)2016–2018
Shaktikanta Das (25th)2018–2024
Sanjay Malhotra (26th)from 11 Dec 2024 (3-year term)
Mnemonic

"Just Read Some Really Useful Dictionaries, Man": Jalan, Reddy, Subbarao, Rajan, Urjit, Das, Malhotra.

2.2 Structure, functions and Acts

3/36★ Must-learnCentral Board and local boards

  • Central Board → up to 21 members = Governor + up to 4 Deputy Governors + 10 nominated directors (Centre) + 4 directors from local boards + 2 government officials
  • Four local boards → Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, New Delhi
  • Governor and DGs appointed by the Centre; Governor's term fixed by appointment (usually 3 years)
  • Subsidiaries → DICGC, BRBNMPL (note printing), RBI Innovation Hub (RBIH, 2021), IFTAS
  • Training institutions → IDRBT (Hyderabad), NIBM (Pune), CAB (Pune)
Mnemonic

21 = 1 (Gov) + 4 (DG) + 10 + 4 + 2.

4/36★ Must-learnKey laws and sections

Law / SectionWhat
RBI Act 1934RBI's parent law
RBI Act s.7Centre can issue directions to RBI (after consulting the Governor)
RBI Act s.22RBI's sole right to issue notes
RBI Act s.42Cash Reserve Ratio
RBI Act s.45ZBMonetary Policy Committee
RBI Act s.45IA / 45LNBFC registration / RBI's power to regulate NBFCs
RBI Act s.47Allocation of surplus to Centre
Banking Regulation Act 1949Bank licensing and regulation (earlier Banking Companies Act 1949; extended to cooperative banks in 1966)
BR Act s.5(b) / s.22Definition of "banking" / licence
BR Act s.24Statutory Liquidity Ratio (max 40%)
BR Act s.35A / s.45RBI's directions to banks / amalgamation and moratorium
FEMA 1999Forex management (replaced FERA 1973; effective 1 Jun 2000)
Payment and Settlement Systems Act 2007RBI regulates payment systems
Credit Information Companies Act 2005Regulates credit bureaus (CIBIL, Equifax, Experian, CRIF High Mark)
Government Securities Act 2006Issue and management of G-secs
SARFAESI Act 2002Recovery without court
DICGC Act 1961Deposit insurance
IBC 2016Insolvency and bankruptcy
Negotiable Instruments Act 1881Cheques, bills, promissory notes
Remember

CRR sits in the RBI Act (s.42); SLR sits in the Banking Regulation Act (s.24).

5/36★ Must-learnRBI functions in one view

  • Currency → sole issuer of notes (except ₹1 note, issued by the Finance Ministry; coins also from the government)
  • Banker → to Centre and states, and "banker's bank" (lender of last resort)
  • Regulator → banks, NBFCs, payment systems, cooperative banks, credit bureaus
  • Monetary authority → inflation targeting through the MPC
  • Forex manager → FEMA and reserves
  • Debt manager → G-secs, T-bills, SDLs (State Development Loans)
  • Development → financial inclusion and literacy
Mnemonic

"CBR MFD D": Currency, Banker, Regulator, Monetary, Forex, Debt, Development.

2.3 Monetary Policy Committee and inflation targeting

6/36★ Must-learnMPC at a glance

  • Source → RBI Act s.45ZB (inserted by Finance Act 2016) | suggested by Urjit Patel Committee 2014
  • Constituted 29 Sep 2016 | first meeting 3–4 Oct 2016
  • Members → 6 = 3 from RBI (Governor as ex-officio chair; DG in charge of monetary policy; one officer nominated by the Board) + 3 external (appointed by the Centre on a search committee's recommendation; 4-year term, no reappointment)
  • Meets → at least 4 times a year (in practice 6 times, bimonthly) | quorum → 4, with the Governor (or in his absence the DG) present
  • Decision by majority; Governor has a casting vote only in a tie | minutes published after 14 days
  • Before 2016 → Governor decided, with a Technical Advisory Committee only advising
Mnemonic

3 + 3 = 6, chair casts the tiebreak. "4-4-14": 4-year term, 4 minimum meetings, 4 quorum, 14 days for minutes.

7/36★ Must-learnFlexible Inflation Targeting (FIT)

  • Legal basis → RBI Act (amended 2016) + Monetary Policy Framework Agreement (Feb 2015)
  • Target → 4% CPI (Combined) with tolerance band ±2% (2% to 6%)
  • Target is set by the Centre, in consultation with RBI, every 5 years
  • Failure → inflation outside the band for 3 consecutive quarters → RBI must report to the Centre with reasons and remedial steps
  • Index used → CPI-Combined (rural + urban), not WPI
  • Sections → 45ZA (Centre sets the target every 5 years, in consultation with RBI) | 45ZN (failure report)
  • Same 4% ± 2% retained in March 2021 and again by a Department of Economic Affairs Gazette notification of 25 Mar 2026 → valid 1 Apr 2026 to 31 Mar 2031
Exam angle

What is the target and band? What counts as failure?

Remember

4 ± 2 | failure = 3 quarters in a row outside 2–6%.

2.4 Instruments of monetary policy

8/36★ Must-learnRate corridor (Liquidity Adjustment Facility)

InstrumentWhatPosition vs repo
Repo rateRBI lends to banks against G-secs (policy rate)Anchor
SDF (Standing Deposit Facility, Apr 2022)Banks park surplus with RBI, no collateralRepo − 0.25% (floor)
MSF (Marginal Standing Facility, 2011)Overnight emergency borrowing, can dip into SLRRepo + 0.25% (ceiling)
Bank RateLong-term lending rate; penalty basisSame as MSF
Reverse repoRBI absorbs liquidity against G-secs; separate fixed-rate windowBelow SDF; not the floor since 2022
  • LAF introduced June 2000 (Narasimham Committee II) | corridor width = 50 bps
  • SDF replaced the fixed-rate reverse repo as the floor
Mnemonic

Floor-Anchor-Ceiling: SDF → Repo → MSF (−25, 0, +25). Bank Rate = MSF.

Remember

Repo = money comes to the bank. Reverse repo = money goes to RBI. SDF needs no collateral, that is the difference.

9/36★ Must-learnCRR, SLR and other tools

ToolKey points
CRR (Cash Reserve Ratio)Share of NDTL (Net Demand and Time Liabilities) held as cash with RBI; no interest; s.42 RBI Act; statutory floor and ceiling removed by 2006 amendment
SLR (Statutory Liquidity Ratio)Held by banks themselves in cash, gold or G-secs; s.24 BR Act; statutory ceiling 40%
OMO (Open Market Operations)RBI buys G-secs → injects liquidity; sells → absorbs
VRR / VRRRVariable Rate Repo / Reverse Repo (auctions; durations beyond overnight)
MSS (Market Stabilisation Scheme, 2004)Sterilisation of forex inflows through special bills
Operation TwistSimultaneous buy of long-term and sale of short-term G-secs
Selective credit controlMargin requirements, caps on sector credit
Moral suasionInformal guidance
Remember

CRR → cash with RBI, earns nothing. SLR → kept with the bank, in liquid assets.

Mnemonic

Quantitative = CRR, SLR, repo, reverse repo, OMO, MSF. Qualitative = margin, rationing, moral suasion.

10/36★ Must-learnMoney supply measures

MeasureComposition
M0 (Reserve / High-powered money)Currency in circulation + bankers' deposits with RBI + other RBI deposits
M1 (Narrow money)Currency with public + demand deposits + other RBI deposits
M2M1 + savings deposits with post office
M3 (Broad money)M1 + time deposits with banks
M4M3 + all post office deposits (no longer reported)
  • M3 = main aggregate | Money multiplier = M3 ÷ M0
  • Higher CRR/SLR → lower multiplier
  • RBI also publishes newer measures (NM1, NM2, NM3) with liquidity aggregates L1 to L3, adopted after a 2020 review
Mnemonic

"Narrow to Broad, 1-2-3-4": each step adds a bigger, less liquid layer. M3 = M1 + time deposits.

2.5 Lending-rate regimes

11/36★ Must-learnBPLR → Base Rate → MCLR → EBLR

RegimeStartIdea
BPLR (Benchmark Prime Lending Rate)2003Each bank's own benchmark; sub-BPLR lending allowed, so opaque
Base Rate1 Jul 2010Floor; no lending below it
MCLR (Marginal Cost of funds based Lending Rate)1 Apr 2016Based on marginal cost of funds; better transmission
EBLR (External Benchmark-linked Lending Rate)1 Oct 2019Linked to an external benchmark (repo, T-bill, etc.); mandatory for new floating-rate retail and MSE loans
  • EBLR spread over benchmark is fixed by the bank; reset at least once every 3 months
Mnemonic

"Big Boys Make Everything": BPLR, Base, MCLR, EBLR (2003, 2010, 2016, 2019).

14/36★ Must-learnTypes of banks

TypeKey facts
Scheduled banksIn the Second Schedule of RBI Act 1934
Public Sector Banks (PSBs)Government owned (12 after 2020)
Small Finance Banks (SFBs)Serve small borrowers; 75% priority sector; capital ₹200 crore
Payments BanksNo lending; deposit cap ₹2 lakh per customer; Nachiket Mor Committee
Cooperative banksUCBs (Urban Cooperative Banks), State and District Central Cooperative Banks; dual regulation (RBI + state/registrar)
Local Area BanksSmall, district-level; 1996 scheme
Foreign banksBranches or wholly owned subsidiaries
Remember

Payments banks accept deposits but cannot lend. SFBs lend, with 75% to priority sectors.

15/36★ Must-learnNBFC classification and Scale-Based Regulation

LayerWho sits here
Base (NBFC-BL)Non-deposit-taking NBFCs with assets below ₹1,000 crore; plus NBFC-P2P, NBFC-AA, NOFHC and NBFCs with no public funds or customer interface
Middle (NBFC-ML)All deposit-taking NBFCs (NBFC-D) of any size; non-deposit-taking with assets ₹1,000 crore or more; plus SPD, IDF-NBFC, CIC, HFC, NBFC-IFC
Upper (NBFC-UL)Identified by RBI through a scoring method; the top 10 NBFCs by asset size are always here
Top (NBFC-TL)Kept empty; filled only if RBI sees much higher systemic risk in an Upper-layer NBFC
  • Scale-Based Regulation (SBR) from 1 Oct 2022 (RBI circular of 22 Oct 2021); replaced the old deposit-taking / systemically-important split
  • Always Base: P2P, AA, NOFHC. Never Base: NBFC-D, CIC, IFC, HFC. Always Middle: SPD, IDF-NBFC. Government-owned NBFCs: Base or Middle, not Upper
  • Activity types: ICC (Investment and Credit Company), IFC (Infrastructure Finance Company), CIC (Core Investment Company), IDF-NBFC (Infrastructure Debt Fund), MFI (Microfinance Institution), Factors, MGC (Mortgage Guarantee Company), AA (Account Aggregator), P2P (Peer-to-Peer), HFC (Housing Finance Company)
  • NBFC vs bank: no demand deposits, no cheque issue, not in the payment and settlement system as a bank, no DICGC cover
Mnemonic

Layers = BMUT: Base, Middle, Upper, Top (empty). Base cut-off = ₹1,000 crore.

Exam angle

From which date does Scale-Based Regulation apply, and which layer is kept empty?

16/36★ Must-learnBanking Laws (Amendment) Act, 2025

LayerDetail
PassedLok Sabha Dec 2024; Rajya Sabha Mar 2025
Acts amendedReserve Bank of India Act 1934; Banking Regulation Act 1949; State Bank of India Act 1955; Banking Companies (Acquisition and Transfer of Undertakings) Acts 1970 and 1980 → 19 amendments
NomineesUp to 4 (was 1) for accounts, deposits and lockers; simultaneous (share in %, totalling 100%) or successive (order of precedence); in force 1 Nov 2025
Substantial interestDirector's interest threshold ₹5 lakh → ₹2 crore (unchanged since 1968)
Cooperative banksDirector tenure 8 → 10 years (excluding chairperson and whole-time directors)
AuditorsPublic sector bank boards now set statutory auditors' remuneration (earlier fixed by RBI with the Centre)
Unclaimed moneyPublic sector banks may transfer unclaimed shares, interest and bond money to the IEPF (Investor Education and Protection Fund)
Reporting datesTied to the 15th and the last day of the month (earlier last Friday / alternate Fridays)
In force1 Aug 2025 for Sections 3–5 and 15–20
Remember

Substantial interest: ₹5 lakh → ₹2 crore. Nominees: 1 → 4. Co-op director: 8 → 10 years.

17/36★ Must-learnBankers' Books Evidence Act, 2026

  • Replaces the Bankers' Books Evidence Act, 1891; in force 1 Oct 2026
  • Electronic or digital bank records are admissible if a true copy, no unauthorised change, no tampering; certification by manual, digital or electronic signature
  • A certified copy proves the entry → no need to produce the original book
  • Bank officers cannot be made to produce books or testify (where the bank is not a party) unless a written court order records a "special cause"
  • Applies to courts, arbitration, inquiries and investigations under the BNSS (Bharatiya Nagarik Suraksha Sanhita) 2023; "bank" includes banking companies, post office savings banks and money order offices; Centre can extend it to other financial entities

18/36☆ Good to knowRegional Rural Banks (RRBs)

ItemDetail
OriginNarasimham Working Group (1975) → ordinance 26 Sep 1975 → five RRBs on 2 Oct 1975; RRB Act 1976 (Act No. 21 of 1976)
First fivePrathama Bank (Moradabad, UP; Syndicate Bank, the first RRB); Gorakhpur Kshetriya Gramin Bank (SBI); Haryana Kshetriya Gramin Bank (PNB); Gaur Gramin Bank (UCO); Jaipur-Nagaur Anchalik Gramin Bank (UCO)
OwnershipCentre 50% : State 15% : Sponsor bank 35%
Count82 (2010) → 67 (2013) → 56 (2016) → 43 (2020) → 28 (1 May 2025, "One State One RRB"; 22,966 branches)
RecapitalisationGovernment support to lift CRAR to at least 9%
Mnemonic

Ownership 50-15-35 = Centre-State-Sponsor. Born on 2 Oct 1975 (Gandhi Jayanti).

2.7 Prudential norms

19/36★ Must-learnLicence cancellation: Section 22 and Paytm Payments Bank

Section (Banking Regulation Act 1949)Meaning
22(1)Licence needed to carry on banking business (e.g. SMBC subsidiary approval)
22(3)Grounds for refusing or cancelling: (b) affairs detrimental to depositors, (c) management prejudicial to public interest, (e) no useful purpose in continuing
22(4)RBI may cancel the licence
5(b)Defines "banking": accepting deposits for lending or investment, repayable on demand
  • Paytm Payments Bank → licence cancelled by RBI under Section 22(4), effective 24 Apr 2026; barred from "banking" (Section 5(b)) and Section 6 business; bank had enough liquidity to repay all deposits
  • History → onboarding of new customers stopped 11 Mar 2022; business curbs on 31 Jan and 16 Feb 2024
Exam angle

Under which Section can RBI cancel a bank's licence? (22(4), Banking Regulation Act 1949)

20/36☆ Good to knowPayments banks and SFB-to-universal bank conversion

TopicKey facts
Payments banksSet up on the Nachiket Mor Committee advice; public limited company; minimum paid-up capital ₹100 crore; deposit cap ₹2 lakh per customer; cannot lend
First and newestAirtel Payments Bank was first to launch (2017); India Post Payments Bank (IPPB) launched 1 Sep 2018, the first fully government-owned
Five payments banksAirtel, IPPB, Fino, Jio, NSDL
SFB → universal bankScheduled status with 5-year record, listed, net worth ₹1,000 crore, CRAR met, GNPA ≤ 3% and NNPA ≤ 1% in the last two years
Who convertedOnly AU Small Finance Bank so far; Ujjivan SFB's application was returned (to diversify its loan book)
Mnemonic

SFB conversion: 5 – 1000 – 3 – 1 (years, ₹ crore, GNPA %, NNPA %).

21/36★ Must-learnBasel norms

Basel IBasel IIBasel III
Year198820042010 (after the 2008 crisis)
FocusCredit riskThree pillars: capital, supervisory review, market disciplineCapital quality, buffers, liquidity, leverage
Min capital8%8%8% (India: 9% CRAR)
  • BCBS (Basel Committee on Banking Supervision) → set up 1974 under the BIS (Bank for International Settlements, 1930, Basel, Switzerland)
  • India's Basel III from 1 Apr 2013:
CapitalIndia minimum
CET1 (Common Equity Tier 1)5.5%
Tier 1 (CET1 + AT1)7%
Total capital (CRAR)9%
Capital Conservation Buffer (CCB)2.5% → total with CCB = 11.5%
Liquidity Coverage Ratio (LCR) / NSFR100% / 100%
Leverage ratio4% for D-SIBs, 3.5% for others
  • D-SIBs (Domestic Systemically Important Banks) → SBI, HDFC Bank, ICICI Bank
Mnemonic

"5.5, 7, 9, +2.5 = 11.5". Basel I-II-III = 88, 04, 10.

Remember

India (9%) is stricter than the Basel floor (8%).

22/36☆ Good to knowBasel III market-risk capital directions (2026)

  • RBI (Commercial Banks - Minimum Capital Requirements for Market Risk) Directions, 2026: final directions issued 21 Sep 2026; effective 1 Apr 2027
  • Aligns India with the revised Basel III framework for market risk (capital banks must hold against losses from price and rate movements in their trading positions); interim transition scalars have applied since 1 Apr 2024
  • Changes from the Feb 2023 draft: trading-book definition now refers to the Investment Directions; revised net open position and forex risk charges; revised specific-risk tables for interest-rate risk; revised treatment of debt mutual funds and ETFs in the trading book; total return swaps covered for hedged positions
Remember

Basel III market-risk directions: issued 21 Sep 2026, effective 1 Apr 2027 (not 2026).

Exam angle

From which date do the revised market-risk capital norms apply to commercial banks?

23/36★ Must-learnBasel III credit-risk (standardised approach) directions

  • RBI final directions on Basel III credit risk (Standardised Approach) apply from 1 Apr 2027, the same day as the ECL norms (they sit on top of ST-037 Basel norms)
  • Unrated corporate and NBFC exposures → 150% risk weight only above ₹500 crore (₹200 crore was proposed)
  • Unrated bank exposures → long-term 100%, short-term 50%; foreign bank branches may use parent ratings
  • Regulatory retail → all small businesses (turnover up to ₹500 crore); per-borrower limit ₹7.5 crore → ₹10 crore
  • Real estate AD&C (Acquisition, Development and Construction) → minimum borrower contribution 25% of project cost
  • Tier 2 capital → general provisions on standard assets (Stage 1/2) plus excess provisions from NPA sales count up to 1.25% of credit risk-weighted assets
Remember

Unrated corporate: 150% only above ₹500 cr. Retail limit ₹10 cr.

24/36★ Must-learnNPA (Non-Performing Asset) classification

  • NPA → loan overdue for more than 90 days (crop loans: 2 crop seasons for short-duration crops, 1 for long-duration)
StageOverdue
SMA-0 (Special Mention Account)1–30 days
SMA-131–60 days
SMA-261–90 days
Sub-standardNPA for up to 12 months
DoubtfulNPA for more than 12 months
LossLoss identified, mostly uncollectable
ProvisioningRate
Standard assets (general)0.40% (agriculture and SME 0.25%; commercial real estate 1%; residential housing 0.75%)
Sub-standard (secured / unsecured)15% / 25%
Doubtful secured: up to 1 yr / 1–3 yrs / over 3 yrs25% / 40% / 100%
Doubtful unsecured portion, and Loss100%
Mnemonic

SMA = 30-60-90 days, then NPA. Provision climbs 15 → 25 → 40 → 100.

Remember

From 1 Apr 2027 the incurred-loss ladder gives way to ECL stages (see the next card).

25/36★ Must-learnRBI Expected Credit Loss (ECL) norms and the IRAC replacement

  • RBI released the final Expected Credit Loss (ECL) directions with a package on 27 Apr 2026; they apply from 1 Apr 2027 to commercial banks (directions for All India Financial Institutions to follow separately)
  • Shift → from incurred loss (provide after a loan turns bad) to expected loss (provide in advance, forward-looking) using PD (Probability of Default), LGD (Loss Given Default) and EAD (Exposure at Default)
  • A Repeal Directions, 2026 ends the earlier income recognition, asset classification and provisioning (IRAC) framework for commercial banks (see ST-038 for the old SMA/NPA ladder); 13 amendment directions were issued alongside (stressed assets, credit risk, capital adequacy, investments, disclosures)
StageMeaningMinimum provision
Stage 1Normal loans0.4% (12-month expected loss)
Stage 2Risky loans (e.g. 60–90 days overdue)5% (lifetime expected loss)
Stage 3Credit-impaired (NPA)Full lifetime loss, highest provision
  • Transition → extra provision from the switch can be spread over 4 years, to 31 Mar 2031, with CET1 add-back during that period
  • Existing loans at 31 Mar 2027 move to the Effective Interest Rate (EIR) method by 31 Mar 2030 (three-year window)
  • Lower floors reported for some products → Stage 1 for individual housing loans 0.25%; Stage 2 for direct lending to State governments 2.5%
  • Wilful defaulter → from 1 Apr 2027 the bank must classify within 6 months of the account turning NPA
Mnemonic

Stages 1-2-3 = 0.4 – 5 – Full. "ECL = PD × LGD × EAD".

Remember

Old rule: provision after default. New rule: provision before default. Start date 1 Apr 2027, spread ends 31 Mar 2031.

Exam angle

What are the minimum Stage 1 and Stage 2 provisions under RBI's ECL norms, and from when do they apply?

26/36★ Must-learnRecovery channels

RouteLaw / bodyKey point
Lok AdalatLegal Services Authorities Act 1987Compromise, no court fee
DRT (Debt Recovery Tribunal)RDDBFI Act 1993Claims of ₹20 lakh and above (s.1(4) text says ₹10 lakh; raised by Finance Ministry notification S.O. 4312(E), 6 Sep 2018); also applies to SARFAESI s.13(10) applications
SARFAESI2002Seize and sell security without court; 60-day notice; ARCs (Asset Reconstruction Companies; first: ARCIL)

NARCLAnnounced Budget 2021"Bad bank" for stressed assets, with IDRCL
Mnemonic

Smallest to biggest: Lok Adalat → DRT → SARFAESI → IBC.

27/36★ Must-learnPriority Sector Lending (PSL)

  • Target → 40% of ANBC (Adjusted Net Bank Credit) or CEOBE (Credit Equivalent of Off-Balance Sheet Exposure), whichever is higher
Sub-targetShare
Agriculture18%
Small and marginal farmers (within agriculture)10%
Micro enterprises7.5%
Weaker sections12%
  • Small Finance Banks, RRBs → 75%
  • PSLC (Priority Sector Lending Certificates) → trading of PSL surplus, from April 2016
Mnemonic

Sub-targets read "18, 10, 7.5, 12": Agriculture, Small farmers, Micro, Weaker. Remember "AS-MW".

2.8 Payment systems and currency

28/36★ Must-learnBank dividend cap and lending limits (2026)

  • Dividend rules (2026 Directions, from FY27) → payout depends on capital strength in 10 buckets; adjusted PAT = PAT − 50% of net NPAs; no dividend out of exceptional income, unrealised fair-value gains or reversal of excess provisions
Bank typeMaximum dividend payout
Commercial banks (CET1 buckets), payments banks, small finance banks75% of PAT
Local Area Banks, Regional Rural Banks80% of PAT
  • Earlier cap was 40% of PAT (draft proposed raising it to 75%)
Loans against securities (from 1 Apr 2026)Maximum LTV
Listed shares60%
Equity mutual funds, ETFs, REITs, InvITs75%
Debt mutual funds; AAA listed debt85%
  • Cap ₹1 crore per individual; secondary-market purchase and IPO/FPO/ESOP loans ₹25 lakh each; breach to be cured in 7 working days; all such loans count as Capital Market Exposure (CME)
  • MSE loans → no collateral up to ₹20 lakh (also for all PMEGP units); banks may raise it to ₹25 lakh for good track record; effective 1 Apr 2026
Mnemonic

Dividend: 75 for banks, 80 for LAB/RRB. LTV: 60 – 75 – 85.

Exam angle

What is the maximum dividend payout for RRBs and LABs? What is the maximum LTV for loans against listed shares?

30/36★ Must-learnCurrency management

  • Minimum Reserve System (from 1956) → ₹200 crore of assets, of which ₹115 crore gold + ₹85 crore foreign securities
  • ₹1 note → issued by the Ministry of Finance, signed by the Finance Secretary; other notes → RBI, signed by the Governor
  • Printing → BRBNMPL (Mysuru, Salboni); SPMCIL (Nashik, Dewas)
  • Mints (coins) → Mumbai, Kolkata, Hyderabad, Noida
  • 8 Nov 2016 → ₹500 and ₹1,000 demonetised | ₹2,000 introduced then | 19 May 2023 → ₹2,000 withdrawn from circulation
Mnemonic

Mysuru and Salboni = RBI's BRBNMPL. Nashik and Dewas = SPMCIL (government).

31/36☆ Good to knowMDR and the economics of UPI

  • MDR (Merchant Discount Rate) = fee a merchant pays its bank to accept a digital payment (shared among issuer bank, acquirer bank and network)
  • Section 10A, Payment and Settlement Systems Act 2007: lets the Government bar charges on specified payment modes; zero MDR on RuPay debit and UPI from 1 Jan 2020
  • Sept 2026: Finance Ministry notified that banks cannot levy charges on UPI and RuPay debit card transactions up to ₹2,000; MDR on higher-value payments to apply only to select transactions
  • NPCI (National Payments Corporation of India): set up 2008; runs UPI (Unified Payments Interface), RuPay and IMPS
Exam angle

Up to what amount are UPI transactions free of charge?

Remember

Free ≤ ₹2,000; legal hook = Sec 10A of the PSS Act 2007.

32/36☆ Good to knowPayments Regulatory Board (PRB)

  • PRB (Payments Regulatory Board): under Section 3, Payment and Settlement Systems Act 2007; Section 3 amendments (Finance Act 2017) took effect 9 May 2025
  • Replaced the BPSS (Board for Regulation and Supervision of Payment and Settlement Systems), which was a committee of the RBI Central Board
  • Chair: RBI Governor; members include the Deputy Governor and Executive Director in charge of payment systems, Secretary of the Department of Financial Services, Secretary of the Ministry of Electronics and IT, and an external member
  • Payment aggregators need RBI authorisation under Section 7, PSS Act 2007
Remember

PRB = chaired by the Governor and includes two Union Secretaries; BPSS was RBI-only.

2.9 Depositor protection, forex and publications

33/36★ Must-learnDICGC, Ombudsman and credit bureaus

  • DICGC (Deposit Insurance and Credit Guarantee Corporation) → RBI subsidiary; formed 1978 by merging the Deposit Insurance Corporation (1962) and Credit Guarantee Corporation (1971); Act 1961
  • Cover → ₹5 lakh per depositor per bank (since 4 Feb 2020); payout within 90 days of restrictions (DICGC Amendment Act 2021); banks pay the premium
  • RBI Integrated Ombudsman Scheme → launched 12 Nov 2021 ("One Nation One Ombudsman"); replaced the 1995 Banking Ombudsman Scheme and two others
  • From 1 Apr 2026 → flat premium of 12 paise per ₹100 replaced by Risk-Based Premium (RBP) under Section 15(1), DICGC Act 1961; Tier 1 = commercial banks (not RRBs), Tier 2 = RRBs and cooperative banks; risk categories A–D card rates 8, 10, 11, 12 paise; vintage incentive up to 25%; reviewed at least every 3 years; Local Area Banks and Payments Banks stay at the old rate
  • First credit bureau → TransUnion CIBIL (2000); four licensed under CICRA 2005
Remember

₹5 lakh per depositor per bank, not per account.

34/36★ Must-learnCustomer protection: ombudsman, e-mandate and 2FA

ItemKey facts
Internal Ombudsman (IO) Directions 2026Effective 30 Jun 2026; covers commercial banks, SFBs, payments banks, NBFCs, non-bank PPI issuers, credit information companies; every bank needs at least one IO; replaces the 2023 Directions
IO rank and tenureServing or retired officer of rank equal to General Manager, 7 years' experience, age up to 70; Deputy IO at DGM level with 5 years; tenure at least 3 years, total up to 5; final decision to the complainant within 30 days
RBI Ombudsman compensationNo limit on amount in dispute; up to ₹30 lakh for consequential loss plus up to ₹3 lakh for time lost, expenses, harassment or mental anguish; complaints on the CMS portal (cms.rbi.org.in)
e-mandate (recurring digital payments)No OTP up to ₹15,000 per transaction after one-time registration with Additional Factor of Authentication (AFA); ₹1 lakh for insurance premiums, mutual fund SIPs and credit card bills; pre-debit notification at least 24 hours before
Two-factor authenticationCompulsory for all digital payments from 1 Apr 2026 (one factor dynamic); cross-border card-not-present deadline 1 Oct 2026
Remember

Ombudsman: ₹30 lakh (loss) + ₹3 lakh (harassment). e-mandate: ₹15,000 normal, ₹1 lakh for insurance/SIP/card bills.

Exam angle

What is the no-OTP limit for e-mandates, and the higher limit for insurance, SIPs and card bills?

35/36☆ Good to knowForex basics

  • FEMA 1999 (civil law) replaced FERA 1973 (criminal) | effective 1 Jun 2000
  • Current account convertibility → Aug 1994 (IMF Article VIII) | capital account → partial
  • Tarapore Committees on Capital Account Convertibility → 1997, 2006
  • Exchange rate regime → managed float
  • LRS (Liberalised Remittance Scheme) → $250,000 per person per financial year
Mnemonic

FERA = Fear (criminal) → FEMA = Manage (civil).

36/36☆ Good to knowRBI publications and calendar

  • Annual Report → by end Aug | Report on Trend and Progress of Banking in India → annual | Financial Stability Report → half-yearly (June, Dec)
  • Monetary Policy Report → half-yearly (Apr, Oct)
  • FSDC (Financial Stability and Development Council) → set up 2010, chaired by the Finance Minister

Static Bank · Section 3GST, Taxation and Budget15 items · 10 ★ must-learn · 5 ☆ good to know

3.1 GST journey

2/15★ Must-learnGST timeline

YearEvent
2000Vajpayee government begins GST discussions; Asim Dasgupta-led Empowered Committee of state finance ministers
2006-07Budget speech (P Chidambaram) announces GST from 1 Apr 2010
2009Kelkar Task Force (13th Finance Commission) roadmap for GST
2011115th Constitution Amendment Bill (lapsed)
2013GSTN (Goods and Services Tax Network) set up as a Section 8 company
Dec 2014122nd Amendment Bill introduced (Arun Jaitley); Lok Sabha passed May 2015; Rajya Sabha Aug 2016
Sep 2016101st Amendment Act (assent 8 Sep) → GST Council constituted 12 Sep; first meeting 22–23 Sep 2016
1 Jul 2017GST launched at midnight session in Central Hall of Parliament (July 1 = GST Day)
Jul 2017J&K joins (8 Jul 2017) → "One Nation, One Tax, One Market"
2018E-way bill (1 Apr 2018)
3 Sep 202556th GST Council → two-slab structure, effective 22 Sep 2025 ("GST 2.0")
Mnemonic

Idea 2000 → announced 2006 → Bill 2014 → Amendment 2016 → Launch 2017 → 2.0 in 2025. "Sixteen to Seventeen": Act 2016, launch 2017.

Exam angle

Which Amendment, which Article, which date?

3/15★ Must-learnGST structure and Acts

TaxLevied onGoes to
CGST (Central GST)Intra-state supplyCentre
SGST (State GST)Intra-state supplyState
IGST (Integrated GST)Inter-state supply and importsCollected by Centre, apportioned to the destination state
UTGST (Union Territory GST)Intra-UT supply (UTs without legislature)UT
Compensation CessSin and luxury goodsCompensation Fund (from 22 Sep 2025 merged into the 40% rate, except pan masala, gutkha and tobacco products)
  • Dual GST → Centre and states both levy | destination-based | input tax credit (ITC) chain
  • Acts of 2017 → CGST, IGST, UTGST, GST (Compensation to States) Act (5 years of compensation; 14% annual growth over the 2015-16 base)
  • Constitutional hooks → 246A (power), 269A (inter-state levy), 279A (Council), 366(12A) (definition)
  • Outside GST at launch → crude oil, diesel, petrol, natural gas, aviation turbine fuel; alcohol for human consumption; electricity (not subsumed)
Mnemonic

"CSI-U": CGST, SGST, IGST, UTGST. IGST = Inter-state, Import. Intra = CGST + SGST together (equal split).

3.2 GST Council

4/15★ Must-learnGST Council (Art 279A)

  • Inserted by 101st Amendment 2016; President constitutes it | joint forum of Centre and states | office in New Delhi
  • Chair → Union Finance Minister | members → Union MoS (Finance/Revenue) + state finance/taxation ministers (one among them elected Vice-Chair)
  • Secretary → Union Revenue Secretary (ex-officio) | CBIC Chairperson is a permanent invitee (no vote)
  • Voting → Centre 1/3 weight, all states together 2/3 | decision needs 3/4 of weighted votes of members present and voting
  • Recommends → taxes subsumed, exemptions, rates (including floor rates), model laws, IGST apportionment, special provisions for hill and north-east states, compensation
  • Supreme Court (2022, Mohit Minerals) → Council recommendations are persuasive, not binding
Mnemonic

1/3 + 2/3 = 100% of weight; 3/4 needed. Neither side can pass a decision alone (33% and 67% are both below 75%). The Centre alone can block, since its 33% is above the 25% blocking minority. Remember "1/3, 2/3, 3/4".

5/15★ Must-learnGST 2.0: slab overhaul of 2025

  • 56th Council → 3 Sep 2025 (chair: Nirmala Sitharaman) → unanimous | effective 22 Sep 2025 (tobacco from 1 Feb 2026: 40% GST on cigarettes, pan masala and tobacco, 18% on bidis; the compensation cess replaced by excise and a health and national security cess)
  • Four slabs 5 / 12 / 18 / 28 → two slabs 5% (merit) and 18% (standard), plus special 40% (demerit/luxury)
BeforeAfterExamples
28%18%ACs, TVs, dishwashers, small cars, cement
12% / 18%5%Toothpaste, umbrellas, sewing machines, bicycles, FMCG sachets
18%NilIndividual life and health insurance premiums
28%40%Luxury cars and SUVs, tobacco products, pan masala
Cess items40% (cess merged)Aerated drinks, casinos, online gaming, race clubs
  • 12% and 28% slabs abolished | estimated revenue loss over ₹47,000 crore
Mnemonic

"5-18-40": merit, standard, sin. Four slabs became two plus one special.

6/15☆ Good to knowRegistration, composition and GSTN

  • Registration threshold → ₹40 lakh (goods), ₹20 lakh (services); lower for special category states
  • Composition scheme → turnover up to ₹1.5 crore (goods)
  • GSTN → IT backbone; became 100% government-owned in 2019
  • CBIC (Central Board of Indirect Taxes and Customs) administers GST and customs; renamed from CBEC in 2017
Remember

These thresholds changed over the years; keep as indicative until verified.

3.3 Taxation basics

7/15★ Must-learnDirect vs indirect taxes

DirectIndirect
BurdenCannot be shiftedShifted to consumer
ExamplesIncome tax, corporate tax, capital gains tax, STT (Securities Transaction Tax, 2004)GST, customs duty, central excise (on petroleum and a few items)
BodyCBDT (Central Board of Direct Taxes)CBIC
NatureProgressive (rate rises with income)Regressive in effect (same rate for all)
  • Income tax first introduced in India in 1860 by James Wilson | Income Tax Day → 24 July
  • Wealth tax abolished from Budget 2015-16 (replaced by a surcharge on the super-rich)
  • Cess and surcharge are not shared with states (outside the divisible pool)
Mnemonic

Direct = DIrect hit on the taxpayer (CBDT). Indirect = INcluded in price (CBIC).

8/15★ Must-learnIncome-tax Act, 2025

  • Replaced the Income-tax Act, 1961 (Act 43 of 1961) from 1 Apr 2026
  • Act 30 of 2025 | Presidential assent 21 Aug 2025 | first Income-tax Bill introduced 13 Feb 2025, withdrawn 8 Aug 2025, revised Bill passed
  • Sections 819 → 536 | Chapters 47 → 23 | Schedules → 16
  • "Previous Year" and "Assessment Year" replaced by one "Tax Year" (Section 3) → income of 2026-27 = Tax Year 2026-27
  • Policy-neutral: no new taxes, no change in rates, slabs or regimes
  • Transition → FY 2025-26 (AY 2026-27) returns still under the 1961 Act
  • TDS compressed into 3 sections (392 to 394)
Exam angle

When did the new Act come into force and what replaced the assessment-year concept?

Mnemonic

819 → 536 (about a third cut) | 47 → 23 chapters | 1961 → 2025, in force 2026. "Tax Year" = one year replaces two (previous and assessment).

9/15☆ Good to knowCapital gains: LTCG and STCG

AssetLong-term if heldLTCG rateShort-term
Listed shares, equity mutual funds, business trust unitsMore than 12 months12.5% above ₹1.25 lakh a year20% (STCG on listed equity)
Property, gold, unlisted shares, other assetsMore than 24 months12.5%, no indexationSlab rates
Debt mutual funds (since Apr 2023)–Slab rates, any holding periodSlab rates
  • Budget July 2024: LTCG on listed equity 10% → 12.5%; exemption ₹1 lakh → ₹1.25 lakh; STCG 15% → 20%; indexation removed from 23 Jul 2024
  • Land or building bought before 23 Jul 2024: resident individual / HUF may choose 12.5% without indexation or 20% with indexation
  • Old Act sections: 111A (STCG on listed equity), 112A (LTCG on listed equity); the Income-tax Act 2025 (in force 1 Apr 2026) renumbers sections
  • STT (Securities Transaction Tax) is separate from capital gains tax
Remember

Equity: 12 months, 12.5%, ₹1.25 lakh exempt. Short-term: 20%.

10/15☆ Good to knowCustoms duty and import levies

DutyLegal basisWhat it does
BCD (Basic Customs Duty)Sec 12, Customs Act 1962Main import duty on assessable value (Sec 14)
SWS (Social Welfare Surcharge)Sec 110, Finance Act 2018Surcharge on top of BCD
IGST on importsSec 5, IGST Act 2017 with Sec 3(7), Customs Tariff ActReplaces the old additional duties for GST goods
CVD (Countervailing Duty)Sec 9, Customs Tariff Act 1975Offsets a foreign subsidy
Safeguard dutySec 8B, Customs Tariff ActTemporary duty when an import surge hurts domestic industry
ADD (Anti-Dumping Duty)Sec 9A, Customs Tariff ActOffsets dumping (export below normal value), up to the margin of dumping
AIDC (Additional Duties of Customs)Sec 3, Customs Tariff ActMatches excise or local levies on a like domestic article
  • CBIC (Central Board of Indirect Taxes and Customs) administers customs and GST
  • DGTR (Directorate General of Trade Remedies) recommends anti-dumping and safeguard duties; the Finance Ministry notifies them
Mnemonic

Section numbers: 8B – 9 – 9A = Safeguard (surge), CVD (subsidy), ADD (dumping).

3.4 Budget basics

11/15★ Must-learnBudget in the Constitution

ArtSubject
112Annual Financial Statement (Budget)
113Procedure in Parliament (demands for grants)
114Appropriation Bill
115Supplementary, additional, excess grants
116Vote on account, vote of credit, exceptional grants
117Financial Bills
265No tax without law
266Consolidated Fund (1) and Public Account (2)
267Contingency Fund (President's advance)
  • Budget presented on 1 Feb (from 2017; earlier last working day of February) | Rail Budget merged with Union Budget from 2017-18
  • Plan / non-plan classification abolished 2017-18 → now revenue / capital
  • Two Bills follow the Budget → Appropriation Bill authorises withdrawal from the Consolidated Fund; Finance Bill carries the tax proposals
Mnemonic

112-113-114-115-116-117 reads "Budget, Demand, Appropriation, Supplementary, Vote on account, Financial Bills". 266 = fundS (Consolidated + Public), 267 = emergency fund.

12/15★ Must-learnReceipts, expenditure and deficits

ItemFormula / meaning
Revenue receiptsTax revenue + non-tax revenue (no liability created, no asset reduced)
Capital receiptsBorrowings, recovery of loans, disinvestment (create liability or reduce asset)
Revenue deficitRevenue expenditure − Revenue receipts
Effective revenue deficitRevenue deficit − grants for creation of capital assets
Fiscal deficitTotal expenditure − (Revenue receipts + non-debt capital receipts) = borrowing need
Primary deficitFiscal deficit − Interest payments
Budgetary deficitOld measure; abolished from 1997-98
Mnemonic

"R → F → P": Revenue deficit, Fiscal deficit, Primary deficit. Primary = Fiscal minus interest. Fiscal deficit = how much the government must borrow.

13/15☆ Good to knowBudget passage and cut motions

  • Stages → presentation → general discussion → scrutiny by departmentally related standing committees → voting on demands for grants (Lok Sabha only; guillotine closes undiscussed demands) → Appropriation Bill → Finance Bill
  • Cut motions → policy cut (₹1 symbolic; disapproves policy), economy cut (specified amount), token cut (₹100; grievance)
  • Rajya Sabha only discusses; does not vote on demands for grants
  • Vote on account → temporary spending cover before the Budget passes (Art 116) | interim budget in election years

14/15★ Must-learnFRBM Act and the N K Singh Committee

  • FRBM Act 2003 (Fiscal Responsibility and Budget Management) | rules 2004 | fiscal deficit target 3% of GDP
  • N K Singh Committee → set up May 2016, report Jan 2017 (five members)
  • Recommendations → debt-to-GDP 60% (Centre 40% + states 20%) by 2023 | fiscal deficit 3% for 2017-20, then 2.5% by 2022-23 | escape clause up to 0.5% of GDP | three-member independent Fiscal Council | replace FRBM with a Debt and Fiscal Responsibility Act
  • Government accepted the debt anchor and escape clause in part; the Fiscal Council was not set up as recommended
Mnemonic

"60 = 40 + 20", "3 → 2.5", "0.5 escape".

15/15☆ Good to knowEconomic Survey

  • First Economic Survey 1950-51; prepared by the Economic Division, Department of Economic Affairs (Ministry of Finance) under the Chief Economic Adviser (V. Anantha Nageswaran since 28 Jan 2022)
  • Tabled in Parliament by the Finance Minister one day before the Union Budget
  • No constitutional or statutory mandate: a convention
  • Separated from the Budget speech in 1964; since 2022-23 one consolidated volume plus a Statistical Appendix (earlier two volumes)
Remember

Survey = convention, not law. Budget = Art 112 (Annual Financial Statement).

Static Bank · Section 4Financial Regulators and Institutions16 items · 8 ★ must-learn · 8 ☆ good to know

4.1 Who regulates whom

1/16★ Must-learnRegulator map

RegulatorRegulates
RBIBanks, NBFCs, housing finance companies (since 2019), payment systems, credit bureaus, cooperative banks (with NABARD supervising RRBs and rural cooperatives), forex

Mnemonic

"B-M-I-P": Banks → RBI, Markets → SEBI, Insurance → IRDAI, Pensions → PFRDA.

2/16☆ Good to knowHow regulator heads are chosen (FSRASC)

  • FSRASC (Financial Sector Regulatory Appointments Search Committee) → set up from the FSLRC (Justice B N Srikrishna Commission) recommendation; recommends heads and members of SEBI, IRDAI, PFRDA, and RBI Governor and Deputy Governors
  • Composition → Cabinet Secretary (chair); Additional Principal Secretary to PM; Secretary DEA or DFS; chair of the concerned regulator; three outside experts
  • Final approval → Appointments Committee of the Cabinet (ACC)
  • For PSB and financial institution heads → FSIB (Financial Services Institutions Bureau)
Remember

Search committee recommends; ACC (chaired by PM) approves.

4.2 SEBI

3/16★ Must-learnSEBI at a glance

  • Set up 12 Apr 1988 (non-statutory, under Finance Ministry) → statutory under the SEBI Act 1992 (statutory powers from 30 Jan 1992, after the Harshad Mehta scam) | HQ Mumbai (BKC)
  • Replaced the Controller of Capital Issues (Capital Issues (Control) Act 1947): merit-based control → disclosure-based regulation
  • Board (s.4) → 9 members = Chairperson + 2 Finance Ministry officials + 1 RBI nominee + 5 other members (at least 3 whole-time)
  • Functions → s.11 | appeals → SAT (Securities Appellate Tribunal, from 1995) | then Supreme Court
  • First woman Chairperson → Madhabi Puri Buch (2022 to Feb 2025, also first from private sector)
  • Current Chairperson → Tuhin Kanta Pandey, 11th Chairperson, took charge 1 Mar 2025 for 3 years (appointed by ACC on 27 Feb 2025)
  • Chairperson's term → up to 5 years or age 65 as appointed
Mnemonic

88 born, 92 sworn: "Born 88, Statutory 92".

Exam angle

Which Act gave SEBI statutory powers? Which body did it replace?

4/16★ Must-learnSecurities Markets Code, 2025 (pending Bill)

  • Introduced in Lok Sabha on 18 Dec 2025 (Bill No. 200 of 2025) → referred to the Parliamentary Standing Committee on Finance (report presented 23 Jul 2026)
  • Would repeal and replace → SCRA 1956, SEBI Act 1992 and Depositories Act 1996
  • Key proposals → SEBI Board enlarged from 9 to up to 15; separation of investigation and adjudication; mandatory registration of MIIs (Market Infrastructure Institutions: exchanges, depositories, clearing corporations); definition of market abuse; regulatory sandbox; conflict-of-interest disclosure by board members
  • Budget 2021 had announced the plan to consolidate the three Acts
Mnemonic

Three old Acts → One Code: 1956 + 1992 + 1996 → 2025.

Remember

Status: Bill, not yet an Act as of the last check (Oct 2026). Update this entry if it becomes law.

Mnemonic

Multi Cap: 25 – 25 – 25 = 75.

4.3 IRDAI and insurance

6/16★ Must-learnIRDAI at a glance

  • Recommended by the Malhotra Committee (R N Malhotra, 1993) → IRDA Act 1999 → IRDAI statutory body formed in 2000 | HQ Hyderabad
  • Insurance Act 1938 | LIC (life insurance nationalised 1956; LIC Act 1956, formed 1 Sep 1956) | GIC and four subsidiaries (1972-73)
  • Current Chairman → Ajay Seth (former DEA Secretary), took charge 1 Sep 2025 for 3 years or age 65; succeeded Debasish Panda
Mnemonic

"38, 56, 72, 99": Insurance Act, LIC nationalisation, general insurance nationalisation, IRDA Act.

FDI limit in insuranceYear
26%2000 (private entry)
49%2015
74%2021
100%2025 (Sabka Bima Sabki Raksha Act)
Mnemonic

26 → 49 → 74 → 100: the ladder climbs about every 6 years.

7/16★ Must-learnSabka Bima Sabki Raksha (Amendment of Insurance Laws) Act, 2025

  • Introduced in Lok Sabha 16 Dec 2025 → passed → now an Act
  • Amends three laws → Insurance Act 1938, LIC Act 1956, IRDA Act 1999
  • FDI 74% → 100% | conditions via the Indian Insurance Companies (Foreign Investment) Amendment Rules, notified 30 Dec 2025
  • Penalty cap for non-compliance → ₹1 crore → ₹10 crore
  • Notified 21 Dec 2025; in force 5 Feb 2026 | IRDAI approval needed to transfer shares: threshold 1% → 5% | foreign reinsurer's net owned fund ₹5,000 crore → ₹1,000 crore | one-time registration of intermediaries
  • IRDAI powers expanded → order recovery of wrongful gains, supersede an insurer's board and appoint an administrator, inspect intermediaries
  • Creates a Policyholders' Education and Protection Fund | Insurance laws aligned with the Digital Personal Data Protection Act 2023
  • Vision tag → "Insurance for All by 2047"
Exam angle

Which three Acts does it amend, and what is the new FDI limit?

8/16★ Must-learnIRDAI: Ind AS, D-SIIs and health insurance rules

ItemKey facts
Ind AS for insurersFrom 1 Apr 2026; IRDAI (Actuarial, Finance and Investment Functions of Insurers) (Amendment) Regulations 2026; applies to Life, General, Stand-Alone Health insurers and reinsurers (earlier IGAAP); parallel reporting for 2 years; one-year forbearance if an insurer cannot comply; Ind AS is converged with IFRS and implemented by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs
D-SIIs (Domestic Systemically Important Insurers)FY2025-26: LIC, GIC Re, New India Assurance retain status (too big to fail by size, importance and interconnectedness)
Health insurance rulesMoratorium 8 → 5 years (60 months); 30-day free look; grace period 15 days (monthly) or 30 days (other modes); pro-rata refund on mid-term cancellation
GSTIndividual life and health policies (incl. family floater) and reinsurance exempt from 22 Sep 2025 (was 18%)
Cyber security normsInformation Security Risk Management Committee meets quarterly (earlier twice a year); CISO independent of IT function
Remember

D-SIIs = LIC + GIC Re (state-owned reinsurer) + New India. D-SIBs = SBI, HDFC Bank, ICICI Bank.

Exam angle

Name the three D-SIIs. Since when must insurers follow Ind AS?

4.4 PFRDA

9/16★ Must-learnPFRDA and pensions

  • Interim authority set up 2003 by government resolution → statutory under PFRDA Act 2013 (in force Feb 2014) | HQ New Delhi
  • NPS (National Pension System) → central government employees from 2004; all citizens from 2009
  • APY (Atal Pension Yojana) → 2015 (unorganised sector)
  • NPS Vatsalya → announced in Budget 2024-25, launched 18 Sep 2024; for minors (under 18, incl. NRI/OCI) through a guardian; minimum ₹250 a year, no maximum; partial withdrawal after 3 years up to 25% of own contribution; at 18 fresh KYC and choice (by 21) to continue, move to NPS Tier I or exit (80% lump sum, 20% annuity; full withdrawal if corpus up to ₹8 lakh)
  • UPS (Unified Pension Scheme) → implemented 1 Apr 2025
  • Current Chairperson → Sivasubramanian Ramann, took charge 20 Jun 2025 for 5 years or age 65; earlier CMD of SIDBI (2021-24), Deputy CAG; succeeded Deepak Mohanty
Mnemonic

PFRDA: "03 interim, 13 Act". NPS 04 for government, 09 for all.

14/16☆ Good to knowIDBI Bank: from development finance institution to bank

15/16☆ Good to knowFIU-IND and the anti-money-laundering framework

  • FIU-IND (Financial Intelligence Unit–India): set up 2004; central agency to receive, analyse and share suspicious-transaction information
  • Works under PMLA 2002 (Prevention of Money Laundering Act); the Enforcement Directorate investigates and prosecutes
  • Enforces AML/CFT (anti-money-laundering / countering financing of terrorism) norms, including for virtual digital asset (VDA) providers

16/16☆ Good to knowMinimum public shareholding and public offer (SCRR 1957)

  • SCRR (Securities Contracts (Regulation) Rules, 1957), Rule 19(2)(b): a listed company must keep 25% public shareholding (MPS, minimum public shareholding); the minimum public offer (MPO) at IPO depends on post-issue market capitalisation
  • Amended by the SCRR Amendment Rules 2026 (notified 13 Mar 2026): 25% MPS benchmark unchanged; big issuers get longer timelines
Post-issue market capMinimum public offer (MPO)Time to reach 25% MPS
Up to ₹1,600 crore25%3 years
₹1,600 – 4,000 crore₹400 crore3 years
₹4,000 – 50,000 crore10%3 years
₹50,000 crore – ₹1 lakh crore₹1,000 crore and at least 8%5 years
₹1 lakh crore – ₹5 lakh crore₹6,250 crore and at least 2.75%5 years (10 years if public holding under 15% at listing; 15% due in 5)
Above ₹5 lakh crore₹15,000 crore and at least 1% (at least 2.5% of post-issue capital offered)Same as the row above
  • IFSC-listed companies: MPS reduced from 25% to 10%
  • Superior-voting-rights shares must be listed on the same exchange as ordinary shares
Remember

25% MPS never changed; only the timeline and offer size for very large issuers did.

Static Bank · Section 5Government Committees, Commissions and Reports10 items · 7 ★ must-learn · 3 ☆ good to know

5.1 Finance Commissions

1/10★ Must-learnFinance Commission basics

  • Art 280 → President constitutes it every 5 years | chairman + 4 members | conditions of service → Finance Commission (Miscellaneous Provisions) Act 1951
  • Art 281 → President lays its report before Parliament with an explanatory memorandum (action taken)
  • Recommends → vertical devolution (Centre → states share of net tax proceeds), horizontal devolution (among states), grants-in-aid (Art 275), local body grants, and disaster funding
  • Recommendations are advisory, but conventionally accepted
  • Art 270 → taxes shared between Centre and states | cess and surcharge stay outside the divisible pool
Mnemonic

280 = Constitutes, 281 = Reports to Parliament (28 + 0/1: "zero starts, one lays it before").

Remember

Jalan Committee details are source-checked; other rows are from knowledge and listed in Agent Notes.

6/10★ Must-learnRBI Economic Capital Framework (Bimal Jalan, 2018-19)

  • Committee set up by RBI in Nov 2018 under Bimal Jalan (former Governor) | accepted in Aug 2019
  • Two components of economic capital → realised equity (the Contingent Risk Buffer, CRB) and revaluation balances
  • CRB range → 5.5% to 6.5% of RBI's balance sheet | RBI set it at 5.5% in 2019 (was 6.5% as at 31 Mar 2024)
  • Framework to be reviewed every 5 years | an RBI internal committee is reviewing it (Governor Malhotra, Feb 2025)
  • RBI's accounting year July–June aligned with April–March from 2020-21; interim dividends only in extraordinary conditions
Surplus transfer to CentreAmount
FY19 (Aug 2019)₹1.76 lakh crore = ₹1.23 lakh crore surplus + ₹52,637 crore excess provisions
FY20 / FY21 / FY22 / FY23₹57,128 crore / ₹99,122 crore / ₹30,307 crore / ₹87,416 crore
FY24₹2.1 lakh crore (highest ever at the time)
Mnemonic

"5.5 to 6.5, review in 5, 1.76 in 2019".

Exam angle

What is the CRB band recommended by the Jalan Committee?

Static Bank · Section 6Schemes and Acts with Launch Year and Ministry20 items · 11 ★ must-learn · 9 ☆ good to know

6.1 Financial inclusion and social security

1/20★ Must-learnScheme-at-a-glance: inclusion and Jan Suraksha

SchemeLaunchMinistryTarget groupKey feature
PMJDY (Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojana)28 Aug 2014Finance (Dept of Financial Services)Unbanked householdsZero-balance account, RuPay debit card with accident cover, overdraft facility, DBT
PMJJBY (Jeevan Jyoti Bima)9 May 2015Finance (DFS)Age 18–50₹2 lakh life cover (death by any cause); renewable yearly
PMSBY (Suraksha Bima)9 May 2015Finance (DFS)Age 18–70₹2 lakh accidental death or total disability; ₹1 lakh partial; ₹20 a year
APY (Atal Pension Yojana)9 May 2015 (Kolkata)Finance (DFS), run by PFRDAAge 18–40, unorganised sectorGuaranteed pension from age 60; extended by Cabinet to FY 2030-31 (21 Jan 2026)
Mnemonic

Age ceilings "50-70-40": PMJJBY 50 (Jeevan = Jee-Vita 50), PMSBY 70, APY 40. All three launched on one day, 9 May 2015, from Kolkata.

How it works → Open PMJDY account → get RuPay card → enrol in PMJJBY / PMSBY / APY through the same bank (auto-debit) → claim or pension paid to account → DBT of subsidies lands in the same account.

Remember

JAM Trinity = Jan Dhan + Aadhaar + Mobile. PMJDY launched 28 Aug 2014 (its anniversary is 28 Aug).

3/20★ Must-learnPM SVANidhi (restructured 2025)

  • Full form → Pradhan Mantri Street Vendor's AtmaNirbhar Nidhi | Central sector scheme of the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs | launched 2020 (Covid relief)
  • Cabinet approved restructuring on 27 Aug 2025 → lending period extended to 31 Mar 2030 (earlier ended 31 Dec 2024) | outlay ₹7,332 crore | target 1.15 crore vendors, including 50 lakh new
  • Loan ladder (enhanced): ₹15,000 (was ₹10,000, 12 months) → ₹25,000 (was ₹20,000, 18 months) → ₹50,000 (unchanged, 36 months)
  • Extras → 7% interest subvention on timely repayment | UPI-linked RuPay credit card after repaying the second loan | digital cashback up to ₹1,600 | coverage widened to census towns and peri-urban areas
Mnemonic

"15-25-50" replaces "10-20-50": only the first two tranches moved.

4/20☆ Good to knowSovereign Gold Bonds (SGB)

  • Launched 5 Nov 2015 alongside the Gold Monetisation Scheme and Indian Gold Coin; notified 14 Jan 2016 under the Government Securities Act 2006
  • Issued by RBI on behalf of the Government of India; denominated in grams (1 gram and multiples)
  • Interest 2.5% a year, paid half-yearly; tenor 8 years, premature exit from the 5th year
  • Eligible: resident individuals, HUFs, trusts, universities, charitable institutions; limit up to 4 kg per financial year
  • Capital gains on redemption exempt for individuals; interest taxed at slab rates
  • Last tranche: Series 2023-24 IV (21 Feb 2024); Government confirmed in Feb 2025 that no further tranches will be issued; existing bonds continue
Mnemonic

2.5 – 8 – 5 = interest %, years to maturity, year of exit.

6.2 Employment, labour and rural livelihoods

5/20★ Must-learnVB-G RAM G Act, 2025 (replaced MGNREGA)

  • Full form → Viksit Bharat – Guarantee for Rozgar and Ajeevika Mission (Gramin) Act, 2025 | Ministry of Rural Development
  • Bill introduced in Lok Sabha 16 Dec 2025 (Shivraj Singh Chouhan) → passed → President's assent Dec 2025
  • Repeals MGNREGA 2005 | in force from 1 Jul 2026 (MGNREGA provisions continued during the interim)
  • Guarantee raised 100 → 125 days of wage employment per rural household per financial year
  • Focus on durable infrastructure and digital governance; existing e-KYC job cards stay valid until new cards are issued
Mnemonic

"100 to 125", "2005 to 2025", "1 July 2026".

How it works → Rural household registers → demand for work → employment within the guarantee of 125 days → wages paid → assets created (infrastructure) → payment in lieu if work is not provided.

6/20★ Must-learnPM Viksit Bharat Rozgar Yojana (PM-VBRY)

  • Earlier called ELI (Employment Linked Incentive) | Cabinet approval 1 Jul 2025 | effective 1 Aug 2025 | announced by PM on 15 Aug 2025 | Ministry of Labour and Employment, via EPFO
  • Outlay ₹99,446 crore | aim → 3.5 crore jobs (1.92 crore first-timers) | jobs created from 1 Aug 2025 to 31 Jul 2027
  • First-time employees (salary up to ₹1 lakh) → one month's EPF wage, up to ₹15,000, in two instalments after 6 and 12 months (second needs a financial literacy programme)
  • Employers → up to ₹3,000 a month per additional employee; extra bonus for manufacturing
Mnemonic

"99,446 crore, 3.5 crore jobs, 15,000 first pay".

7/20★ Must-learnFour Labour Codes (in force 21 Nov 2025)

CodeLaws merged
Code on Wages, 20194 (Payment of Wages, Minimum Wages, Payment of Bonus, Equal Remuneration)
Industrial Relations Code, 20203 (Industrial Disputes, Trade Unions, Standing Orders)
Code on Social Security, 20209 (including EPF Act, ESI Act, Maternity Benefit, Gratuity)
OSH Code, 2020 (Occupational Safety, Health and Working Conditions)13 (including Factories Act, Mines Act)
  • 4 + 3 + 9 + 13 = 29 central labour laws replaced | Ministry of Labour and Employment | all four notified in force on 21 Nov 2025, some provisions to be phased in
  • Coverage extended to unorganised, gig and platform workers
Mnemonic

"4-3-9-13 = 29": Wages 4, IR 3, Social Security 9, OSH 13.

8/20☆ Good to knowEPFO and the wage ceiling

  • EPFO (Employees' Provident Fund Organisation): under the Ministry of Labour and Employment; Employees' Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Provisions Act 1952
  • Three schemes: EPF (provident fund), EPS (Employees' Pension Scheme, 1995), EDLI (Employees' Deposit Linked Insurance, 1976)
  • Sept 2026: Cabinet raised the mandatory-coverage wage ceiling ₹15,000 → ₹25,000 a month (₹15,000 since Sept 2014); about 51 lakh more employees covered; annual Government outgo about ₹11,339 crore; effective 17 Sep 2026
Exam angle

What is the new EPFO wage ceiling for mandatory coverage?

11/20☆ Good to knowOperation Flood and the White Revolution

  • Operation Flood launched 13 Jan 1970 by NDDB (National Dairy Development Board, Anand, Gujarat); architect Verghese Kurien, "Milkman of India"
  • Phases: I 1970–80, II 1981–85, III 1985–96
  • Funded by sale of skimmed milk powder and butter oil gifted by the EEC (European Economic Community) through the World Food Programme; Phase II also had a World Bank loan
  • Model: Amul (Kaira District Cooperative Milk Producers' Union, Anand; Tribhuvandas Patel) → a national milk grid
  • India became the world's largest milk producer in 1998, passing the US

ItemDetail
SagarmalaApproved March 2015; National Perspective Plan launched April 2016; programme of the Ministry of Shipping (now Ports, Shipping and Waterways)

14/20★ Must-learne-NAM (National Agriculture Market)

18/20☆ Good to knowSWAMIH Fund: stalled housing finance

EligibilityRERA-registered; up to 200 sq m carpet area; price cap ₹2 crore (MMR), ₹1.5 crore (NCR, metros), ₹1 crore elsewhere; net-worth positive; NPA and NCLT projects eligible
SWAMIH Fund-2Announced in Budget 2025-26 (1 Feb 2025); ₹15,000 crore blended finance; target 1 lakh more units
Remember

Last-mile funding for stalled RERA-registered housing. Manager = SBICAP Ventures (SBI Capital Markets subsidiary).

Remember

Don't mix ADB (Manila, 1966) with AIIB (Beijing, 2016). Some sources print ADB as 1956, which is wrong.

6/13★ Must-learnOPEC, OPEC+ and the UAE exit

  • UAE exit → announced 28 Apr 2026; effective 1 May 2026 from OPEC and OPEC+ after a review of its production policy and capacity; the UAE had joined OPEC in 1967
  • OPEC (Organization of the Petroleum Exporting Countries) → founded at Baghdad in 1960; founders Iran, Iraq, Kuwait, Saudi Arabia, Venezuela; HQ Vienna; had 12 members including the UAE; publishes the World Oil Outlook
  • OPEC+ (Vienna Group, 2016) → OPEC plus 10 non-OPEC states: Azerbaijan, Bahrain, Brunei, Kazakhstan, Malaysia, Mexico, Oman, Russia, South Sudan, Sudan
Mnemonic

Founders = I-I-K-S-V (Iran, Iraq, Kuwait, Saudi, Venezuela). OPEC+ born 2016.

Exam angle

Which country left OPEC and OPEC+ on 1 May 2026?

9/13★ Must-learnUnited Nations: principal organs and the Security Council

Principal organDetail
General AssemblyAll 193 members; one vote each; elects non-permanent UNSC members
Security Council (UNSC)15 members: 5 permanent with veto (China, France, Russia, UK, US) + 10 non-permanent
ECOSOCEconomic and Social Council
Trusteeship CouncilSuspended operations in 1994
International Court of JusticeThe Hague, Netherlands; 15 judges
SecretariatHeaded by the Secretary-General; HQ New York
  • UN Charter in force 24 Oct 1945 (UN Day)
  • Non-permanent members: staggered 2-year terms; five elected every year by the General Assembly (secret ballot, two-thirds majority); seats by region: Africa 3, Asia-Pacific 2, Latin America and Caribbean 2, Western Europe and Others 2, Eastern Europe 1
  • 2026–27 members: Bahrain, Colombia, DR Congo, Latvia, Liberia (Latvia first time); 2025–26: Denmark, Greece, Pakistan, Panama, Somalia
Mnemonic

UNSC = 5 + 10 = 15; veto only for the P5 (C-F-R-U-U: China, France, Russia, UK, US).

7.4 Environment conventions

13/13☆ Good to knowRamsar Convention and India's wetland sites

  • Ramsar Convention: adopted 1971 at Ramsar, Iran; in force 1975; India joined 1982
  • India has 103 Ramsar sites across 28 States and UTs (as of 15 Oct 2026): latest additions Sukhna Wetland (Chandigarh) and Sauj Jheel (Uttar Pradesh); Glaw Lake became Arunachal Pradesh's first (3 Aug 2026)
  • Most sites: Tamil Nadu 18, then Uttar Pradesh 15; largest site: Sundarban Wetland (West Bengal), about 4,230 sq km
Mnemonic

Ramsar 71 → 75 → 82: adopted, in force, India joins.

Remember

The count changes every few months; always check the latest figure in the monthly notes.
