8.2 Banking crises and rescues

2/10★ Must-learnCrises that shaped regulation

DateEventOutcome
Feb 2018PNB–Nirav Modi fraud (about ₹11,000 crore)Fugitive Economic Offenders Act 2018; tighter LoU/SWIFT controls
Sep 2018IL&FS defaultNBFC liquidity crunch; government took over board
Sep 2019PMC Bank restrictionsLater merged into Unity Small Finance Bank (2022)
5 Mar 2020Yes Bank moratoriumSBI-led reconstruction (see ST-094A)
Nov 2020Lakshmi Vilas BankMerged with DBS Bank India
Mnemonic

Chain: PNB fraud 2018 → IL&FS 2018 → PMC 2019 → Yes Bank 2020 → LVB 2020.

3/10★ Must-learnYes Bank reconstruction (March 2020)

  • 5 Mar 2020 → Centre placed Yes Bank under moratorium (RBI request) until 3 Apr; withdrawals capped at ₹50,000 per depositor; RBI superseded the board and appointed Prashant Kumar (ex-SBI CFO) as administrator
  • 6 Mar 2020 → RBI released the draft Yes Bank Ltd. Reconstruction Scheme, 2020
  • 13 Mar 2020 → Cabinet approved; scheme came into force the same day
  • SBI as investor bank → up to 49%, invested ₹7,250 crore (725 crore shares at ₹10); lock-in of 3 years, cannot fall below 26% within that time; other lenders (ICICI, HDFC, Axis, Kotak, Bandhan) joined
  • Moratorium lifted at 6 pm on 18 Mar 2020; Prashant Kumar became MD and CEO
Mnemonic

"5-6-13-18 March": moratorium, draft, scheme, lifted. "49-26-3".

Exam angle

What was the withdrawal cap, SBI's stake and the lock-in?

8.3 Tax and disinvestment

4/10★ Must-learnCorporate tax cut of Sep 2019

  • Taxation Laws (Amendment) Ordinance, 20 Sep 2019 (later an Act) → announced by the Finance Minister to revive growth
  • Section 115BAA → option for domestic companies: 22% base (effective 25.17% with 10% surcharge and 4% cess); no exemptions; no MAT
  • Section 115BAB → 15% (effective 17.16%) for new manufacturing companies incorporated on or after 1 Oct 2019 and starting production before 31 Mar 2023
  • MAT for others cut from 18.5% to 15% | estimated revenue loss about ₹1.45 lakh crore
Mnemonic

"22 and 15; 25.17 and 17.16".

5/10★ Must-learnLIC IPO (May 2022)

  • Subscription 4–9 May 2022 | listed on BSE and NSE 17 May 2022 | price band ₹902–949 (issue price ₹949)
  • Offer for sale of about 3.5% stake (22.13 crore shares), cut from the planned 5% | raised ₹20,557 crore (issue size ₹21,008 crore before discounts) → largest IPO in India at the time, ahead of Paytm (₹18,300 crore, Nov 2021)
  • Run by DIPAM (Department of Investment and Public Asset Management); Secretary then: Tuhin Kanta Pandey
  • LIC was formed 1956 by merging 245 private insurers (LIC Act 1956)
Mnemonic

"4-9 May, list 17 May, 3.5%".

8.4 India on the world stage

6/10★ Must-learnG20 Presidency, BRICS expansion and space

  • India's G20 Presidency → 1 Dec 2022 to 30 Nov 2023 | 18th Summit, 9–10 Sep 2023, Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi | theme "One Earth, One Family, One Future" (Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam)
  • Outcomes → New Delhi Leaders' Declaration adopted by consensus; African Union made a permanent member (21st member, 9 Sep 2023); Global Biofuels Alliance launched
  • BRICS expansion → decided at the Johannesburg Summit (Aug 2023); Egypt, Ethiopia, Iran, UAE joined from 1 Jan 2024 (Saudi Arabia was invited); Indonesia joined 6 Jan 2025; partner-country category created at Kazan (Oct 2024)
  • Chandrayaan-3 → launched 14 Jul 2023; Vikram lander touched down near the lunar south pole on 23 Aug 2023 (India: 4th country to soft-land on the Moon, first near the south pole); Chandrayaan-2 (2019) failed at landing
  • Aditya-L1 → solar mission launched 2 Sep 2023
Mnemonic

G20 "1 Dec 22 → 9-10 Sep 23, AU = 21st". Chandrayaan: launch 14 July, land 23 August.

8/8★ Must-learnLook-alike pairs

PairDifference
ADB vs AIIBADB: Manila, 19 Dec 1966. AIIB: Beijing, 16 Jan 2016
CRR vs SLRCRR: cash with RBI, s.42 RBI Act, earns nothing. SLR: held by the bank in cash, gold or G-secs, s.24 BR Act, ceiling 40%
Repo vs reverse repo vs SDFRepo: RBI lends against G-secs. Reverse repo: RBI absorbs liquidity. SDF: deposit with no collateral, floor of the corridor
FERA vs FEMAFERA 1973: criminal. FEMA 1999: civil, effective 1 Jun 2000
Art 123 vs Art 213President's ordinance vs Governor's ordinance
Art 32 vs Art 226Supreme Court (Fundamental Rights only) vs High Courts (FRs and other legal rights)
73rd vs 74th Amendment73rd: Panchayat, Part IX, 11th Schedule, 29 subjects. 74th: Municipality, Part IXA, 12th Schedule, 18 subjects
Fifth vs Sixth ScheduleFifth: 10 states, President, advisory TAC. Sixth: 4 NE states, Governor, Councils with real power
DRT vs IBC thresholdsDRT: ₹20 lakh and above. IBC: default of ₹1 crore and above
NSDL vs CDSLNSDL: 1996, NSE-promoted. CDSL: 1999, BSE-promoted
Money Bill vs Financial BillMoney Bill: Art 110, Lok Sabha only, Rajya Sabha recommends only. Financial Bill: Art 117, full powers for Rajya Sabha
CRAR vs CCBCRAR: 9% total capital. CCB: extra 2.5% → 11.5%
PRB vs BPSSPRB: chaired by the RBI Governor with two Union Secretaries (from 9 May 2025). BPSS: RBI-only board it replaced
1st vs 15th vs 16th FCNeogy (1951); N K Singh (2021-26, 41%); Panagariya (2026-31, 41%)

Static Bank · Section 10Revision168 questions

10.1 Constitution, polity and amendments

  1. Which amendment changed the Preamble and what did it add?
    Show answer

    42nd Amendment, 1976: Socialist, Secular, Integrity.

  2. Original vs current count of Articles, Parts and Schedules?
    Show answer

    395, 22, 8 → about 448, 25 (Part VII repealed), 12.

  3. Which Articles form Part III, and what is the ladder of the six rights?
    Show answer

    Arts 12–35; Equality 14–18, Freedom 19–22, Exploitation 23–24, Religion 25–28, Cultural-Educational 29–30, Remedies 32 (5-4-2-4-2-1).

  4. Which writ for illegal detention, quashing an order, and challenging the right to hold office?
    Show answer

    Habeas Corpus; Certiorari; Quo Warranto.

  5. Art 32 vs Art 226?
    Show answer

    Art 32: Supreme Court, Fundamental Rights only. Art 226: High Courts, FRs and other legal rights.

  6. Which Articles did the 42nd Amendment add to the DPSP?
    Show answer

    39A (free legal aid), 43A (workers in management), 48A (environment).

  7. How many Fundamental Duties, and which amendments?
    Show answer

    11: ten by the 42nd (1976), the 11th by the 86th (2002).

  8. What are the committee sizes: PAC, COPU, Estimates?
    Show answer

    PAC 22 (15 LS + 7 RS); COPU 22; Estimates 30 (Lok Sabha only).

  9. What is the quorum, and which Article provides a joint sitting?
    Show answer

    One-tenth of members (Art 100); joint sitting Art 108, presided by the Speaker.

  10. Which Articles, Parts and Schedules do the 73rd, 74th and 97th Amendments relate to?
    Show answer

    73rd: Part IX, 11th Schedule (29 subjects). 74th: Part IXA, 12th Schedule (18). 97th: Part IXB (cooperatives).

  11. Which Articles cover National Emergency, President's Rule and Financial Emergency?
    Show answer

    352, 356, 360 (financial never used).

  12. What majority is needed for most amendments?
    Show answer

    Majority of total membership and two-thirds of those present and voting; federal features also need half of the state legislatures.

  13. Basic structure chain?
    Show answer

    Golaknath 1967 (no) → Kesavananda Bharati 1973 (13 judges, basic structure) → Minerva Mills 1980 (reaffirmed).

  14. Which judgments: Sec 66A struck down, privacy a Fundamental Right, electoral bonds struck down?
    Show answer

    Shreya Singhal 2015; Puttaswamy 2017 (9 judges); ADR 2024.

  15. Which amendment introduced GST, and which Articles?
    Show answer

    101st (2016); Arts 246A, 269A, 279A.

  16. Fifth vs Sixth Schedule?
    Show answer

    Fifth: 10 states, President declares Scheduled Areas, advisory TAC. Sixth: Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram, Governor, Autonomous District Councils (26 + 4 = 30).

  17. Modes of acquiring and losing citizenship?
    Show answer

    Acquire: Birth, Descent, Registration, Naturalisation, Incorporation. Lose: Renunciation, Termination, Deprivation.

  18. Under which Act is the Census held, and which Entry?
    Show answer

    Census Act 1948; Entry 69, Union List.

  19. When is the Census 2027 reference date?
    Show answer

    1 Mar 2027 (1 Oct 2026 for snow-bound areas); houselisting from 1 Apr 2026.

  20. Maximum Bharat Ratna and Padma awards a year?
    Show answer

    Bharat Ratna 3; Padma 120 (excluding posthumous and foreign / NRI awardees).

  21. Which Article covers Karnataka, Mizoram and Nagaland (special provisions)?
    Show answer

    371J; 371G; 371A. Proposed 371K is for Ladakh (not enacted).

  22. Which constitutional status did NCBC get and when?
    Show answer

    Art 338B, 102nd Amendment 2018.

10.2 RBI, monetary policy and banking

  1. RBI chain: commission, Act, start, Mumbai, nationalisation?
    Show answer

    1926 Hilton Young → 1934 Act → 1 Apr 1935 → 1937 Mumbai → 1 Jan 1949.

  2. First Governor and first Indian Governor?
    Show answer

    Sir Osborne Smith; C D Deshmukh (1943).

  3. Who is the 26th RBI Governor and since when?
    Show answer

    Sanjay Malhotra, from 11 Dec 2024.

  4. RBI Act sections for directions, notes, CRR, MPC?
    Show answer

    s.7; s.22; s.42; s.45ZB.

  5. MPC: members, quorum, formation?
    Show answer

    6 members (3 RBI + 3 external); quorum 4; constituted 29 Sep 2016; at least 4 meetings a year; minutes after 14 days.

  6. Inflation target and failure?
    Show answer

    4% CPI ±2% (valid 1 Apr 2026 to 31 Mar 2031); failure = outside the band for 3 consecutive quarters.

  7. Rate corridor?
    Show answer

    SDF = repo − 0.25 (floor); MSF = repo + 0.25 (ceiling); Bank Rate = MSF; width 50 bps.

  8. CRR vs SLR?
    Show answer

    CRR: cash with RBI (s.42 RBI Act). SLR: with the bank itself, ceiling 40% (s.24 BR Act).

  9. M3?
    Show answer

    M1 + time deposits with banks; money multiplier = M3 ÷ M0.

  10. Order and years of lending-rate regimes?
    Show answer

    BPLR 2003 → Base Rate 1 Jul 2010 → MCLR 1 Apr 2016 → EBLR 1 Oct 2019.

  11. Nationalisation dates and numbers?
    Show answer

    19 Jul 1969: 14 banks (deposits above ₹50 crore); 15 Apr 1980: 6 banks.

  12. Which four anchor banks absorbed others in 2020?
    Show answer

    PNB, Canara, Union Bank, Indian Bank; 12 PSBs remain.

  13. NBFC layers and the cut-off?
    Show answer

    Base, Middle, Upper, Top (empty); SBR from 1 Oct 2022; Base cut-off ₹1,000 crore.

  14. Banking Laws (Amendment) Act 2025: three headline changes?
    Show answer

    Nominees 1 → 4; substantial interest ₹5 lakh → ₹2 crore; co-operative bank director tenure 8 → 10 years.

  15. Bankers' Books Evidence Act 2026?
    Show answer

    Replaces the 1891 Act; in force 1 Oct 2026; digital records admissible.

  16. RRB ownership and count?
    Show answer

    Centre 50 : State 15 : Sponsor bank 35; 28 RRBs from 1 May 2025; first five on 2 Oct 1975.

  17. India's Basel III minimums?
    Show answer

    CET1 5.5%; Tier 1 7%; CRAR 9%; CCB 2.5% → 11.5%.

  18. When do the Basel III market-risk directions apply?
    Show answer

    1 Apr 2027 (issued 21 Sep 2026).

  19. NPA definition and SMA stages?
    Show answer

    Overdue more than 90 days; SMA-0 1–30, SMA-1 31–60, SMA-2 61–90 days.

  20. Provisioning ladder?
    Show answer

    Sub-standard 15% (secured) / 25% (unsecured); doubtful 25 → 40 → 100%; loss 100%.

  21. Recovery thresholds: DRT and IBC?
    Show answer

    DRT ₹20 lakh and above; IBC default ₹1 crore and above (330 days).

  22. PSL targets?
    Show answer

    40% of ANBC/CEOBE: agriculture 18, small and marginal farmers 10, micro 7.5, weaker sections 12; SFB and RRB 75.

  23. When did UPI and RTGS start?
    Show answer

    UPI 11 Apr 2016; RTGS 2004 (minimum ₹2 lakh).

  24. Which note does the Finance Ministry issue?
    Show answer

    ₹1 note (signed by the Finance Secretary).

  25. UPI zero-charge limit and its legal hook?
    Show answer

    Up to ₹2,000; Section 10A, PSS Act 2007.

  26. Who chairs the Payments Regulatory Board and from when?
    Show answer

    RBI Governor; effective 9 May 2025; replaced BPSS.

  27. DICGC cover and payout time?
    Show answer

    ₹5 lakh per depositor per bank (since 4 Feb 2020); within 90 days; risk-based premium from 1 Apr 2026.

  28. Integrated Ombudsman Scheme launch?
    Show answer

    12 Nov 2021 ("One Nation One Ombudsman").

  29. FERA vs FEMA; LRS limit?
    Show answer

    FERA 1973 criminal; FEMA 1999 civil (effective 1 Jun 2000); LRS $250,000 per person per year.

  30. RBI ECL norms: stage provisions and start date?
    Show answer

    Stage 1 0.4%, Stage 2 5%, Stage 3 full lifetime loss; from 1 Apr 2027; transition spread to 31 Mar 2031.

  31. What does ECL replace?
    Show answer

    The incurred-loss IRAC ladder; a Repeal Directions 2026 ends the old framework (issued 27 Apr 2026).

  32. Basel III credit-risk directions: key numbers?
    Show answer

    From 1 Apr 2027; 150% on unrated corporates above ₹500 crore; regulatory retail limit ₹7.5 → ₹10 crore; AD&C borrower contribution 25%.

  33. Under which Section did RBI cancel Paytm Payments Bank's licence?
    Show answer

    Section 22(4), Banking Regulation Act 1949, effective 24 Apr 2026.

  34. Payments banks: minimum capital, first to launch, number?
    Show answer

    ₹100 crore; Airtel (2017); IPPB 1 Sep 2018; five banks (Airtel, IPPB, Fino, Jio, NSDL).

  35. SFB to universal bank: eligibility?
    Show answer

    Scheduled, 5-year record, listed, net worth ₹1,000 crore, GNPA ≤ 3%, NNPA ≤ 1%; only AU SFB so far.

  36. Maximum dividend payout for banks?
    Show answer

    75% of PAT for commercial, payments and small finance banks; 80% for LABs and RRBs (from FY27).

  37. Loans against securities: LTV and cap?
    Show answer

    Shares 60%, equity MFs 75%, debt MFs 85%; ₹1 crore cap per individual; from 1 Apr 2026.

  38. e-mandate no-OTP limits?
    Show answer

    ₹15,000 per transaction (₹1 lakh for insurance, SIPs, card bills); 24-hour pre-debit notice.

  39. RBI Ombudsman compensation limit?
    Show answer

    Up to ₹30 lakh for consequential loss plus ₹3 lakh for harassment.

  40. Utkarsh 2029: period, pillars, deliverables?
    Show answer

    2026-29; 6 pillars; 49 deliverables; released 30 Mar 2026.

  41. RBI Digital Payments Index base?
    Show answer

    March 2018 = 100.

  42. Index base years now?
    Show answer

    CPI 2024; GDP, IIP, WPI and ICI 2022-23; ICI now has nine industries (Iron Ore added).

  43. First release of new IIP and new WPI?
    Show answer

    IIP 1 Jun 2026 (April data); WPI 15 Jun 2026 (May data).

10.3 GST, taxation and budget

  1. GST milestones: Act, Council, launch?
    Show answer

    101st Amendment 2016 (assent 8 Sep); Council constituted 12 Sep 2016; launched 1 Jul 2017.

  2. GST Council voting?
    Show answer

    Centre 1/3, States 2/3; decisions need 3/4 of weighted votes.

  3. Four GST taxes?
    Show answer

    CGST, SGST, IGST, UTGST (IGST on inter-state supply and imports).

  4. GST 2.0 slabs and date?
    Show answer

    5 / 18 / 40 (special); 56th Council 3 Sep 2025; effective 22 Sep 2025; insurance premiums Nil.

  5. Which boards administer direct and indirect taxes?
    Show answer

    CBDT (direct); CBIC (indirect: GST and customs).

  6. Income-tax Act 2025: date and changes?
    Show answer

    In force 1 Apr 2026; sections 819 → 536; chapters 47 → 23; one "Tax Year".

  7. LTCG and STCG on listed equity?
    Show answer

    LTCG 12.5% above ₹1.25 lakh (held more than 12 months); STCG 20%.

  8. Customs Tariff Act sections for safeguard, CVD, ADD?
    Show answer

    8B; 9; 9A.

  9. Budget Articles 112 to 117?
    Show answer

    112 Budget; 113 demands; 114 Appropriation Bill; 115 supplementary grants; 116 vote on account; 117 Financial Bills.

  10. Fiscal deficit and primary deficit?
    Show answer

    Fiscal = total expenditure − (revenue receipts + non-debt capital receipts); primary = fiscal − interest.

  11. Types of cut motion?
    Show answer

    Policy cut (₹1), economy cut, token cut (₹100).

  12. N K Singh Committee: key numbers?
    Show answer

    Debt 60% of GDP (Centre 40 + States 20); fiscal deficit 3%; escape clause 0.5%.

  13. What is the Economic Survey's legal status?
    Show answer

    Convention, not law; tabled one day before the Budget; first in 1950-51.

10.4 Financial regulators and institutions

  1. Which regulator for banks, markets, insurance, pensions?
    Show answer

    RBI, SEBI, IRDAI, PFRDA (B-M-I-P).

  2. Who chairs the FSRASC and who approves heads?
    Show answer

    Cabinet Secretary chairs; the Appointments Committee of the Cabinet approves.

  3. SEBI: set up, statutory, replaced which office?
    Show answer

    12 Apr 1988; statutory 30 Jan 1992 (SEBI Act); replaced the Controller of Capital Issues.

  4. Who is SEBI Chairperson, and who was the first woman?
    Show answer

    Tuhin Kanta Pandey (from 1 Mar 2025); Madhabi Puri Buch.

  5. Which three Acts would the Securities Markets Code 2025 replace?
    Show answer

    SCRA 1956, SEBI Act 1992, Depositories Act 1996 (still a Bill).

  6. IRDAI origin and HQ?
    Show answer

    Malhotra Committee 1993 → IRDA Act 1999 → IRDAI 2000; Hyderabad.

  7. FDI limit ladder in insurance?
    Show answer

    26% (2000) → 49% (2015) → 74% (2021) → 100% (2025).

  8. Which three laws does the 2025 insurance Act amend?
    Show answer

    Insurance Act 1938, LIC Act 1956, IRDA Act 1999.

  9. PFRDA and NPS dates?
    Show answer

    PFRDA Act 2013 (interim 2003); NPS 2004 (government), 2009 (all); APY 2015; UPS 1 Apr 2025.

  10. Set-up years and HQs: NABARD, EXIM, NHB, SIDBI?
    Show answer

    NABARD 12 Jul 1982 Mumbai; EXIM 1 Jan 1982 Mumbai; NHB 9 Jul 1988 New Delhi; SIDBI 2 Apr 1990 Lucknow.

  11. Newest AIFI?
    Show answer

    NaBFID (2021), Act 2021, Mumbai.

  12. IFSCA and IBBI?
    Show answer

    IFSCA: 2020, GIFT City, Act 2019. IBBI: from Oct 2016 under IBC, New Delhi.

  13. NSDL vs CDSL?
    Show answer

    NSDL 1996 (NSE-promoted); CDSL 1999 (BSE-promoted); Depositories Act 1996.

  14. How did LIC come to own IDBI Bank?
    Show answer

    Acquired 51% on 21 Jan 2019 (₹21,624 crore); recategorised as private sector bank.

  15. Minimum public shareholding?
    Show answer

    25% (Rule 19(2)(b), SCRR 1957); IFSC 10%; amended 13 Mar 2026.

  16. Who are the D-SIIs?
    Show answer

    LIC, GIC Re and New India Assurance (FY2025-26).

  17. Since when do insurers follow Ind AS?
    Show answer

    1 Apr 2026; parallel reporting for 2 years.

  18. Sabka Bima Sabki Raksha Act: in force and key thresholds?
    Show answer

    In force 5 Feb 2026; IRDAI approval threshold 1% → 5%; foreign reinsurer NOF ₹5,000 → ₹1,000 crore.

  19. Health insurance moratorium period?
    Show answer

    5 years (60 months), earlier 8; 30-day free look.

  20. NPS Vatsalya: launch and minimum contribution?
    Show answer

    18 Sep 2024; ₹250 a year; for minors via guardian.

  21. Multi Cap fund minimum allocation (SEBI)?
    Show answer

    75% equity with at least 25% each in large, mid and small cap.

  22. Which body is the SRO for forex dealers?
    Show answer

    FEDAI (set up 1958), recognised by RBI as a Self-Regulatory Organisation.

10.5 Committees, commissions and reports

  1. Which Article constitutes the Finance Commission, and which has its report laid?
    Show answer

    Art 280; Art 281.

  2. States' share ladder?
    Show answer

    13th 32%; 14th 42%; 15th 41%; 16th 41%.

  3. 16th Finance Commission: chair, award period, report?
    Show answer

    Arvind Panagariya; 2026-27 to 2030-31; tabled 1 Feb 2026.

  4. Which RBI Governors chaired Finance Commissions?
    Show answer

    C Rangarajan (12th) and Y V Reddy (14th).

  5. Narasimham I and II years?
    Show answer

    1991 (financial system) and 1998 (banking reforms).

  6. Which committee recommended payments and small finance banks?
    Show answer

    Nachiket Mor, 2014.

  7. Which committee led to inflation targeting?
    Show answer

    Urjit Patel Committee, 2014.

  8. Jalan Committee: key number?
    Show answer

    Contingent Risk Buffer 5.5% to 6.5% of RBI's balance sheet; accepted Aug 2019.

  9. Committee for microfinance, GAAR, MSME?
    Show answer

    Malegam 2011; Shome 2012; U K Sinha 2019.

  10. Panchayat committees?
    Show answer

    Balwant Rai Mehta 1957 (three tiers); Ashok Mehta 1977 (two tiers).

  11. Swaminathan formula; Dalwai target?
    Show answer

    MSP at C2 + 50%; doubling farmers' income by 2022.

  12. Who publishes: GHI, HDI, Corruption Perceptions Index, Global Innovation Index?
    Show answer

    Concern Worldwide and Welthungerhilfe; UNDP; Transparency International; WIPO.

  13. Who publishes the Global Gender Gap Index and the World Economic Outlook?
    Show answer

    WEF; IMF.

10.6 Schemes and Acts

  1. PMJDY launch?
    Show answer

    28 Aug 2014.

  2. PMJJBY, PMSBY, APY: launch, ages, cover?
    Show answer

    9 May 2015 (Kolkata); ages 50 / 70 / 40; ₹2 lakh cover for the two insurance schemes.

  3. PMMY loan categories?
    Show answer

    Shishu up to ₹50,000; Kishore ₹50,000–5 lakh; Tarun ₹5–10 lakh; Tarun Plus ₹10–20 lakh.

  4. Stand-Up India?
    Show answer

    5 Apr 2016; ₹10 lakh to ₹1 crore for SC/ST and women entrepreneurs.

  5. PM SVANidhi loan ladder after restructuring?
    Show answer

    ₹15,000 → ₹25,000 → ₹50,000; lending period to 31 Mar 2030.

  6. Sovereign Gold Bond features?
    Show answer

    2.5% interest, 8-year tenor, exit from the 5th year; launched 5 Nov 2015.

  7. VB-G RAM G Act?
    Show answer

    Replaces MGNREGA; 125 days of guaranteed work; in force 1 Jul 2026.

  8. PM-VBRY outlay and target?
    Show answer

    ₹99,446 crore; 3.5 crore jobs; effective 1 Aug 2025.

  9. Four Labour Codes?
    Show answer

    Wages, Industrial Relations, Social Security, OSH; replace 29 laws; in force 21 Nov 2025.

  10. New EPFO wage ceiling?
    Show answer

    ₹25,000 a month (from ₹15,000), effective 17 Sep 2026.

  11. PM-KISAN and PMFBY details?
    Show answer

    PM-KISAN: ₹6,000 a year in three instalments (24 Feb 2019). PMFBY: premium 2% kharif, 1.5% rabi.

  12. Operation Flood: start, institution, face?
    Show answer

    13 Jan 1970; NDDB; Verghese Kurien.

  13. Which colour revolution is for oilseeds, fish, horticulture?
    Show answer

    Yellow; Blue; Golden.

  14. Year of the Companies Act, PFRDA Act, IBC, Competition Act, SARFAESI?
    Show answer

    2013; 2013; 2016; 2002; 2002.

  15. FCRA: year and ministry?
    Show answer

    2010; Home Affairs.

  16. Sagarmala: approval and plan years; Vadhavan SPV split?
    Show answer

    Approved March 2015; plan April 2016; JNPA 74% : Maharashtra Maritime Board 26%.

  17. Sagarmala: how many pillars?
    Show answer

    Five: port modernisation, connectivity, port-led industrialisation, coastal community development, coastal shipping and inland waterways.

  18. Sagarmala Development Company became what?
    Show answer

    Sagarmala Finance Corporation Ltd (June 2025), the first maritime NBFC.

  19. PMFBY launch date?
    Show answer

    18 Feb 2016.

  20. e-NAM launch and what is e-NWR?
    Show answer

    April 2016; e-NWR = electronic negotiable warehouse receipt (Warehousing Act 2007, WDRA).

  21. SMILE: launch and target groups?
    Show answer

    12 Feb 2022; transgender persons and persons engaged in begging; Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment.

  22. Sukanya Samriddhi: age, deposit limits, tenure?
    Show answer

    Open till girl is 10; ₹250 minimum, ₹1.5 lakh maximum a year; deposits for 15 years; matures after 21 years; maximum two girls.

  23. Jal Jeevan Mission: extension and outlay?
    Show answer

    Extended to Dec 2028; total ₹8.69 lakh crore (Centre ₹3.59 lakh crore).

  24. PLI food processing: outlay and period?
    Show answer

    ₹10,900 crore; 2021-22 to 2026-27; launched 31 Mar 2021.

  25. PM-JANMAN: launch, outlay, target?
    Show answer

    15 Nov 2023; ₹24,000 crore; 75 PVTGs in 18 States and 1 UT.

  26. PMFBY: premium rates and delay interest?
    Show answer

    2% kharif, 1.5% rabi, 5% commercial/horticulture; 12% interest if claims not settled in 21 days.

  27. Who manages the SWAMIH Fund?
    Show answer

    SBICAP Ventures Ltd; sponsor DEA; Cabinet approval 6 Nov 2019.

  28. National Cooperation Policy: launch date and nodal financier?
    Show answer

    24 Jul 2025; NCDC (set up 1963).

Growth, outlook and data releases

1/64★ Must-learnGDP First Advance Estimates FY2025-26 (Jan 2026)

In plain words

A first rough estimate of how much the economy grew this year, made before the year ends. It says India grew 7.4% in FY26, faster than 6.5% the year before.

  • Released by NSO (National Statistics Office, MoSPI) at constant (2011-12) and current prices
  • Real GDP growth FY26: 7.4% (FY25: 6.5%)
  • Real GDP ₹201.90 lakh crore; nominal GDP growth 8.0% → ₹357.14 lakh crore; real GVA growth 7.3% (₹184.50 lakh crore)
  • Sectors (real): finance, real estate and professional services + public administration 9.9%; trade, hotels, transport 7.5%; manufacturing and construction 7.0%; agriculture 3.1%; utilities 2.1%; GFCF 7.8% (7.1% earlier); PFCE 7.0%
Exam angle

What is the first advance estimate of real GDP growth for FY2025-26?

2/64★ Must-learnCPI inflation Dec 2025 and base-year revision

In plain words

CPI measures how fast consumer prices rise. December's 1.33% was the last reading on the old basket of goods; from January a new, wider basket (base year 2024) is used, with less weight on food.

  • Dec 2025 all-India CPI inflation: 1.33% (provisional), up 62 bps
  • Last release on old base (2012=100); new CPI series on base 2024=100 due 12 Feb 2026
  • Food & beverages weight in new CPI: 45.86% → 36.75%
  • New series: items 299 → 358; housing (incl. water, electricity, gas) weight 10.07% → 17.66%; first used for January 2026 data (released in Feb)
  • Timeline: Dec 2025 (old series) 1.33% → Jan 2026 (first on new series, base 2024) 2.75% → Feb 2026 3.21% (rural 3.37%, urban 3.02%, food 3.47%)
  • New GDP series (base 2022-23) due 27 Feb 2026; new IIP series (base 2022-23) due 28 May 2026
Exam angle

Which base year does the revised CPI series use, and what happens to the food weight?

Remember

Food weight falls 45.86% → 36.75% because consumption patterns have changed since 2011-12.

Three global bodies (UN, World Bank, IMF) each forecast India's growth for the coming years. Questions usually ask India's figure from one of them.

  • Real GDP growth projection FY27: 6.8–7.2%

India's economy is now the 4th largest in the world, having passed Japan, and is expected to pass Germany by 2030.

  • GDP USD 4.18 trillion: India passes Japan; expected to pass Germany for 3rd place by 2030 (US 1st, China 2nd)
  • Real GDP growth: Q4 FY25 7.4% → Q1 FY26 7.8% → Q2 FY26 8.2%

8/64★ Must-learnStartup India: 10 years and National Startup Awards 5.0

In plain words

Startup India turned 10, and the government celebrated with awards and state rankings. The number of recognised startups grew from under 500 in 2014 to over 2 lakh.

  • 16 Jan = National Startup Day; Startup India launched 2016. DPIIT-recognised startups above 2.07 lakh (under 500 in 2014); unicorns 4 → nearly 125; India = 3rd largest startup ecosystem
  • Maharashtra leads with 35,991 recognised startups; startups with at least one woman director: 99,640
  • States' Startup Ranking (SRF 5.0): Best Performers: Gujarat (Category A); Arunachal Pradesh and Goa (Category B). Top Performers include Karnataka, Punjab, Tamil Nadu, UP (A) and Himachal Pradesh (B)
  • Support schemes: Fund of Funds for Startups ₹10,000 crore (managed by SIDBI, invests via SEBI-registered AIFs); Seed Fund Scheme ₹945 crore; Atal Innovation Mission (NITI, 2016) ₹2,750 crore till Mar 2028; GENESIS (MeitY, 2022) ₹490 crore, 1,600 deep-tech startups
  • About 50% of startups from Tier II/III cities

  • January 2026: gross GST ₹1.93 trillion (+6.2%, import-led); UPI 21.70 bn transactions (+28%), ₹28.33 lakh crore (+21%)
  • HSBC PMI: manufacturing 55.0 (38-month low; Nov 56.6); services 58 (Nov 59.8). Above 50 = expansion
  • India has the world's 2nd-largest 5G subscriber base (400 million+) after China

12/64★ Must-learnNational Accounts base year revision (GDP new series)

In plain words

The government changed the base year (the reference year that all GDP is compared with) from 2011-12 to 2022-23. This makes GDP figures more up to date and also changes numbers like the fiscal deficit ratio.

  • MoSPI revising base year of national accounts to 2022-23; new series published 27 Feb 2026: nominal GDP ₹318.07 lakh crore (2024-25), ₹289.84 lakh crore (2023-24)
  • Fiscal deficit/GDP revised on new base: 2024-25 4.9% (was 4.8%), 2023-24 5.7% (5.63%), 2022-23 6.7% (6.4%)
  • Advisory Committee on National Account Statistics (ACNAS) chaired by Prof B N Goldar; 5 sub-committees (new data sources, methodology, constant price, regional accounts, SNA 2025 update)
  • Second Advance Estimates FY2025-26 (new series): real GDP ₹322.58 lakh crore, growth 7.6% (over ₹299.89 lakh crore); nominal GDP ₹345.47 lakh crore, +8.6%; real GVA ₹294.40 lakh crore, +7.7%; Q2 8.4%, Q3 7.8%
  • Earlier years (new base): real GDP growth FY24 7.2%, FY25 7.1%; gross capital formation 34.3% of GDP (FY25); gross saving ₹111.13 lakh crore (households 62.1%); per capita NNI ₹1,92,774
  • Method changes: double deflation in manufacturing and agriculture; Supply and Use Tables aligned; COICOP 2018; hired domestic workers and gig/platform activity included; old 2011-12 series replaced. Other rebasing: CPI 2024 (12 Feb 2026), IIP 2022-23 (28 May 2026); trade indices base FY2022-23 (earlier FY2012-13)
Exam angle

Who chaired the committee advising on the new GDP series?

13/64★ Must-learnEconomic Stabilisation Fund and West Asia shock (Mar 2026)

In plain words

West Asia's war hit energy supplies and prices. The government proposed a ₹1 lakh crore fund to cushion India and rationed natural gas by priority.

  • FM Sitharaman: proposed Economic Stabilisation Fund corpus ₹1 lakh crore to buffer shocks from the West Asia war; ~₹57,000 crore fresh cash, ₹43,000 crore from savings
  • Government invoked Essential Commodities Act 1955 to ration natural gas: Tier 1 domestic PNG, CNG, LPG production (100%); Tier 2 fertiliser 70%; Tier 3 and 4 industry/CGD 80%; LPG refill gap 21 → 25 days
  • WTO Global Trade Outlook (Mar 2026): world trade growth 4.6% (2025) → 1.9% (2026) → 2.6% (2027); global GDP 2.9% → 2.8%

14/64★ Must-learnIIP and EPF decisions (Mar 2026)

In plain words

Two monthly numbers on factory output (IIP) and a decision on how much interest provident fund savers get this year (8.25%).

  • IIP January 2026: 4.8% (December 7.8%); base 2011-12=100
  • EPFO 239th CBT (chair Mansukh Mandaviya): 8.25% interest recommended for FY 2025-26; new EPF Scheme 2026, EPS 2026 and EDLI Scheme 2026; inoperative account = no contribution for 3 years after age 55; pilot auto-settlement for balances up to ₹1,000
  • IIP Feb 2026: 5.2% (Jan 2026 quick estimate 4.8%); base 2011-12; quick estimate released on 28th of each month

The monthly health check of eight core industries (coal, steel, power and others) that feed the rest of the economy, plus some energy and farm facts.

  • Core weights: refinery products 28.04%, electricity 19.85%, steel 17.92%, coal 10.33%, crude oil 8.98%, natural gas 6.88%, cement 5.37%, fertilizers 2.63%

February's main economic numbers in one place: inflation, factory output, business activity (PMI) and GST collection.

Trade, FDI and tax

18/64★ Must-learnIndia–EU Free Trade Agreement concluded (26 Jan 2026)

In plain words

India and the European Union finished a free trade deal after nearly 20 years of talks. It lowers barriers on goods and services and opens parts of insurance and banking.

  • Talks launched 2007, relaunched 2022, concluded 26 Jan 2026; EU = India's 22nd FTA partner
  • EU banks allowed 15 branches over 4 years (earlier offer 12)
  • India binds FDI in insurance at 100%; EU eases visas for Indian professionals; AYUSH practitioners allowed
  • Trade: India–EU goods trade USD 136.53 bn (2024-25; exports 75.85, imports 60.68); EU is the largest goods partner; services trade ~USD 83 bn (2024)
  • EU FDI in India 2024: over €132 bn (leading investor)
  • Context: EU leaders Costa and von der Leyen were chief guests at the 77th Republic Day (26 Jan); 16th India–EU Summit on 27 Jan 2026 (15th was virtual, 15 Jul 2020); strategic partners since 2004
  • Mar 2026: India and EU grant each other MFN status for 5 years from FTA entry into force (review in 4th year; auto-terminates unless extended)
  • India–Euratom cooperation on peaceful nuclear energy agreed. Euratom = European Atomic Energy Community, set up by Treaty of Rome 1957
  • India's access: 97% of tariff lines (99.5% of trade value); India offers 92.1% of lines (97.5% of EU exports); social security agreements framework in 5 years; EU 144 services subsectors; India = 4th, EU = 2nd largest economies (25% of world GDP)
  • Financial services: banking FDI 74%, insurance 100%; EU offer excludes meat, dairy, honey, rice, sugar, tobacco; bilateral safeguard transition period 22 years; MFN in services active for 5 years; India banks in EU: SBI, BoB, BoI (5 branches)
Exam angle

Which number of FTA partner is the EU, and how many bank branches did India allow?

Remember

15 branches in 4 years (not 12). FTA partners earlier: Mauritius, UAE, UK, EFTA, Oman, Australia; New Zealand concluded 2025.

19/64★ Must-learnTobacco and pan masala: new levies from 1 Feb 2026

In plain words

From 1 Feb 2026 tobacco and pan masala get a new set of taxes (GST at 40%, a health cess and extra excise duty) in place of the old compensation cess.

  • Pan masala, cigarettes, tobacco: GST 40%; biris: 18%
  • Health and national security cess on pan masala; additional excise duty on tobacco
  • These levies replace the compensation cess on sin goods
Exam angle

What happens to the GST compensation cess on sin goods from 1 Feb 2026?

22/64★ Must-learnIndia–France DTAC amending protocol; India–GCC FTA

In plain words

Two international tax and trade items: India and France changed their tax treaty, and India started talks for a trade deal with the Gulf countries (GCC).

  • India–France DTAC (signed 29 Sept 1992) amended: capital gains on shares taxed by company's residence state; MFN clause deleted; dividend tax single 10% → 5% (holding ≥10%) / 15% (others); Service PE added; BEPS MLI provisions incorporated
  • India–GCC FTA: Terms of Reference signed (Feb 2026); GCC = India's largest trading bloc: trade $178.56 bn in FY25 (exports $56.87 bn, imports $121.68 bn), 15.42% of India's trade; ~10 million Indians in GCC
Exam angle

What is the new dividend tax rate split in the India-France DTAC?

23/64★ Must-learnIncome Tax Rules 2026 (effective 1 Apr 2026)

In plain words

New income tax rules, in force from 1 April 2026, put the simplified Income-tax Act 2025 into practice, with changes like HRA in 8 cities and new PAN limits.

  • Implement Income-tax Act, 2025 (replaces 1961 Act): sections 819 → 536, chapters 47 → 23, words 5.12 lakh → 2.6 lakh
  • HRA 50% exemption: 4 metros → 8 cities (Mumbai, Kolkata, Delhi, Chennai, Hyderabad, Pune, Ahmedabad, Bengaluru); others 40%
  • PAN: cash deposit/withdrawal ₹10 lakh a year (earlier ₹50,000 a day); vehicle above ₹5 lakh; hotel/event above ₹1 lakh (earlier ₹50,000); property above ₹20 lakh (earlier ₹10 lakh)
  • Significant economic presence: ₹2 crore transactions or 3 lakh users; exchanges keep audit trails 7 years; credit cards need PAN
Exam angle

By how many sections was the new Income-tax Act shortened?

24/64★ Must-learnMinimum public shareholding norms revised (SCRR 1957)

In plain words

Listed companies must keep a minimum share held by the public. The rules now give larger companies more time to reach 25%, and the minimum offer in an IPO is cut.

  • Post-issue capital categories: 3 → 6 (₹1,600 cr; to ₹4,000 cr; to ₹50,000 cr; to ₹1 lakh cr; to ₹5 lakh cr; above ₹5 lakh cr)
  • Time to reach 25% public shareholding: 3 to 10 years; minimum IPO offer 5% → 2.5%

Banking

25/64☆ Good to knowSIDBI equity support and pay revisions

  • Cabinet: ₹5,000 crore equity in SIDBI via DFS: ₹3,000 cr (FY26) at book value ₹568.65, ₹1,000 cr each in FY27 and FY28
  • PSGICs wage revision from 1 Aug 2022: 12.41% overall; NPS contribution 10% → 14%; outgo ₹8,170.30 cr
  • NABARD and RBI revision effective 1 Nov 2022: NABARD pay hike ~20%; RBI pension +10%, cost ₹2,696.82 cr
  • Cabinet (Jan 2026): ₹5,000 crore equity to SIDBI in three tranches (₹3,000 crore FY26; ₹1,000 crore each FY27, FY28); MSMEs assisted 76.26 lakh → ≈102 lakh by FY28

26/64☆ Good to knowBanking data: credit-deposit ratio and co-operative bank directors

  • Credit-deposit ratio at record 81.6% (15 Dec 2025); Q3 growth strongest at Bank of Maharashtra 19.61%, Central Bank 19.57%, Bank of India 15.07%
  • Industry credit growth 9.6% (Nov 2025) vs 8.3%; retail 12.8% vs 13.4%; NBFCs 9.5% vs 7.5%
  • Another report puts record CD ratio at 81.75%: 2025 credit +11.4% vs deposits +10.1%. SBI Research: optimal CD ratio 76–80%; above 80% (70% for foreign banks) hurts profitability
  • RBI draft: co-operative bank directors with 10 years continuous tenure need a 3-year cooling-off before reappointment (cap raised 8 → 10 years in Aug 2025)

27/64☆ Good to knowNiryat Protsahan: interest subvention under Export Promotion Mission

  • Interest subvention on pre- and post-shipment export credit under the Export Promotion Mission (EPM), scheme named Niryat Protsahan
  • Export Promotion Mission (approved Nov 2025): outlay ₹25,060 crore, FY2025-26 to FY2030-31; two sub-schemes → NIRYAT PROTSAHAN (trade finance) and NIRYAT DISHA (non-financial: market access, branding, logistics)
  • Interest subvention: base 2.75%; cap ₹50 lakh per IEC (Importer Exporter Code) in FY26; positive list ≈75% of tariff lines (HS 6-digit); rates reviewed in March and September
  • Collateral support with CGTMSE (Credit Guarantee Fund Trust for Micro and Small Enterprises): cover up to 85% (micro, small) and 65% (medium); max exposure ₹10 crore per exporter per year; pilot basis
  • Feb 2026: seven more interventions launched → 10 of 11 operational. Niryat Protsahan: export factoring (2.75% subvention, ₹50 lakh cap); e-commerce credit (direct ₹50 lakh with 90% cover; overseas inventory ₹5 crore with 75% cover); emerging markets risk-sharing 10–90%. 29/64★ Must-learnRBI recognises FEDAI as a Self-Regulatory Organisation (SRO)

In plain words

RBI recognised FEDAI, the association of banks that deal in foreign exchange, as a self-regulatory organisation: a body that keeps its own members in line under a regulator.

  • FEDAI (Foreign Exchange Dealers' Association of India): set up 1958, association of Authorised Dealer banks, incorporated under Section 25 of Companies Act 1956
  • SRO = non-government body authorised by a regulator to oversee a sector

40/64★ Must-learn100% FDI in insurance (automatic route)

In plain words

Foreign companies can now own up to 100% of an Indian insurer without government approval (earlier limit 74%).

  • Feb 2026: DPIIT operationalised up to 100% foreign ownership in insurance; aligns with Sabka Bima Sabki Raksha (Amendment of Insurance Laws) Act, 2025 (in force from 5 Feb, except Section 25)
  • Earlier: 74% → 100%; insurance intermediaries 100% since 2020; LIC 20% FDI since 2022
  • Chair/MD/CEO must be a resident Indian citizen; pricing per RBI FEMA norms; majority foreign-owned intermediaries must be limited companies
  • Also applies to brokers, reinsurance brokers, corporate agents, TPAs, surveyors, MGAs, insurance repositories
  • Sabka Bima Sabki Raksha (Amendment of Insurance Laws) Act 2025 amends Insurance Act 1938, LIC Act 1956, IRDAI Act 1999: one-time registration for intermediaries; IRDAI approval threshold for share transfer 1% → 5%; foreign reinsurer Net Owned Fund ₹5,000 crore → ₹1,000 crore
Exam angle

Which Act raised FDI in insurance to 100%?

43/64★ Must-learnCredit Guarantee Scheme for Exporters (CGSE) operational

In plain words

A scheme that lets small exporters get extra loans without giving collateral, because the government guarantees 100% of the additional loan.

  • Operational from 1 Dec 2025 via Jan Samarth portal; run by DFS (Department of Financial Services) through NCGTC (National Credit Guarantee Trustee Company Ltd)
  • 100% guarantee on additional loan → collateral-free credit up to ₹20,000 crore to direct and indirect exporter MSMEs through MLIs (Member Lending Institutions)
  • Extra working capital up to 20% of existing export credit limit; open till 31 Mar 2026 or until ₹20,000 crore guarantees issued
  • Context: exports ≈21% of GDP; MSMEs ≈45% of exports
Exam angle

What guarantee cover and corpus does CGSE offer?

Remember

100% cover, ₹20,000 cr, 20% of limit.

64/64★ Must-learnRELIEF: export support amid West Asia disruptions

In plain words

With shipping and insurance costs shooting up because of the West Asia war, the government is covering exporters' extra risk through ECGC.

  • Three parts: (1) up to 100% extra risk cover for insured consignments 14 Feb–15 Mar 2026; (2) up to 95% cover for consignments 16 Mar–15 Jun 2026; (3) up to 50% reimbursement (max ₹50 lakh) for non-insured MSME exporters

January–March 2026 · Section 2Regulatory Bodies, PIB, Circulars and Notifications67 items · 42 ★ must-learn · 25 ☆ good to know

RBI

1/67★ Must-learnRBI: related-party lending rules (effective 1 Apr 2026)

In plain words

RBI has set rules on when a bank or NBFC lends to its own promoters, directors or their relatives. The aim is to stop such loans from being given on easy terms.

  • Credit Risk Management Amendment Directions, 2026 for banks and NBFCs; effective 1 Apr 2026
  • Related party: promoters, directors, KMP and relatives, >10% shareholders, entities they control; trusts covered; banks also get "reciprocally related persons"
  • "Lending" = fund-based + non-fund-based + debt investments; equity investment excluded
  • Board must approve a related-party lending policy; material loans go to the board or a committee (not the audit committee)
EntitySize / layerBoard approval above (single transaction)
BanksAssets above ₹10 lakh crore₹25 crore
Banks₹1–10 lakh crore₹10 crore
BanksBelow ₹1 lakh crore₹5 crore
NBFCsUpper / Top layer₹10 crore
NBFCsMiddle layer₹5 crore
NBFCsBase layer₹1 crore

2/67★ Must-learnRBI: risk weights for NBFC infrastructure lending eased

In plain words

RBI cut the capital NBFCs must hold against loans to good infrastructure projects, so they can lend more to them.

  • Amendment Directions: "high-quality" project definition widened to include revenues from concessions or contracts of Centre, States, PSUs, statutory or regulatory bodies
  • Repayment needed for 75% risk weight: 5% (proposed) → 2%; for 50% risk weight: 10% → 5%
  • Effective 1 Apr 2026 (earlier adoption allowed); apply at next review or by 31 Mar 2027

3/67★ Must-learnRBI draft: bank dividend payout cap 75% of PAT

In plain words

RBI proposed that banks may pay out up to 75% of profit as dividend (earlier 40%), but a weaker bank must still keep more back.

  • Proposed cap: 40% → 75% of profit after tax
  • Payout linked to CET1 ratio through a ten-bucket structure; adjusted PAT is after deducting net NPAs
  • No dividend from exceptional income, unrealised fair-value gains or reversal of excess provisions

4/67★ Must-learnRBI: Internal Ombudsman Directions, 2026

In plain words

Every bank and many other regulated lenders must have an internal ombudsman, a senior officer who reviews complaints before they reach RBI.

  • Covers commercial banks, SFBs, payments banks, NBFCs, non-bank PPI issuers, credit information companies; effective 30 Jun 2026
  • Internal Ombudsman: serving or retired officer of rank equal to General Manager, 7 years' experience, age not above 70
  • Deputy IO: rank equal to Deputy General Manager, 5 years' experience, age not above 70
  • Every bank must appoint at least one IO
  • IO tenure: fixed, not less than 3 years, total not above 5 years; final decision to complainant within 30 days; replaces 2023 Internal Ombudsman Directions

5/67★ Must-learnRBI: FEMA (Guarantees) Regulations, 2026

In plain words

New RBI rules on guarantees where a non-resident is involved, replacing older circulars, with limits on residents guaranteeing for NRIs.

  • Framework for guarantees involving non-residents; earlier A.P. (DIR Series) circulars superseded
  • Resident Indians barred from issuing credit guarantees in favour of NRIs; surety or principal debtor role only if the transaction is permitted and both parties are eligible to lend or borrow
  • Reporting: guarantees issued, modified or invoked reported quarterly via AD banks; trade-credit guarantee quarterly reporting discontinued from quarter ending Mar 2026

6/67★ Must-learnPayments Regulatory Board (PRB): first meeting

In plain words

The Payments Regulatory Board, set up by law in May 2025, held its first meeting. It is the body that now oversees payment systems.

  • Chaired by RBI Governor Sanjay Malhotra, Mumbai
  • PRB created by amendment to the Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 2007, in force 9 May 2025

7/67★ Must-learnInflation target to be retained at 4%

In plain words

RBI's inflation target is 4% (with a 2–6% band), and the government was expected to keep it for the next five years.

  • RBI target 4%, midpoint of a 2–6% band set by the government
  • Target is reviewed every five years; next due in March 2026; Government likely to retain it

8/67★ Must-learnDICGC deposit insurance data (March 2025)

In plain words

DICGC insures bank deposits up to ₹5 lakh per depositor. The data shows how many accounts and how much money are covered.

  • Insured: 97.6% of accounts and 41.5% of deposit value (₹100.12 lakh crore of ₹241.08 lakh crore); a year earlier 43.1%
  • Cover ₹5 lakh per depositor; premium flat 12 paise per ₹100 of assessable deposits
  • 1,982 insured banks (139 commercial + 1,843 co-operative); deposit insurance fund ₹2.29 lakh crore (+15.2%)
  • FY25 claims settled ₹476 crore; recoveries ₹1,309 crore; DICGC set up 1961

9/67☆ Good to knowRBI Financial Stability Report (Dec 2025): key points

  • Unsecured retail loans = 53.1% of SCB retail slippages (Sept 2025)
  • Slippage ratio in unsecured retail: PSBs 1.4% < PVBs 4.7% < foreign banks 6.8% < SFBs 10.4%
  • Gold loans = 5.8% of total advances of SCBs and NBFCs; sharp growth
  • Yield curve steepened: short rates down with easing, long yields up on heavy G-sec supply

10/67☆ Good to knowRBI: sDQI, GNCTD agreement, WMA and reserves

  • sDQI (Supervisory Data Quality Index) Sept 2025: SCBs 90.7 (87 banks); bands: below 70 major concerns, 70–80 needs improvement, 80–90 acceptable, above 90 good; no entity below 80
  • RBI–Delhi (GNCTD) agreement under Sec 21A, RBI Act 1934 from 9 Jan 2026: RBI does general banking business and manages public debt
  • WMA limit Delhi ₹890 crore; aggregate States/UTs limit ₹60,118 → ₹61,008 crore; highest UP ₹6,519 crore
  • Gold reserves 880.18 tonnes (Oct 2025) vs 866.8 tonnes a year earlier; US Treasury holding below $200 bn
  • India's International Investment Position (Sept 2025): net claims of non-residents $274.3 bn (down $38.4 bn); assets/liabilities 81.3%; reserve assets $700.1 bn

11/67★ Must-learnRBI Ombudsman: compensation limits

In plain words

This card says how much compensation the RBI Ombudsman can award when a bank customer's complaint is upheld.

  • No limit on the amount in dispute the RBI Ombudsman can settle or award
  • Compensation for consequential loss up to ₹30 lakh; plus up to ₹3 lakh for time lost, expenses, harassment or mental anguish
  • Online complaints registered on the CMS portal (cms.rbi.org.in)
Remember

₹30 lakh (loss) + ₹3 lakh (harassment).

12/67☆ Good to knowRBI: forex rules (draft NOP norms, FEMA export-import regulations)

  • Draft revised net open position (NOP) and forex capital charge norms aligned to Basel; proposed from 1 Apr 2027; computed at close of each business day
  • FEMA (Export and Import of Goods and Services) Regulations, 2026: in force from 1 Oct 2026; principle-based; EDF details of non-EDI port exports entered into EDPMS within 5 working days

13/67★ Must-learnRBI: priority sector lending (PSL) tightening

In plain words

RBI tightened the rules on counting loans as priority sector lending (farm, small business), so the same loan cannot be claimed by two banks.

  • External auditor certificate needed from NBFCs, NBFC-MFIs and HFCs so no loan is claimed as PSL by more than one bank
  • Bank loans to NBFCs for on-lending to agriculture and MSEs count as PSL up to 5% of the bank's previous-year PSL; to NBFC-MFIs up to 10%
  • Credit to NCDC for on-lending to co-operatives now eligible

14/67★ Must-learnRBI: resolution framework for loans hit by natural calamities

In plain words

When a flood or other disaster hits borrowers, banks can now relax repayments (rescheduling or moratorium) under a clear RBI framework.

  • Principle-based regime; accounts standard and not beyond SMA-0 (up to 30 days overdue) on the event date are eligible
  • Relief: rescheduling, conversion of interest into another facility, moratorium; considers SLBC and DCC decisions
  • Effective 1 Apr 2026

15/67☆ Good to knowRBI report: State Finances, Budgets 2025-26

  • States grouped by share aged 60+: youthful <10%, intermediate 10–15%, ageing 15%+
  • Old-age dependency ratio ranges from 14 (Bihar) to 30.1 (Kerala); pensions take over 30% of social-sector spending in ageing states
  • Consolidated states' fiscal deficit widened to 3.3% of GDP in 2024-25 after three years of consolidation
  • Working-age population peaks nationally around 2031; by 2036 over half the states have 15%+ aged 60+

16/67☆ Good to knowRBI: BRICS CBDC link and e-rupee

  • RBI proposed linking BRICS central bank digital currencies for cross-border payments; wants it on the 2026 BRICS summit agenda
  • e-rupee: 7 million retail users since Dec 2022 launch; offline payments, programmability, fintech wallets
  • Digital food currency pilot (programmable e-rupee tokens usable only at ration shops) in Chandigarh, Puducherry and 3 Gujarat districts; RBI regulator, Consumer Affairs Ministry implements with NPCI

17/67★ Must-learnDICGC: Risk-Based Premium (RBP) framework

In plain words

Banks used to pay DICGC the same deposit insurance premium. From 1 April 2026 a riskier bank pays more and a safer bank pays less.

  • Replaces flat premium (12 paise per ₹100 of assessable deposits) with risk-based rates from 1 Apr 2026; reviewed at least every 3 years; Sec 15(1) DICGC Act 1961 allows differential premium
  • Tier 1: scheduled commercial banks (not RRBs): supervisory ratings + CAMELS + potential loss to Deposit Insurance Fund. Tier 2: RRBs and co-operative banks: CAMELS + potential loss
  • Maximum risk-model incentive 33.33% off card rate; vintage incentive up to 25%
  • Local Area Banks and Payments Banks stay at card rate; UCBs under SAF/PCA stay at card rate until they exit

18/67★ Must-learnRBI: Voluntary Retention Route (VRR) folded into General Route

In plain words

A special route for foreign investors in Indian bonds (VRR) is merged into the normal route, which simplifies the rules.

  • From 1 Apr 2026 VRR investments count under FPI limits of the General Route (G-secs, T-bills, SDLs, corporate debt); existing VRR holdings transferred; FPIs may exit after minimum retention period
  • Issued under Sec 10(4) and 11(1), FEMA 1999

19/67★ Must-learnRBI: MSME lending directions amended (2026)

In plain words

Small businesses can borrow up to ₹20 lakh from banks without giving collateral, and banks may raise this to ₹25 lakh.

  • No collateral for MSE loans up to ₹20 lakh; collateral-free ₹20 lakh also for all PMEGP units (administered by KVIC)
  • Banks may raise the collateral-free limit to ₹25 lakh for MSEs with good track record; effective 1 Apr 2026; voluntarily pledged gold/silver not a violation
Remember

₹20 lakh now (earlier norm ₹10 lakh under PMEGP guidance).

20/67☆ Good to knowRBI: digital fraud relief, REIT lending, BC review

  • Small digital fraud victims: one-time compensation up to ₹25,000 (85% of loss or ₹25,000, whichever lower); banks 15%, customer 15%; draft (RBI Responsible Business Conduct Third Amendment Directions 2026, from 1 Jul 2026): fraud reported within 5 days to bank and 1930 helpline; zero liability for bank deficiency or third-party breach reported in 5 days; instant SMS alert for transactions above ₹500
  • Banks may lend directly to REITs (India has 5 listed REITs: Brookfield India, Embassy Office Parks, Knowledge Realty, Mindspace, Nexus Select)
  • RBI committee (RBI, DFS, IBA, NABARD) to review business correspondent guidelines

21/67★ Must-learnRBI final acquisition finance framework for banks

In plain words

Banks may now lend for company takeovers, but under strict limits (at most 75% of the deal value, debt not above 3 times equity).

  • Overseas syndicated acquisition finance: Indian banks capped at 20% of total funding (draft 10%)
  • Bank financing at most 75% of acquisition value; post-deal consolidated debt-to-equity at most 3:1
  • Acquirer: listed with net worth ≥ ₹500 crore and profit in 3 preceding years; unlisted also needs investment-grade rating (BBB− or above)
  • Bridge finance to be replaced by equity within 12 months; effective 1 Jul 2026 (RBI deferred from 1 Apr in Mar 2026)

22/67★ Must-learnRBI: ECB framework eased (Borrowing and Lending First Amendment Regulations 2026)

In plain words

RBI made it easier for Indian companies to borrow from abroad (ECB): a higher limit, fewer cost restrictions and more allowed uses.

  • Limit: earlier $750 million → higher of $1 billion or 300% of net worth
  • Minimum average maturity 3 years; manufacturers may raise up to $150 million at 1–3 years
  • All-in-cost cap removed; acquisition of "control" a permitted end-use; RBI-regulated entities may on-lend ECB to individuals (not for real estate business)

23/67☆ Good to knowRBI: broker funding, UCB lending, UTI

  • Bank credit to stockbrokers only fully secured (from 1 Jul 2026; deferred from 1 Apr); bank guarantees to exchanges need 50% collateral (25% cash); equity collateral minimum 40% haircut
  • Draft for UCBs: unsecured advances ceiling 20% of total advances (earlier 10% of total assets); consumer durable limit ₹2.5 lakh
  • Unique Transaction Identifier for OTC derivatives deferred to 1 Jan 2027
  • Draft revised Lead Bank Scheme (introduced 1969) strengthens SLBC and Lead District Manager offices

24/67★ Must-learnRBI Digital Payments Index (RBI-DPI)

In plain words

RBI's Digital Payments Index measures how much digital payment is used in India. The higher the number, the more digital the country.

  • Sept 2025: 516.76 (March 2025: 493.22); base March 2018 = 100; published since 2021
  • Five parameters: Payment Enablers 25%, Infrastructure demand-side 10%, supply-side 15%, Payment Performance 45%, Consumer Centricity 5%

25/67☆ Good to knowRBI: Financial Literacy Week and data centre

  • 11th Financial Literacy Week, 9–13 Feb 2026 (held yearly since 2016); theme "KYC – Your First Step to Safe Banking"
  • RBI's second data centre at Bhubaneswar (primary at Kharghar, Navi Mumbai)
  • NRI deposits Apr–Dec 2025: $11.20 bn (−15.97%) vs $13.33 bn; schemes: FCNR, NRE, NRO

26/67★ Must-learnRBI: 2FA from 1 Apr 2026; dividend cap; CCR norms

In plain words

Three RBI changes: two-step verification for digital payments, a 75% dividend cap for banks and new capital rules for derivative exposures.

  • Two-factor authentication compulsory for all digital payments from 1 Apr 2026 (one factor dynamic); cross-border card-not-present deadline 1 Oct 2026
  • Bank dividend cap: payout up to 75% of PAT, linked to CET1; adjusted PAT = PAT − 50% of net NPAs; from FY27
  • Counterparty credit risk (Capital Adequacy Third Amendment Directions 2026): equity add-on 6%/8%/10% by maturity (≤1, 1–5, >5 years); interest rate contracts >1 year floor 0.50%; QCCP trade exposure risk weight 2%
Exam angle

What maximum share of PAT can banks pay out as dividend?

27/67★ Must-learnRBI Central Board 622nd meeting (Patna); BoP Q3 2025-26

In plain words

RBI's board met in Patna and approved the 2026-27 budget and the Utkarsh 2029 strategy. The card also records India's external-sector data for Oct–Dec 2025.

  • 622nd meeting chaired by Governor Sanjay Malhotra: approved budget 2026-27 and Utkarsh 2029 medium-term strategy 2026-29 (first framework Utkarsh 2022, Jul 2019)
  • BoP Oct–Dec 2025: CAD Apr–Dec 2025 $30.1 bn (1% of GDP) vs $36.6 bn; net FDI $3 bn vs $0.6 bn; FPI net outflow $4.3 bn (vs inflow $9.4 bn); reserves depleted $30.8 bn
  • .bank.in domain: RBI won Central Banking "Initiative of the Year"; India first to mandate exclusive banking domain; IDRBT sole registrar
  • DEA Fund: PSBs transferred ₹60,518 crore (to 31 Jan 2026); funds unoperated 10 years; effective 24 May 2014 (Section 26A BR Act); Banking Laws (Amendment) Act 2025: up to 4 nominees

28/67☆ Good to knowRBI: surveys, awareness, penalties

  • March 2026 consumer confidence surveys: Rural (31 States/UTs, Ipsos) and Urban (19 cities, Hansa Research)
  • 6th Digital Payments Awareness Week 9–15 Mar ("Har Payment Digital"); campaign "Thoda Dhyan Se"
  • Penalty ₹11.50 lakh on Mahindra and Mahindra Financial Services (Fair Practices Code, Internal Ombudsman)
  • Digital lending apps directory live since 1 Jul 2025; MeitY blocks fraudulent loan apps under Section 69A IT Act; states borrowed ₹45,960 crore via SGS (Karnataka ₹10,000 crore)

29/67★ Must-learnInflation target renewed to March 2031; Payments Vision 2028

In plain words

The government kept RBI's 4% inflation target for another five years (to March 2031). RBI also released its payments plan to 2028 and fixed the government's short-term borrowing limit.

  • Government (with RBI) notified 4% CPI target, ±2% band (2–6%) for 1 Apr 2026 – 31 Mar 2031; first mandate 2016 for five years to 31 Mar 2021
  • Payments Vision 2028 ("Shaping India's Payment Frontier"): till Dec 2028; 15 initiatives; PaSS (Payments Switching Service), shared liability of issuer and beneficiary bank for unauthorised payments, TReDS integration; vision documents since 2001
  • WMA limit H1 2026-27: ₹2.5 lakh crore; WMA at repo rate, overdraft repo +2%; fresh market loan at 75% use
  • WMA: introduced 1 Apr 1997 under Section 17(5) RBI Act (replaced ad-hoc treasury bills); 90 days; states' Special Drawing Facility 1% below repo
  • Payments Vision history: 2001-04 RTGS (2004); 2005-08 PSS Act 2007, NPCI 2008; 2019-21 "Empowering Exceptional (E)-payment Experience"; 2025 "4Es"; India handles ~50% of global real-time payments; 2028 also studies a uniform Domestic LEI (DLEI)
Exam angle

What is the inflation target band for 2026-2031?

30/67☆ Good to knowRBI: FX position, ECB filing, fraud infrastructure

  • Authorised dealers' net open position in INR (onshore deliverable) capped at $100 million daily; comply by 10 Apr 2026
  • ECB returns forwarded by AD-I banks within 7 days from 1 Apr 2026; Form ECB 2 late fee per return
  • IDPIC (Indian Digital Payment Intelligence Corporation; Section 8 company, 16 Oct 2025); MuleHunter.AI live in 26 banks; DigiLocker verification for high-risk transactions from 1 Apr 2026
  • Unclaimed money portals: UDGAM (RBI deposits), Bima Bharosa (IRDAI), MITRA (SEBI mutual funds)
  • Penalty ₹31.8 lakh on HSBC (inoperative accounts and unclaimed deposits)

SEBI

31/67★ Must-learnSEBI: merchant banker net worth overhaul

In plain words

SEBI raised the capital merchant bankers (the firms that manage IPOs) must hold, so only financially stronger ones can handle large issues.

  • New applicants from 3 Jan 2026 must meet higher net worth and liquid net worth; existing bankers phased
  • Underwriting obligations capped at 20× liquid net worth; existing entities comply by 2 Jan 2028
  • Minimum revenue from permitted activities (3-year cumulative): Category I ₹25 crore; Category II ₹5 crore

32/67☆ Good to knowSEBI: brokers, AIFs and distributors

  • Brokers may offer services regulated by IRDAI or RBI (still regulated by those bodies); must appoint a compliance officer; may underwrite from own net worth; FPIs can apply for a DSC with the common application form
  • Technical-glitch framework now only for brokers with over 10,000 clients (~60% exit); report time 1 hr → 2 hrs; single Common Reporting Platform. Proposed margin-trading net worth ₹3 cr → ₹5 cr
  • AIF manager compliance officers need NISM certification from 1 Jan 2027 (NISM Series-III-C)
  • MF distributor extra incentive (B-30 cities and new women investors) deferred to 1 Mar 2026: 1% of first lump sum or first-year SIP, up to ₹2,000, if invested for a year

33/67★ Must-learnSEBI: closing auction session (CAS) and FPI reforms

In plain words

A 20-minute auction at market close will decide each share's closing price, and foreign investors get one simplified registration.

  • CAS in equity cash market: 20 minutes, 3:15–3:35 pm; starts with stocks having derivatives; price band ±3% of reference price; implemented from 3 Aug 2026; revised pre-open auction from 7 Sep 2026
  • Earlier closing price = VWAP of last 30 minutes of trading
  • SWAGAT-FI (Single Window Automatic and Generalized Access for Trusted Foreign Investors): unified registration for FPIs and FVCIs from 1 Jun 2026
  • Proposal: FPIs to settle net funds instead of gross on the same day

34/67☆ Good to knowSEBI: demat, KYC and market statistics

  • One-year special window to transfer and dematerialise physical shares bought or sold before 1 Apr 2019: 5 Feb 2026 – 4 Feb 2027; one-year lock-in
  • From 2 Apr 2026 letter of confirmation scrapped: transfer time 150 → 30 days
  • Proposals: KYC supplementary data centralised at KRA level; "significant index" = linked to MF schemes above ₹20,000 crore AUM
  • Working group set up on mutual fund distributors vs registered investment advisers (RIAs earn fees, not commissions)
  • SEBI chair Tuhin Kanta Pandey: India leads the world in IPO count, third by value; market cap/GDP 69% (FY16) → above 130%; investors 43 mn (FY20) → 137 mn
  • SEBI Investor Survey 2025: 63% households aware of a securities product, only 9.5% invested; urban 74% vs rural 56%

35/67☆ Good to knowSEBI proposals (Feb 2026)

  • SWP and STP standing instructions for demat-mode MF units; AIF winding-up flexibility (retain funds for litigation; 75% investor consent for anticipated liabilities; 3-year cap for expenses)
  • Commodity clearing corporations: stress-test Z-score 10 → 5; Cover 2 vs Cover 1 standards
  • SEBI barred NCDEX and MSE (Metropolitan Stock Exchange) from equity derivatives until share trading builds up

36/67☆ Good to knowSEBI: ETF price bands and social media disclosure

  • Proposal: ETF price band base shifts to T-1 data; equity/debt index ETFs 10% (flex to 20%); gold/silver ETFs 6% initial; overnight ETFs 5%. Today: 20% (5% overnight) on T-2 NAV
  • From 1 May, SEBI-regulated entities must show registered name and number on social media content

37/67★ Must-learnSEBI Mar 2026: board reforms, MF safeguards, claims

In plain words

SEBI changes in March: better protection for mutual fund investors (voluntary lock on the folio), easier claims by nominees and tighter conflict-of-interest rules.

  • Board: social impact funds minimum ₹2 lakh → ₹1,000; Pratyush Sinha committee on conflict of interest — Chairman and whole-time members classed as insiders; abridged prospectus for IPOs
  • Debit freeze (voluntary lock) for mutual fund folios (demat and non-demat); intra-day borrowing for MFs (outside 20% cap) from 1 Apr
  • Proposals: nominee cap 4 (earlier proposal 10); nominee details name and relationship only; transmission STP limits ₹10,000 (physical) / ₹30,000 (demat); simplified documents ₹10 lakh / ₹30 lakh; process within 21 days
  • Custodians (non-bank) may run non-SEBI financial services through separate SBU; net worth requirement ₹75 crore; from 24 Mar 2026
  • SEBI removed 1.2 lakh finfluencer posts; AI tool Sudarshan; SEBI SETU platform for investment advisers

38/67☆ Good to knowSEBI: life cycle funds, gift cards, IT Resilience Index

  • Life cycle funds: open-ended, fixed maturities 5/10/15/20/25/30 years; exit load 3% (1 yr), 2% (2 yrs), 1% (3 yrs); AMCs keeping children's fund cannot launch 20-year, retirement fund cannot launch 30-year
  • Proposed MF gift instruments: max ₹10,000 each, valid 1 year; ₹50,000 per MF per FY via prepaid/wallet/cash
  • IT Resilience Index (proposal): 9 parameters; system availability and security 20% each, others 10%; half-yearly; Verified App Label for registered intermediaries (Chair Tuhin Kanta Pandey)

Other regulators and bodies

39/67☆ Good to knowNFRA (National Financial Reporting Authority)

  • Constituted 1 Oct 2018 under Section 132, Companies Act 2013
  • Duties: recommend accounting and auditing standards, monitor and enforce compliance, oversee quality of the profession
  • Jan 2026: IndiaAI + NFRA launched AI-based Financial Reporting Compliance Challenge (up to 10 teams get ₹5 lakh each; winner gets contract up to ₹1 crore)

40/67★ Must-learnPFRDA: NPS Swasthya Pension Scheme

In plain words

A new NPS scheme where retirement savings can also be used partly for medical costs.

  • Voluntary contributory pension scheme for medical expenses; pilot (Proof of Concept) in PFRDA's Regulatory Sandbox
  • Partial withdrawals for OPD and IPD up to 25% of own contribution, unlimited number; first withdrawal after corpus of ₹50,000
  • Critical illness: if one inpatient bill exceeds 70% of corpus, premature exit with 100% lump sum for treatment
  • Subscribers above 40 (non-government) can move up to 30% from the common scheme account

41/67★ Must-learnTRAI: spam call penalty and 1600 series

In plain words

TRAI fined telecom companies for not stopping spam calls and made banks and government offices use a separate 1600-series number, so customers can tell genuine service calls from spam.

  • ₹150 crore penalty on telcos for failing to curb spam (three years from 2020); disincentive up to ₹50 lakh per month per licensed service area
  • Complaint window 3 → 7 days; action against sender after 5 complaints in 10 days
  • BFSI entities and government must use 1600-series numbers for transactional and service calls

42/67★ Must-learnMoEFCC: second round of GHG Emission Intensity targets

In plain words

Industries must now cut the greenhouse gases they release per unit of product. The government has set legally binding targets for 208 more industries under its carbon credit scheme.

  • Greenhouse Gases Emission Intensity (GEI) Target (Amendment) Rules under the Carbon Credits Trading Scheme (CCTS) (launched 2023)
  • Legally binding targets for 208 industries in petroleum refineries, petrochemicals, textiles and secondary aluminium
  • GEI = GHG emitted per unit of product (e.g. per tonne of cement)
  • India commitment: cut GDP emissions intensity 45% by 2030 vs 2005
  • Notified 13 Jan 2026: 208 more obligated entities (petroleum refineries, petrochemicals, textiles, secondary aluminium) → ICM covers 490 entities; first notification Oct 2025 covered 282 (aluminium, cement, chlor-alkali, pulp and paper); CCTS notified 2023 with compliance and offset mechanisms
  • Mar 2026: Indian Carbon Market Portal launched at Prakriti 2026 (BEE conference); CCTS has 9 notified methodologies; GEI targets cover ≈490 entities in seven sectors

43/67☆ Good to knowPFRDA NPS distributor charges; IRDAI approvals

  • PFRDA: PoP onboarding charge ₹200 per new subscriber (₹100 if fully digital); annual 0.20% of AUM; NPS minimum ₹250 first contribution, ₹10 later
  • IRDAI cleared Allianz Jio Reinsurance (50:50 Allianz–Jio Financial) and Kiwi General Insurance; Sabka Bima Sabki Raksha Act notified 21 Dec 2025, in force 5 Feb 2026; only domestic reinsurers GIC Re and one other; 11 foreign reinsurer branches

44/67★ Must-learnPFRDA: banks may set up pension funds; NPS fee reset (Jan 2026)

In plain words

PFRDA, the pension regulator, will let banks set up their own pension funds for NPS and has changed what fund managers can charge.

  • Who: PFRDA (Pension Fund Regulatory and Development Authority) → in-principle framework letting scheduled commercial banks independently set up pension funds for NPS
  • What changed: slab-based Investment Management Fee (IMF) from 1 Apr 2026, different rates for Government and Non-Government subscribers; also for Multiple Scheme Framework (MSF), MSF corpus counted separately
  • Annual Regulatory Fee unchanged at 0.015% of AUM (0.0025% passed to Association of NPS Intermediaries)
  • NPS Trust: three new trustees; Dinesh Kumar Khara (ex-SBI chairman) is Chairperson
  • Affects: pension funds, NPS subscribers, banks
Exam angle

Who chairs the NPS Trust Board?

Remember

Khara (ex-SBI).

45/67★ Must-learnNPS Vatsalya Scheme Guidelines 2025 (PFRDA)

In plain words

NPS Vatsalya is a pension account that parents open for a child under 18. The money grows until the child grows up and can then continue it as a normal NPS account.

  • Announced Budget 2024-25; launched 18 Sep 2024 by FM; for minors (under 18) incl. NRI/OCI; account by guardian
  • Minimum contribution ₹250 (initial and annual); no maximum
  • Partial withdrawal after 3 years: up to 25% of own contribution (education, medical, disability)
  • At 18: fresh KYC; choose by 21: continue, shift to NPS Tier I, or exit (80% lump sum, 20% annuity; full withdrawal if corpus ≤ ₹8 lakh)
  • Incentives for Anganwadi workers, ASHAs, Bank Sakhis
Exam angle

What is the minimum contribution in NPS Vatsalya?

46/67☆ Good to knowEPF Employees' Enrolment Scheme 2025

  • Special window 1 Nov 2025 – 30 Apr 2026 for employers to enrol left-out employees (joined 1 Jul 2017 – 31 Oct 2025); penal damages only ₹100 per establishment; online with Face Authentication Technology

47/67★ Must-learnBaggage Rules, 2026 and DFS digital platforms

In plain words

New customs baggage rules say how much a returning traveller can bring in duty-free (for example when shifting residence or carrying jewellery).

  • Who: Central Government / CBIC → Baggage Rules 2026 and Customs Baggage (Declaration and Processing) Regulations 2026, plus Master Circular; affects international passengers
  • Transfer of residence duty-free: up to 12 months ₹1.5 lakh; 1–2 years ₹3 lakh; above 2 years ₹7.5 lakh; no allowance for land-border arrivals
  • Jewellery (stay abroad > 1 year): by weight only – female 40 g, others 20 g; one laptop (18+) and pets incorporated
  • DFS launched BAANKNET (Bank Assets Auction Network) mobile app and Digital Balance Confirmation Platform (DBCP) for audits
Exam angle

What duty-free gold limit for a returning woman after 1+ year abroad?

48/67★ Must-learnStartup recognition framework revised (DPIIT, Feb 2026)

In plain words

The government made it easier to be recognised as a startup (higher turnover limit) and gave deep-tech startups a longer life of 20 years.

  • Turnover limit for recognition ₹100 crore → ₹200 crore
  • New "Deep Tech Startup" category: age limit 10 → 20 years; turnover limit ₹300 crore
  • Cooperative societies (multi-state and state) now eligible

49/67★ Must-learnRBI/Government: cooperative banks measures; NCDC loans priority sector

In plain words

Several changes to help cooperative banks lend more, including counting loans to NCDC as priority sector lending.

  • Who: RBI with Government of India → bank loans to NCDC (from 19 Jan 2026) for on-lending to cooperatives count as priority sector lending; banks other than RRBs, UCBs, SFBs and LABs
  • Other measures: UCB housing loan limit 10% → 25% of total loans; directors' term in cooperative banks 8 → 10 years (Banking Regulation Act); rural cooperative banks in Integrated Ombudsman Scheme; Sahakar Sarthi shared services; NUCFDC (non-deposit NBFC umbrella organisation for UCBs)
  • Affects: UCBs, rural cooperative banks, NCDC
Exam angle

Which banks may count NCDC loans as PSL?
