  • Controller General of Accounts (Ms T.C.A. Kalyani) launched Government Bank Manual (standard procedures) and Government Bank Dashboard (real-time tracking of remittance, scroll compliance, reconciliation)

51/67☆ Good to knowIVFRT scheme continued to 2031

  • Immigration, Visa, Foreigners Registration & Tracking (IVFRT): Cabinet approved 1 Apr 2026–31 Mar 2031, outlay ₹1,800 crore (MHA)
  • Earlier: approved 2010 (₹1,011 cr) → 2021-26 phase ₹1,365 cr; linked to Immigration and Foreigners Act, 2025
  • Covers 117 immigration posts, 15 FRROs (Foreigners Regional Registration Officers); 91.24% e-Visas cleared in 72 hrs; FTI-TTP (Fast Track Immigration–Trusted Traveller Programme) at 13 airports → clearance 30 seconds

52/67★ Must-learnLand-border country (LBC) investment guidelines eased (Cabinet)

In plain words

Investment from countries that share a land border with India (such as China) needs government approval. The Cabinet eased this: small non-controlling stakes can come through the automatic route, and some manufacturing proposals get decided faster.

  • Who: Cabinet (DPIIT) → investors from countries sharing land border with India (Press Note 3 regime); affects FDI approval route
  • What changed: Beneficial Owner (BO) definition added (PML Rules 2005); BO test at investor-entity level
  • Non-controlling LBC beneficial ownership up to 10% → automatic route (reporting to DPIIT)
  • Proposals in capital goods, electronic capital goods, electronic components, polysilicon, ingot-wafer → decided within 60 days; Indian citizens/entities keep majority and control
Exam angle

What BO limit is allowed under the automatic route for LBC investments?

53/67★ Must-learnCBDT: record Advance Pricing Agreements (APAs)

In plain words

An Advance Pricing Agreement settles in advance how a company's transactions with its foreign group arm will be priced for tax. The tax board signed a record number this year.

  • Central Board of Direct Taxes signed record 219 APAs in FY2025-26 (174 in FY25; 125 before); total 1,034 (750 unilateral + 284 bilateral)
  • 84 BAPAs in FY26 (65 in FY25) with 13 treaty partners; first-ever BAPAs with France, Ireland, Indonesia, Sweden
  • Finance Act 2026 Safe Harbour: IT services consolidated, 15.5% margin; threshold ₹300 crore → ₹2,000 crore; APA covers up to 5 years
Remember

219 APAs · total 1,034 · margin 15.5%

54/67☆ Good to knowCBIC courier and e-commerce export reforms (1 Apr 2026)

  • ₹10 lakh cap on commercial courier exports removed; Return to Origin (RTO) for goods unclaimed/uncleared over 15 days; risk-based re-import of returned goods; courier regulations 1998 and 2010 amended; uncleared goods after 30 days disposed of

55/67★ Must-learnRBI: loans against eligible securities (from 1 Apr 2026)

In plain words

RBI has fixed how much a bank can lend against shares, mutual funds and bonds kept as security, and the loan caps for each type of borrower.

  • Credit Facilities Amendment Directions 2026 (commercial banks and SFBs); fresh/renewed loans comply, old loans run to maturity; all such loans = Capital Market Exposure (CME)
  • Max LTV (loan-to-value) for individuals/HUFs: listed shares 60%; equity MFs/ETFs/REITs/InvITs 75%; debt MFs 85%; AAA listed debt 85%; AA–BBB 75%; SGBs as gold loans; breach to be cured in 7 working days
  • Caps: ₹1 crore per individual; secondary-market purchase ₹25 lakh; IPO/FPO/ESOP ₹25 lakh (max 75% funding, 25% margin, lien on shares)
  • Banned: loans against own securities (except ₹10 lakh vs own long-term infra bonds), partly paid shares, locked-in shares, IDRs, buy-back; CP/NCD of maturity ≤1 year
  • Capital market intermediaries: 100% collateral, 40% haircut on equity, margin trading at least 50% cash, intraday limits at least 50% collateral; no funding of proprietary trading
Exam angle

What is the maximum LTV for loans against listed shares?

Remember

60 shares · 75 equity MF · 85 debt MF · ₹1 cr cap · 7 days

56/67★ Must-learnRBI: Capital Market Exposure (CME) ceilings and IPC capital treatment

In plain words

RBI limits how much total risk a bank can take in the stock market. The card gives the limits (40% overall) and how the capital is counted.

  • Concentration Risk Management Amendment Directions 2026 (commercial banks and SFBs); limits on solo and consolidated basis
  • Aggregate CME ≤ 40% of eligible capital base (Tier 1 + Tier 2); direct investment CME ≤ 20%; acquisition finance ≤ 20%; separate intra-day limits
  • Direct CME valued at cost; credit exposure = higher of sanctioned limit or outstanding; IPC (Irrevocable Payment Commitment by custodian banks to clearing corporations) counted at 30% (T+1) or 50% (T+2) of net settlement obligation
  • Capital Adequacy Second Amendment: IPC = financial guarantee, CCF 100%, capital only on CME portion at 125% risk weight (₹100 crore → RWA ₹125 crore)
  • Excluded from CME: own subsidiaries/JVs, AT1/Tier 2 of banks, CDs, non-voting preference shares, refinancing part of acquisition finance, 70% of underwriting; Critical Financial Infrastructure (22 bodies: IFCI, TFCI, NHB, SIDBI, NABARD, Exim, LIC, GIC, NSDL, CDSL, NSE, CCIL, MCX, NCDEX, ICEX, NCML, NPCI, BSE etc.)
Exam angle

What is the aggregate CME ceiling for banks?

Remember

40 total · 20 direct · 20 acquisition · RW 125%

57/67★ Must-learnRBI draft: Kisan Credit Card (KCC) Directions 2026

In plain words

A draft RBI rule book for Kisan Credit Cards says how banks should work out a farmer's loan limit, how long it lasts, and when no collateral is needed.

  • Draft Directions for commercial banks (excl. SFBs, payments banks, LABs; not overseas branches); existing loans move to new rules at next review/renewal
  • Composite facility of 6 years: short-term crop + allied + post-harvest + consumption + maintenance + insurance + produce marketing, plus long-term investment credit
  • CMPL (Composite Maximum Permissible Limit) = short-term limit for year 6 + estimated long-term limit = KCC limit
  • Crop drawing limit = Scale of Finance (SoF) × area + 10% (post-harvest/household) + 20% (maintenance/support) + insurance; SoF notified by State Level Technical Committee
  • Crop season: short-duration 12 months, long-duration 18 months; marginal farmer ≤1 ha, small farmer >1 to 2 ha; Flexi KCC for marginal farmers ₹10,000–50,000
  • Collateral and margin waived up to ₹2 lakh (voluntary gold/silver pledge not a breach); eligible: owner cultivators, tenants, oral lessees, sharecroppers, SHGs, JLGs
Exam angle

How is the KCC crop-season drawing limit computed?

Remember

SoF +10% +20% + insurance; 6 years; ₹2 lakh

58/67☆ Good to knowRBI: NBFC DLG treatment; MSME classification

  • NBFCs (IRACP Amendment Directions 2026, effective immediately): Default Loss Guarantee (DLG) may be considered in Expected Credit Loss only if Ind AS compliant, integral to the loan contract and not a separate asset; recompute ECL after every DLG invocation
  • MSMED Act 2006 limits: micro – plant and machinery ≤ ₹2.5 cr, turnover ≤ ₹10 cr; small ≤ ₹25 cr / ₹100 cr; medium ≤ ₹125 cr / ₹500 cr; PM Task Force: 20% yearly credit growth to micro and small, 10% growth in micro accounts, 60% of MSE lending to micro; composite loan ₹1 crore
  • Rural co-operative banks: income on Standard assets on accrual; on NPAs on cash basis, accrued income reversed

59/67★ Must-learnSEBI (Feb 2026): derivatives and market-structure rules

In plain words

SEBI tightened some stock-market trading rules (margins in derivatives, penalties for too many orders) and changed how mutual funds value gold and silver.

  • Single-stock derivatives: no calendar-spread margin benefit on expiry day if one leg expires (aligns with index derivatives); effective 3 months from circular
  • Order-to-Trade Ratio (OTR): options orders within ±40% of LTP or ±₹20 (higher) excluded; Designated Market Makers' orders excluded; existing exemption within ±0.75% of LTP; effective 6 Apr 2026
  • Commodity exchanges: installed capacity at least 2× projected peak load (earlier 4×); action if usage >75%; policy to SEBI within 3 months; AIF NAV to depositories by 1 May 2026 or 30 days from valuation
  • Physical gold/silver in MF schemes valued on exchange polled spot prices (earlier LBMA AM fixing) from 1 Apr 2026
Remember

4× → 2× capacity; OTR 6 Apr

60/67☆ Good to knowSEBI (Feb 2026): disclosure and intermediary rules

  • SEBI-registered name and number on social-media handle home page and start of each securities-market video, from 1 May 2026
  • Green debt securities need an independent third-party reviewer (second party opinion, verification, certification or scoring), disclosed in offer document; ESG debt = green, social, sustainability, sustainability-linked bonds
  • Credit Rating Agencies rating other regulators' instruments: separate grievance emails/web sections, net worth requirement additive, regulator named in rating reports

61/67★ Must-learnSEBI: mutual fund scheme categorisation (key limits)

In plain words

SEBI has fixed what each type of mutual fund must invest in (for example how much a Multi Cap fund must hold in large, mid and small companies).

  • Equity: Multi Cap 75% equity with at least 25% each in large, mid, small cap; Large Cap 80%; Large and Mid Cap 35% + 35%; Mid Cap 65%; Small Cap 65%; Flexi Cap 65%; Focused max 30 stocks; sectoral/thematic/value/contra/dividend yield/ELSS 80%
  • Debt by Macaulay duration: Overnight 1 day; Liquid up to 91 days; Ultra Short 3–6 months; Low-to-short 6–12 months; Short 1–3 yrs; Medium 3–4 yrs; Medium to Long 4–7 yrs; Long above 7 yrs; Corporate Bond 80% in AA+ and above; Credit Risk 65% in AA and below; Gilt 80%
  • Hybrid: Conservative 10–25% equity; Balanced 40–60%; Aggressive 65–80%; Arbitrage 65% equity; Equity Savings 65% gross, net equity 15–40%; Multi Asset at least 10% in each of three asset classes
  • Life Cycle Funds: 5–30 years in multiples of 5, max 6 active; equity falls as maturity nears (65–95% when 15–30 yrs → 5–20% under 1 yr); exit load 3% / 2% / 1% in years 1 / 2 / 3; index funds/FoFs: 95% in index/underlying fund
Exam angle

What are the minimum allocations for a Multi Cap fund?

Remember

Multi Cap: 25-25-25 (75%)

62/67★ Must-learnRBI: dividend norms for all bank types (2026 Directions)

In plain words

RBI has made dividend rules for every bank type: a bank can pay out only a share of its profit, and the share depends on how strong its capital is.

  • Adjusted PAT = PAT − 50% of net NPA; positive adjusted PAT needed; payout in 10 buckets by capital ratio: 0% (lowest) → 100% (highest), 20% in second bucket
  • Cap on payout: commercial banks 75% (CET1 buckets, 8+z% to 20+z%; z = D-SIB buffer); payments banks 75% (Tier 1 7.5% to 19.5%); small finance banks 75%; local area banks 80% (CRAR 9% to 21%); regional rural banks 80% (Tier 1 7% to 19%)
  • Commercial-bank rules cover foreign bank branches too; SFBs, LABs, payments banks, RRBs have separate Directions
Exam angle

What is the maximum dividend payout for RRBs and LABs?

Remember

75% commercial/payments/SFB; 80% LAB/RRB

63/67★ Must-learnRBI: Unique Identifiers in Financial Markets (LEI and UTI)

In plain words

Every party in big financial deals needs a 20-character identity code (LEI), and from 2027 every derivative trade needs its own unique number (UTI), so regulators can track risk.

  • Master Direction 2026: Section A LEI (Legal Entity Identifier, 20 characters) immediate; Section B UTI (Unique Transaction Identifier) from 1 Jan 2027
  • LEI from a GLEIF-accredited Local Operating Unit; India: LEIL (Legal Entity Identifier India Ltd, CCIL subsidiary); mandatory for non-individuals in OTC G-Sec, money market, forex and derivatives; non-derivative forex only for ≥ $1 million
  • UTI: max 52 characters (LEI of generator + unique code), same for whole life of derivative; generated by CCP → clearing member/ETP → agreed entity → CCIL-TR; interim UTI to be corrected within 5 Mumbai business days; CPMI-IOSCO guidance 2017; RBI Act Sections 45W and 45U
Remember

LEI 20 · UTI 52 · 1 Jan 2027

64/67★ Must-learnRBI: Digital Payments e-mandate Framework 2026

In plain words

When a customer sets up an auto-debit (e-mandate), RBI says when it can go through without extra verification and that the customer must be warned a day before.

  • Applies to recurring transactions (domestic or cross-border) on cards, PPI, UPI; one-time registration with validity period; first transaction needs Additional Factor of Authentication (AFA)
  • Without AFA up to ₹15,000 per transaction; up to ₹1 lakh for insurance premiums, mutual fund subscriptions, credit card bills
  • Issuer must send pre-debit notification at least 24 hours before charge
Exam angle

Up to what amount can insurance premium e-mandates skip AFA?

Remember

15,000 general · 1 lakh insurance/MF/card bill · 24 hr

65/67☆ Good to knowRBI: trade relief, currency declaration, ECB returns

  • Trade Relief Measures Directions 2026: export credit period up to 450 days (pre- and post-shipment) for credit disbursed till 30 Jun 2026; banks, co-operative banks, NBFC-Factors, AIFIs
  • Currency declaration form not needed if foreign exchange brought in ≤ US$10,000 (notes ≤ US$5,000)
  • ECB: untraceable borrower = no returns for 4 consecutive quarters + bank unable to reach; Form ECB-1 gives LRN (Loan Registration Number); Revised ECB-1 and ECB-2 within 7 days of month-end; individuals not eligible borrowers

66/67☆ Good to knowSEBI (Mar 2026): MF borrowing, SGF, AIF reporting and others

  • SEBI (Mutual Funds) Regulations 2026 (from 1 Apr 2026), Reg 42: MFs may borrow up to 20% of net assets for up to 6 months; intraday borrowing exempt, from 15 Jul 2026, only against guaranteed receivables (G-Secs, TREPS, reverse repo), cost and loss borne by AMC; index funds/ETFs may borrow for Closing Auction (3 Aug 2026)
  • Commodity Settlement Guarantee Fund cover: default of top 3 clearing members at 100% (earlier top 2 at 100% + all members at 50%)
  • AIFs: one Annual Activity Report within 30 days of 31 Mar (first by 31 May 2026) + limited quarterly report within 15 days; brokers report bank account changes within 7 working days (17 Apr 2026); ICMAI members can audit investment advisers/research analysts (audit within 6 months of FY end, report by 31 Oct); custodians: capacity 1.2× average load

67/67★ Must-learnRBI Utkarsh 2029 strategy framework (2026-29)

In plain words

Utkarsh 2029 is RBI's three-year plan (2026-29) for what it wants to improve: regulation, customer service, markets, technology, its own staff and its global role.

  • Released 30 Mar 2026 (Governor Sanjay Malhotra); covers Apr 2026–Mar 2029 aligned with RBI financial year; 6 strategy pillars, 49 deliverables; vision: "Continue excellence while enabling a stable, inclusive and technologically robust financial system"
  • Pillars: Robust Regulations; Customer Centricity and Inclusive Finance; Competitive Markets; Effective Technology; Future-ready Organisation; Global India
  • History: Utkarsh 2022 (Jul 2019: 24 strategies, 153 goals, 352 milestones) → Utkarsh 2.0 (2023-25, 170 milestones) → Utkarsh 2029; long-term: Project Sa-Mudra, cross-border CBDC, ULI scale-up, UPI and INR internationalisation, AI/quantum framework

6/21★ Must-learnEU CBAM comes into force (1 Jan 2026)

In plain words

The EU's carbon border tax now makes importers pay for the carbon emitted in making goods like steel, cement and aluminium, which raises costs for Indian exporters.

  • Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism: carbon levy on imports of carbon-intensive goods (cement, aluminium, fertilisers, iron and steel, hydrogen, electricity)
  • Aim: stop carbon leakage; raises costs for Indian exporters; transitional phase from 2023

12/21★ Must-learnUN High Seas (BBNJ) treaty enters into force: 17 Jan 2026

In plain words

A UN treaty to protect the oceans beyond any country's waters (the high seas) came into force on 17 January 2026; India has signed it but not yet ratified.

  • Biodiversity Beyond National Jurisdiction (BBNJ) agreement: finalised March 2023 after 15 years of talks; 60th ratification on 19 Sep 2025, force 120 days later
  • Aim: "30 by 30" — protect 30% of oceans by 2030 (about 8% protected now); marine protected areas on the high seas
  • Four areas: marine genetic resources and benefit sharing, area-based tools, environmental impact assessments, capacity building
  • India has signed but not ratified

21/21★ Must-learnIndia–US interim trade framework (joint statement, Feb 2026)

India and the US agreed the outline of a trade deal. The US will charge 18% tariff on Indian goods and India will cut tariffs and buy more American energy, aircraft and technology.

  • Framework for an Interim Agreement under the broader Bilateral Trade Agreement (BTA) talks launched 13 Feb 2025 (Trump–Modi)
  • US reciprocal tariff on Indian goods: 18% (Executive Order 14257, Apr 2025); to be removed on generic pharma, gems and diamonds, aircraft parts once the agreement is concluded
  • India to eliminate or cut tariffs on all US industrial goods and many farm products; Indian agri products get zero-tariff US access

January–March 2026 · Section 4National and State News61 items · 30 ★ must-learn · 31 ☆ good to know

Governance, law and institutions

1/61★ Must-learnNational Sports Governance Act 2025: partial commencement (1 Jan 2026)

In plain words

A new law set up a National Sports Board and a Tribunal to run sports federations more fairly, with rules on women and athletes on their committees.

  • Sets up National Sports Board (NSB) and National Sports Tribunal (NST); covers NOC, NPC, national and regional sports federations
  • Executive committees: at most 15 members, at least 2 Sportspersons of Merit and 4 women; 50% women SOMs in General Body
  • NST members: 4-year term, age cap 67; sports bodies are public authorities under RTI; Board accounts audited by CAG
  • SOM: at least 25 years old, retired from competitive sport

2/61☆ Good to knowPRAGATI and Indian Pharmacopoeia 2026

  • PRAGATI (Pro-Active Governance and Timely Implementation), launched 2015: PM-level real-time review of projects and grievances
  • Indian Pharmacopoeia 2026: 10th edition, 121 new monographs → 3,340 total; published by Indian Pharmacopoeia Commission; India now 8th in WHO pharmacovigilance contributions (was 123rd, 2009–14)

3/61☆ Good to knowRah-Veer (Good Samaritan) and NQAS

  • Rah-Veer: helps accident victim within the Golden Hour (first hour); reward ₹25,000 and certificate, up to 5 times a year; Good Samaritan Rules 2020 under Section 134A of Motor Vehicles (Amendment) Act 2019
  • NQAS (National Quality Assurance Standards): 50,373 public health facilities certified (31 Dec 2025); began 2015 with 10; target 50% of facilities by Mar 2026

4/61★ Must-learnECINET and IICDEM 2026 (Election Commission)

In plain words

The Election Commission launched ECINET, one app for all election services in 22 languages, at a global conference on democracy in New Delhi.

  • ECINET: single digital platform for election services, 22 languages + English; launched at India International Conference on Democracy and Election Management (IICDEM), 21–23 Jan 2026, Bharat Mandapam
  • Delhi Declaration 2026, five pillars: purity of electoral rolls, conduct of elections, research and publications, technology use, training and capacity building
  • CEC Gyanesh Kumar is 2026 chair of International IDEA (Sweden-based; India took over from Switzerland)

5/61☆ Good to knowAI governance white paper; PAIMANA; BharatNet

  • Principal Scientific Adviser's white paper: "techno-legal" AI governance; AI Governance Group (AIGG) chaired by PSA; Technology and Policy Expert Committee (TPEC) in MeitY
  • PAIMANA portal: monitors central sector infrastructure projects of ₹150 crore and above; replaces OCMS-2006
  • BharatNet: connects over 2.5 lakh Gram Panchayats; funded through Digital Bharat Nidhi (replaced USOF)

6/61★ Must-learnSupreme Court: menstrual hygiene is part of Article 21

In plain words

The Supreme Court said menstrual hygiene is part of the right to life (Article 21), so every school must give girls free sanitary napkins.

  • Bench of Justices JB Pardiwala and R Mahadevan
  • States and UTs must ensure every school provides free biodegradable sanitary napkins to adolescent girls

7/61☆ Good to knowNCW, UMEED portal, NLEP

  • National Commission for Women: statutory body set up January 1992; 34th Foundation Day; chair Vijaya Rahatkar
  • UMEED (Unified Waqf Management, Empowerment, Efficiency and Development) portal inaugurated 6 Jun 2025; survey and lease modules added 30 Jan 2026
  • National Leprosy Eradication Programme target: "Zero Transmission, Zero Leprosy" by 2027

8/61★ Must-learnUnion Budget 2026: Sitharaman's 9th consecutive

In plain words

Nirmala Sitharaman presented her ninth Budget in a row, the longest continuous run by any Finance Minister.

  • FM Nirmala Sitharaman presents her ninth consecutive Budget on 1 Feb 2026 — longest continuous run
  • Morarji Desai presented 10 Budgets (not consecutive); P. Chidambaram 9 (non-consecutive); earlier longest continuous run: C. D. Deshmukh (about 6 years 2 months from 1 Jun 1950)
  • First Finance Minister of independent India: R. K. Shanmukham Chetty

9/61★ Must-learnCensus 2027: freeze on administrative boundaries

In plain words

The Census will be done in 2027, so the Centre told States not to change district or tehsil boundaries till March 2027. It will be the first digital census.

  • Home Secretary Govind Mohan told Chief Secretaries: no jurisdictional changes in States till 31 Mar 2027 ahead of the Population Census 2027
  • Two phases: Phase I from 1 Apr 2026 (self-enumeration 1–15 Apr; house listing 16 Apr–15 May in some States/UTs); Phase II Sep 2026 (population enumeration) → first digital census + self-enumeration option
  • Reference date 1 Mar 2027 (1 Oct 2026 for Ladakh and snow-bound areas of J&K, Uttarakhand, Himachal); outlay ₹11,718.24 crore; app/portal in 16 languages
  • 16th census in series, 8th since independence; last census 2011; Registrar General: Mritunjay Kumar Narayan; mascots Pragati (female) and Vikas (male)

10/61★ Must-learn16th Finance Commission report (2026-31)

In plain words

The 16th Finance Commission decides how the Centre shares tax money with States for 2026-31. It kept the States' share at 41%.

  • Report on tax devolution laid in Lok Sabha; Centre retains 41% share of tax devolution to States (same since 2021)
  • Constituted 31 Dec 2023 under Arvind Panagariya; Finance Commission = constitutional body under Article 280
Exam angle

What share of divisible tax pool goes to States under the 16th Finance Commission?

Remember

41% unchanged: 15th FC (2021-26) also 41%.

11/61★ Must-learnInsolvency and Bankruptcy Code (Amendment) Bill, 2025 (Lok Sabha passed)

In plain words

A Bill to speed up bankruptcy cases lets lenders start the insolvency process themselves, with fixed time limits for each step.

  • 12 amendments (11 from Select Committee); new Chapter IV-A: Creditor-Initiated Insolvency Resolution Process (CIIRP) — financial creditors with ≥51% approval; 150 days + 45 days
  • Admission within 14 days; resolution plan approval 30 days; CIRP revival before liquidation once within 120 days; CoC can replace liquidator (66% vote)

12/61☆ Good to knowFCRA amendment bill (Cabinet, Mar 2026)

  • New Section 14B: "deemed cessation" of registration; maximum jail for unauthorised receipt cut to 1 year; Centre approval needed before criminal investigation; Centre can prescribe timelines for receipt and use of funds
  • Census 2027 to recognise all 21 disability categories (MoS Ramdas Athawale)

13/61★ Must-learnIndia–Japan swap renewed; new Seed and Pesticide Acts

In plain words

The Bank of Japan and RBI renewed a $75 billion currency swap (a back-up line of foreign currency), and new Seed and Pesticide Acts were passed.

  • Bilateral Swap Arrangement with Japan renewed from 28 Feb 2026; size unchanged $75 billion (swap local currency for USD)
  • Seed Act 2026: mandatory registration of seed companies, QR codes on packets, fines up to ₹30 lakh; Pesticide Act 2026 to replace Insecticides Act 1968

14/61☆ Good to knowLaw and institutions (Mar 2026)

  • National Dental Commission (NDC Act 2023, in force 19 Mar 2026) replaces Dental Council of India; repeals Dentists Act 1948
  • Supreme Court: adoptive mothers entitled to 12 weeks maternity leave regardless of child's age
  • TV Rating Policy 2026: rating agency net worth ₹20 crore → ₹5 crore; 50% independent directors; 80,000 metered homes (later 1.2 lakh)

In plain words

India produces more rice than any other country, ahead of China.

Infrastructure, transport and energy

16/61★ Must-learnMumbai–Ahmedabad bullet train (MAHSR)

In plain words

India's first bullet train between Mumbai and Ahmedabad is being built with Japanese loans and is meant to start running in 2027.

  • First bullet train targeted 15 Aug 2027; full corridor by Dec 2029; first section Surat–Bilimora
  • 508 km, 12 stations, via Gujarat, Maharashtra and Dadra & Nagar Haveli; travel time 2 hr 7 min
  • Cost ~₹1.08 lakh crore; JICA funds 81% (₹88,000 crore); built by NHSRCL
  • First Vande Bharat sleeper: Howrah–Guwahati, 16 coaches, 823 passengers
  • Hydrogen train: Haryana to launch it on the Jind–Sonipat route (green hydrogen pilot)
  • First hydrogen train in trial (built at ICF, tested by RDSO): 10 coaches, 2,400 kW, world's longest and most powerful on broad gauge

17/61☆ Good to knowKaziranga Elevated Corridor and bio-bitumen

  • Kaziranga Elevated Corridor (NH-715, Kaliabor–Numaligarh): 85.675 km, ₹6,950 crore, EPC mode, CCEA approval 1 Oct 2025
  • CSIR: India first to commercially produce bio-bitumen for roads

18/61★ Must-learnCoking coal notified as Critical and Strategic Mineral

In plain words

Coking coal, a key raw material for steel that India mostly imports, was added to the list of critical minerals, which gives it priority for mining and supply.

  • Notified under Section 11C, MMDR Act 1957: "Coal" now reads "Coal, including Coking Coal"; coking coal added to Part D (critical and strategic minerals)
  • Resources 37.37 billion tonnes (mostly Jharkhand); imports 51.20 mt (FY21) → 57.58 mt (FY25); about 95% of steel sector need imported

19/61☆ Good to knowKavach 4.0 and NSHIP Tamil Nadu

  • Kavach 4.0 (indigenous Automatic Train Protection, RDSO-approved): 472 route km commissioned in one day (Vadodara–Virar 344 km, Tughlakabad–Palwal 35 km, Manpur–Sarmatanr 93.3 km); total over 1,300 route km
  • NSHIP, TN: first shipbuilding SPV, 50:50 VOC Port and SIPCOT; Shipbuilding Development Scheme outlay ₹19,989 crore, aim 4.5 million gross tonnage

22/61★ Must-learnRules from 1 April 2026: FASTag pass, FSSAI, waste

In plain words

Several rules start from 1 April 2026: a new FASTag pass fee, easier FSSAI licences and new waste-management rules.

  • FASTag annual pass fee ₹3,000 → ₹3,075 (FY27); free passage for non-commercial vehicles for 1 year or 200 trips; ~1,150 plazas
  • FSSAI licences get perpetual validity; basic registration turnover ₹12 lakh → ₹1.5 crore; State licence up to ₹50 crore; registered street vendors deemed FSSAI-registered
  • Solid Waste Management Rules 2026 replace 2016 rules: four-way segregation (wet, dry, sanitary, special care); RDF substitution 5% → 15% over six years; polluter pays

Environment, wildlife and heritage

23/61☆ Good to knowPiprahwa relics returned

  • Exhibition "The Light & The Lotus" opened by PM: 349 gemstones from Piprahwa stupa (Siddharthnagar, UP), dug up in 1898 by William Peppé; returned to India in 2025
  • Pali accorded classical language status in 2024

24/61★ Must-learnProject Dolphin: second range-wide dolphin survey

In plain words

A second survey is counting the Ganges river dolphin, India's national aquatic animal, to see how its numbers are changing.

  • Project Dolphin announced 2020; survey run by Wildlife Institute of India (Dehradun) with State forest departments, WWF-India, Aaranyak, Wildlife Trust of India
  • Phase 1: Ganga main stem Bijnor → Ganga Sagar, and Indus; Phase 2: Brahmaputra, Ganga tributaries, Sundarbans, Odisha
  • Earlier survey (2021–23): about 6,327 river dolphins; UP and Bihar highest
  • Ganges River Dolphin ("Susu"): National Aquatic Animal 2009; IUCN Endangered; CITES Appendix I; Wildlife Protection Act 1972 Schedule I

25/61☆ Good to knowIndian Skimmer conservation project

  • BNHS project under NMCG to protect sandbar nesting habitats in the Ganga basin; "Nest Guardians" and "River Guardians"
  • Indian Skimmer (Rynchops albicollis): IUCN Endangered; India holds about 90% of world population

26/61★ Must-learnSolid Waste Management Rules, 2026

In plain words

New waste rules replace the 2016 ones. Households must sort waste into four types and big waste producers have extra duties.

  • Notified under Environment (Protection) Act 1986; supersede 2016 rules; full effect 1 Apr 2026
  • Based on circular economy and Extended Producer Responsibility; mandatory four-stream segregation: wet, dry, sanitary, special care
  • Bulk waste generator: floor area 20,000 sq m or more, or water use 40,000 litres/day, or 100 kg waste/day
  • Fuel substitution rate 5% → 15% over six years
  • Uniform Consent Guidelines amended (Air and Water Acts): Consent to Operate valid until cancelled; Red-category processing 120 → 90 days; one-time CTO fee for 5–25 years allowed
  • Dumpsite Remediation Accelerator Programme (DRAP, Nov 2025): "Lakshya Zero Dumpsites" by Oct 2026; 214 high-impact sites (≈80% of legacy waste); central assistance ₹550 per tonne

27/61★ Must-learnRamsar sites: India now has 98

In plain words

India now has 98 Ramsar sites (wetlands of international importance), up from 26 in 2014.

  • Patna Bird Sanctuary (Etah, UP) and Chhari-Dhand (Kutch, Gujarat) added; total 98 (26 in 2014)
  • Ramsar Convention signed 1971 in Ramsar, Iran; India party from 1 Feb 1982
Remember

Ramsar total: 98 (Jan 2026), up from 26 in 2014.

28/61★ Must-learnMeghalaya living root bridges: UNESCO nomination

In plain words

India has nominated the living root bridges of Meghalaya, grown by Khasi people from tree roots, for UNESCO World Heritage status.

  • Dossier submitted for World Heritage List 2026-27; on tentative list since March 2022
  • Khasi and Jaintia Hills; Khasi name Jing Kieng Jri; made from Ficus elastica roots, 10–15 years to form

29/61☆ Good to knowWildlife and environment (Mar 2026)

Other national items

31/61★ Must-learnEPFO: UPI withdrawals and simplified rules

In plain words

EPF members can now withdraw money directly to their bank account through UPI, and the withdrawal rules have been reduced to five simple categories.

  • EPF withdrawals directly via UPI to bank accounts from 1 Apr 2026; auto-settlement limit ₹1 lakh → ₹5 lakh; settlement within 3 days
  • Withdrawal rules condensed to 5 categories; one-year minimum service; up to 75% of eligible balance; 25% stays as a floor
  • Job loss: 75% at once, rest after one year of unemployment; full withdrawal on retirement at 55, permanent disability, retrenchment, voluntary retirement or settling abroad
  • Monthly pension only after 10 years of pensionable service

32/61☆ Good to knowSchemes and facilities: ECMS, RDI Fund, solar calibration

  • Electronics Components Manufacturing Scheme (MeitY): 22 more proposals (₹41,863 crore investment); total 46 approved, ₹54,567 crore, 11 states
  • ₹1 lakh crore Research, Development and Innovation (RDI) Fund; DSIR removed 3-year viability test for deep-tech startups (aid up to ₹1 crore)
  • CSIR-NPL: national primary standard facility for solar cell calibration (India 5th country after USA, Germany, China, Japan) and National Environmental Standard Laboratory (Asia's first)

33/61☆ Good to knowSocial and youth schemes (Jan 2026 anniversaries)

35/61★ Must-learnHPV vaccination drive launched from Ajmer (Rajasthan)

In plain words

India began a free HPV vaccination drive for 14-year-old girls (launched from Ajmer) to prevent cervical cancer.

  • PM Modi launched national HPV programme for girls aged 14 from Ajmer; free at government facilities ("Swastha Nari")
  • Vaccine: Gardasil (quadrivalent: HPV 16, 18, 6, 11); types 16 and 18 cause over 80% of cervical cancer in India
  • Cervical cancer: second most common cancer in Indian women; 1.2 lakh cases, ~80,000 deaths a year (GLOBOCAN 2022); India joins 160+ countries

40/61★ Must-learnKumbhalgarh Wildlife Sanctuary declared Eco-Sensitive Zone

In plain words

The area just outside Kumbhalgarh sanctuary in Rajasthan is now an Eco-Sensitive Zone, which limits construction and industry there.

  • Located in the Aravalli range, Rajasthan, across Rajsamand, Pali and Udaipur districts; one of the Aravalli's most fragile ecosystems

41/61★ Must-learnRajasthan: Raj-UNNATI project monitoring

In plain words

Rajasthan became the first State to copy the Centre's PRAGATI system, calling it Raj-UNNATI, so that stuck projects and complaints are reviewed regularly.

  • Praised by Cabinet Secretary T V Somanathan; modelled on the Centre's PRAGATI
  • Rajasthan is the first State to adopt it (as Raj-UNNATI) to resolve stuck projects, schemes and grievances

42/61★ Must-learnPESA Act rules in Jharkhand

In plain words

Jharkhand finally made rules for the PESA Act, which gives tribal villages a say in managing their land and resources.

  • Panchayats (Extension to Scheduled Areas) Act enacted 24 Dec 1996 for Fifth Schedule (tribal) areas; inspired by 73rd and 74th Amendments
  • Jharkhand notified rules after 25 years; apply fully in 13 of 24 districts

New IT rules force online platforms to remove unlawful content much faster (within 3 hours of an order) and cover AI-made content.

51/61★ Must-learnKerala to be renamed Keralam

In plain words

The Cabinet agreed to rename Kerala as Keralam. Parliament can change a State's name under Article 3 after hearing the State.

  • Union Cabinet approved renaming; Kerala (Alteration of Name) Bill, 2026 to be referred to State Assembly for views
  • Article 3: Parliament may alter name of a State (proviso: President refers bill to State legislature)

52/61★ Must-learnPM RAHAT scheme (road accident victims)

In plain words

PM RAHAT gives accident victims cashless treatment up to ₹1.5 lakh for the first 7 days, on any road.

  • Launched 13 Feb 2026: cashless treatment up to ₹1.5 lakh per victim for 7 days from accident, on any category of road; Ministry of Road Transport and Highways
  • Stabilisation up to 24 h (48 h if life-threatening); links eDAR (MoRTH) with TMS 2.0 (National Health Authority) and 112 ERSS; paid from Motor Vehicle Accident Fund (insured: general insurers' contribution; uninsured/hit-and-run: Government budget); claims paid within 10 days

53/61☆ Good to knowPolicy and infrastructure items (Feb)

  • From 1 Apr 2026: cash payments to end at NH toll plazas (FASTag/UPI only; proposed); petrol with up to 20% ethanol and minimum RON 95 mandated nationwide
  • Namo Bharat: 82 km Delhi–Meerut corridor fully opened (India's first RRTS; 180 km/h); Meerut Metro fastest metro (120 km/h)
  • Micron ATMP plant, Sanand (Gujarat): ₹22,500 crore, first proposal approved under India Semiconductor Mission
  • Bonded Labour System (Abolition) Act, 1976 in force 9 Feb 1976: golden jubilee 2026
  • Fortified rice distribution under PMGKAY temporarily discontinued (shelf-life concerns)
  • PAIMANA (MoSPI portal) monitors central infrastructure projects ≥ ₹150 crore; replaced OCMS-2006
  • Budget 2026-27: Integrated East Coast Industrial Corridor (node at Durgapur); NICDIT ₹3,000 crore; 11 corridors; NICDC (earlier DMICDC) incorporated Jan 2008
  • Cabinet: POWERGRID equity investment limit ₹5,000 crore → ₹7,500 crore per subsidiary (Maharatna; cap 15% net worth); Srinagar airport civil enclave ₹1,677 crore
  • Villages of Excellence (PM visit to Israel, Feb 2026): extension of Indo-Israel Agriculture Project; target 100 Centres of Excellence

54/61☆ Good to knowState items (Feb)

  • Kerala approved India's first comprehensive graphene policy
  • Sikkim: Pakyong Airport (only airport) to be renamed Trilochan Pokhrel Airport
  • Tirumala: ₹25 crore food testing lab with e-nose/e-tongue (FSSAI-backed)
  • Sangtam Naga body (Nagaland) banned pangolin hunting; all 8 pangolin species in CITES Appendix I

55/61☆ Good to knowPANCHAM chatbot and PESA ranking 2024-25

  • PANCHAM (Panchayat Assistance and Messaging chatbot) launched 25 Jan 2026 with UNICEF; RapidPro; BHASHINI; 22 languages; links 30 lakh panchayat representatives
  • PESA ranking 2024-25 (10 Fifth Schedule states): Maharashtra 1st, Madhya Pradesh 2nd, Himachal Pradesh 3rd

  • 28 Jan – 14 Apr 2026: saturate 5 KPIs in 112 Aspirational Districts (ADP, 2018) and 6 KPIs in 513 Aspirational Blocks (ABP, 2023)

57/61★ Must-learnCBDC-based digital food coupon pilot (Gujarat)

In plain words

A pilot in Gujarat gives ration-card holders digital coupons (using the e-rupee) instead of paper, to be used at fair price shops.

  • Pilot in PDS (public distribution system): digital coupons through RBI credited as programmable e₹; redeemed at fair price shops; districts Ahmedabad, Anand, Valsad, Surat; to extend to Chandigarh, Puducherry, DNHDD; slogan "Har Dana, Har Rupiya, Har Adhikar"

58/61★ Must-learnIndia's NDC 2031-35 (Cabinet approved)

In plain words

India told the world how much more it will cut emissions and add clean power by 2035, as part of its Paris Agreement promise (NDC).

  • Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC) to UNFCCC (United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change); goal → net-zero by 2070
  • Emissions intensity of GDP: 36% cut (2005–2020) → target 47% by 2035 (2030 target of 33–35% already met)
  • Non-fossil share of installed power capacity: 52.57% (Feb 2026; 2030 target of 40% met) → target 60% by 2035
  • Carbon sink (forest + tree cover): 2.29 bn tCO2e created by 2021 → 3.5–4.0 bn tCO2e by 2035 (from 2005 level)
  • Basis: first Global Stocktake, CBDR-RC (Common but Differentiated Responsibilities and Respective Capabilities), equity; NAPCC (National Action Plan on Climate Change) with 9 missions; LiFE, Ek Ped Maa Ke Nam
Exam angle

What is India's 2035 non-fossil capacity target under the new NDC?

Remember

47% intensity · 60% non-fossil · 3.5–4.0 bn t sink

59/61★ Must-learnPLFS Annual Report 2025 (Jan–Dec 2025)

In plain words

The government's yearly job survey shows how many Indians work or look for work and what share are unemployed (3.1%).

  • Periodic Labour Force Survey (NSO, MoSPI); usual status (ps+ss), age 15+
  • LFPR 59.3% (male 79.1%, female 40.0%) | WPR 57.4% | UR 3.1% (male 3.3% → 3.1%; female 3.1%)
  • Youth (15–29) UR 10.3% → 9.9%; rural youth 8.3%; urban youth 13.6%; urban UR 4.8%; rural UR 2.4%
  • Self-employed 58.2% (2023) → 57.5% → 56.2%; regular wage 22.4% → 23.6%; casual labour 20.2%
  • Agriculture share of employment 44.8% → 43.0%; manufacturing 11.6% → 12.1%; workers 61.6 crore (41.6 cr male, 20.0 cr female); NEET (15–29) 25.0%
  • Avg earnings: regular salaried male ₹24,217, female ₹18,353; formal education avg 10.0 yrs; 67.8% at least secondary
Exam angle

What is the all-India LFPR in PLFS 2025?

Remember

LFPR 59.3 · UR 3.1 · youth UR 9.9

60/61☆ Good to knowASUSE 2025 and Time Use Survey 2024

  • ASUSE (Annual Survey of Unincorporated Sector Enterprises) 2025: establishments 7.34 cr → 7.92 crore (+7.97%); GVA +10.87%; GVA per worker ₹1,56,539; workers 12.81 crore; internet use 26.7% → 39.4%; female-owned 27.0%
  • Time Use Survey 2024 (MoSPI, released 25 Feb 2025): unpaid caregiving – men 21.4% (74 min/day), women 41% (140 min/day)

61/61★ Must-learnIndia's biodiversity reports: Nagoya NR1 and CBD NR-7

In plain words

India reported to UN biodiversity bodies how well it is protecting nature: forests, wetlands, tigers and fair sharing of benefits from biological resources.

  • First National Report on Nagoya Protocol (ABS) to CBD (Convention on Biological Diversity): period 1 Nov 2017–31 Dec 2025; 12,830 ABS approvals; 3,556 IRCCs published (>60% of global); ₹216.31 crore mobilised; framework = Biological Diversity Act 2002, Rules 2024, ABS Regulations 2025
  • 7th National Report (NR-7): aligned to NBSAP 2024-2030 and Kunming-Montreal GBF; 142 indicators vs 23 National Biodiversity Targets – all on track
  • Forest area 7,75,377 sq km (23.59%); forest cover 15.83%; forest and tree cover 25.17%; Ramsar sites 26 (2014) → 98 (2026); 58 tiger reserves; 3,682 tigers (>70% of world); 106 national parks; 574 sanctuaries; PARIVESH single window
Remember

Ramsar 98 · tigers 3,682 · F&T cover 25.17%

Science and Technology

1/19☆ Good to knowBio-Safety Levels (BSL) and first state-funded BSL-4 lab

  • Foundation stone laid in Gandhinagar for first state-funded BSL-4 facility
  • BSL-1 low risk; BSL-2 moderate; BSL-3 high-risk airborne pathogens; BSL-4 most dangerous exotic agents

  • India's first open-sea marine fish farming project launched at North Bay, Andaman, by Ministry of Earth Sciences with NIOT: finfish cage culture and seaweed (Blue Economy)
  • ₹1 lakh crore RDI Fund: DST approved TDB and BIRAC as first Second-Level Fund Managers

3/19☆ Good to knowSpace and computing (Jan 2026)

  • PSLV-C62 launched EOS-N1 plus 15 co-passengers incl. Lachit-1 (Northeast's first satellite; Assam Don Bosco University and Dhruva Space; named after Lachit Borphukan)
  • Pixxel-led consortium won India's ₹1,200 crore private national Earth Observation constellation (IN-SPACe)
  • NASA Artemis II: 10-day crewed lunar flyby (Wiseman, Glover, Koch, Hansen of CSA); targeted 6 Feb; Artemis III planned 2027 for lunar south pole landing
  • PARAM Shakti at IIT Madras: C-DAC PARAM RUDRA cluster under National Supercomputing Mission (MeitY + DST)

  • ICMR–NIV with Indian Immunologicals developing indigenous 2-dose KFD (Kyasanur Forest Disease) vaccine, 28 days apart
  • Addverb (Reliance 54%) wheeled humanoid Elixis-W; NEST cluster at IIT Guwahati ₹22.98 crore

7/19☆ Good to knowMilitary Quantum Mission policy framework

  • Released by CDS Gen Anil Chauhan; four areas: quantum communication, quantum computing, quantum sensing and metrology, quantum materials and devices
  • Sagar Maitri (SM-5): INS Sagardhwani (NPOL, DRDO) flagged off from Kochi to Oman, Maldives, Sri Lanka, Thailand, Malaysia, Singapore, Indonesia, Myanmar

8/19★ Must-learnPRAHAAR: national counter-terrorism policy (MHA)

In plain words

PRAHAAR is the government's new national counter-terrorism policy, built around seven pillars.

  • Prevention, Responses, Aggregating capacities, Human rights and rule-of-law processes, Attenuating conditions for terrorism, Aligning international efforts, Recovery and resilience (whole-of-society)

1/8★ Must-learnPadma Awards 2026 and gallantry awards

In plain words

The Padma Awards 2026 were announced, and Group Captain Shubhanshu Shukla, the first Indian at the International Space Station, got the Ashoka Chakra.

  • 131 Padma awards: 5 Padma Vibhushan, 13 Padma Bhushan, 113 Padma Shri; 19 women, 16 posthumous; 6 foreigners/NRI/PIO/OCI
  • Padma Awards instituted 1954; Padma Awards Committee headed by Cabinet Secretary; cap of 120 per year (excluding NRI, foreigners, posthumous)
  • Group Captain Shubhanshu Shukla (first Indian at the ISS) awarded Ashoka Chakra; 70 gallantry awards approved by President

Schemes at a glance

1/29★ Must-learnPM Vishwakarma scheme: benefits

In plain words

PM Vishwakarma supports traditional craftspeople with training, tools, loans without collateral and rewards for digital payments.

  • Identity card and certificate; skill training with stipend ₹500/day; toolkit incentive up to ₹15,000; collateral-free loan up to ₹3 lakh; digital transaction incentives

2/29★ Must-learnHow PM RAHAT works (arrow chain)

In plain words

After a road accident, one emergency call (112) starts a chain: hospital treatment, police confirmation and payment from a government accident fund, without the victim paying first.

  • Accident → dial 112 (ERSS) → nearest designated hospital → stabilisation 24 h (48 h if life-threatening) → police authentication on eDAR → TMS 2.0 (NHA) → claim approved by State Health Agency → payment within 10 days from Motor Vehicle Accident Fund (insured: insurers; uninsured/hit-and-run: Government)

Static links

3/29☆ Good to knowStatic links to the news

  • RBI: Utkarsh strategic frameworks since 2019; Payments Vision documents since 2001; inflation targeting began 2016 (4% ±2%)
  • ESIC: ESI Act 1948; scheme launched 24 Feb 1952; Central Excise Act 1944 (Central Excise Day 24 Feb)
  • Project Tiger launched Apr 1973 (Wildlife Protection Act 1972); Project Cheetah 2022 (world's first intercontinental large carnivore translocation)
  • NCDC set up 1963 (Ministry of Cooperation created 2021); NHB set up 9 Jul 1988 (regulatory powers to RBI 9 Aug 2019); NHAI Act 1988; Lead Bank Scheme 1969
  • GST/CPI: new CPI base 2024; GDP base 2022-23; IIP base 2011-12 (revision due); WTO formed 1 Jan 1995 (MC14 Yaoundé)

4/29☆ Good to knowAyushman Bharat: four components (Dec 2025 data)

  • AB-HWCs renamed Ayushman Arogya Mandir: 1,81,873 operational (30 Nov 2025); PM-JAY cover ₹5 lakh/family/year, 12 crore families, 42.48 crore cards
  • Ayushman Vay Vandana (29 Oct 2024): all aged 70+
  • PM-ABHIM ₹64,180 crore, launched 25 Oct 2021, FY22–FY26
  • ABDM (Sept 2021): four registries – ABHA, HPR, HFR, Drug Registry
  • Mar 2026 update: PM-JAY launched 23 Sep 2018; 434 million cards; family savings ≈₹1.25 lakh crore (2024-25); BE 2026-27 ₹9,500 crore; Arogya Mandirs 1,84,235 (27 Feb 2026); ABHA 863 million; PM-ABHIM BE 2026-27 ₹4,770 crore (total outlay ₹64,180 crore – confirmed by MoHFW; PIB March figure ₹32,928.82 crore = approvals to states)

  • Incentives for startups and MSMEs for chip design; 24 projects sanctioned; Chips to Startup (C2S) → 85,000 skilled people

  • Atal Bhujal Yojana: 25 Dec 2019; 7 states (Gujarat, Haryana, Karnataka, MP, Maharashtra, Rajasthan, UP); ₹6,000 crore (A ₹1,400; B ₹4,600)
  • Jal Shakti Abhiyan: Catch the Rain (22 Mar 2021); Mission Amrit Sarovar (24 Apr 2022; 1 acre, 10,000 cu m); 712 Jal Shakti Kendras

11/29★ Must-learnPM SVANidhi restructured (till 31 Mar 2030)

In plain words

PM SVANidhi gives street vendors small loans that grow in steps (₹15,000, ₹25,000, then ₹50,000) if they repay on time. The scheme now runs till March 2030.

  • Cabinet 27 Aug 2025: lending extended to 31 Mar 2030; outlay ₹7,332 crore; 1.15 crore beneficiaries (50 lakh new); launched Jun 2020
  • Tranches: ₹10,000 → ₹15,000; ₹20,000 → ₹25,000; third ₹50,000; UPI-linked credit card up to ₹30,000
  • MoHUA + DFS implement; "SVANidhi se Samriddhi" links vendors to 8 schemes
Exam angle

What is the first-tranche loan under restructured SVANidhi?

12/29☆ Good to knowFood processing schemes: PMFME and PLISFPI

  • PMFME: ₹10,000 crore, 2020-21 to 2025-26; 35% credit-linked grant, 2 lakh micro units, seed capital ≤₹40,000
  • PLI Food Processing: 169 applications approved; 50% reimbursement of overseas branding capped at 3% of sales or ₹50 crore; IFCI is project management agency

13/29★ Must-learnViksit Bharat–G RAM G Act, 2025 (replaces MGNREGA framework)

In plain words

A new law replaces the MGNREGA job-guarantee scheme: rural households now get up to 125 days of paid work a year (earlier 100), with costs shared between Centre and State.

  • Viksit Bharat–Guarantee for Rozgar and Ajeevika Mission (Gramin): 125 days guaranteed wage employment per rural household (was 100 under MGNREGA)
  • Centrally sponsored, normative funding: Centre–state 60:40; 90:10 for NE and Himalayan states; 100% Centre for UTs without legislature; annual need ₹1,51,282 crore (Centre ₹95,692.31 crore)
  • States can notify up to 60 no-work days a year (sowing, harvest); unemployment allowance after 15 days, state liability; wages weekly, max fortnight
  • Four priority verticals: water security, core rural infrastructure, livelihood infrastructure, extreme-weather works; Gram Panchayats execute ≥ half the works by cost; administrative ceiling 6% → 9%; social audit every 6 months
Exam angle

How many guaranteed days under VB–G RAM G and what is the funding ratio?

Remember

125 days; 60:40.

14/29☆ Good to knowLabour, skill and rural schemes (Feb 2026)

  • e-Shram: launched 26 Aug 2021; 31.48 crore workers registered (26 Jan 2026); One-Stop-Solution 21 Oct 2024; 14 schemes integrated
  • Skill India Programme (Cabinet 7 Feb 2025; FY23–FY26): PMKVY 4.0, PM-NAPS, Jan Shikshan Sansthan; Skill India Digital Hub Sept 2023
  • FPO scheme (2020): 10,000 FPOs, ₹6,865 crore; ₹18 lakh management aid; equity grant ₹2,000 per member (max ₹15 lakh); credit guarantee ₹2 crore

  • Budget 2026-27: DAY-NRLM ₹19,200 crore; RGSA ₹1,142 crore; PMGSY ₹19,000 crore; SHE-Marts proposed; Samaveshi Aajeevika Yojana (Apr 2023, ultra-poor graduation)
  • Sexual Harassment of Women at Workplace Act 2013: Internal Committee at 10+ employees; Local Committee at district; complaint in 3 months (+3); inquiry 90 days; fine ₹50,000; SHe-Box 29 Aug 2024

16/29☆ Good to knowVibrant Villages Programme-II; EEZ Access Pass

  • EEZ Fisheries Rules 2025 (4 Nov 2025): Access Pass free via ReALCRaft portal for mechanised and large (>24 m) vessels (≈64,000); coastline 11,099 km, EEZ ≈24 lakh sq km
  • DAY-NRLM: 10.05 crore women in 90.90 lakh SHGs; bank credit ₹11,10,945 crore; CLCC (25th meeting): SHG individual loans, UPI credit lines; MSE procurement policy: 25% target, 4% SC/ST, 3% women, 358 reserved items

17/29★ Must-learnPM-KISAN 22nd instalment; Kisan Credit Card

In plain words

PM-KISAN sends ₹6,000 a year directly to farmers, and the 22nd instalment was released in March. The card also gives Kisan Credit Card facts, the cheap farm-credit card.

  • PM-KISAN (launched 24 Feb 2019; central sector; ₹6,000/year in 3 × ₹2,000): 22nd instalment ≈₹18,640 crore released 13 Mar 2026 from Guwahati; 9.32 crore farmers (2.15 crore women); total ₹4.27 lakh crore; Budget 2026-27 ₹60,000 crore; Kisan e-Mitra chatbot 11 languages
  • KCC (1998): 7.72 crore active cards, ≈₹10.2 lakh crore outstanding; MISS crop-loan limit ₹3 lakh → ₹5 lakh; fisheries/allied limit ₹2 lakh → ₹5 lakh; collateral-free ₹1.6 lakh → ₹2 lakh (from 1 Jan 2025); effective rate 4% (7% less 3% prompt-repayment subvention); fisheries KCC extended 2018-19; Kisan Rin Portal Sept 2023; 457 banks onboarded
Exam angle

What is the effective KCC interest rate on loans up to ₹3 lakh?

Remember

7% − 3% = 4%.

18/29☆ Good to knowWomen and inclusion schemes (Mar 2026 PIB)

  • NaMo Drone Didi: ₹1,261 crore (FY24–FY26); 15,000 drones for women SHGs; 80% CFA up to ₹8 lakh; Lakhpati Didi: target 6 crore; SHE-Mart in every district
  • PM Mudra Yojana (8 Apr 2015): up to ₹10 lakh; new "Tarun Plus" category up to ₹20 lakh for repeat borrowers; PM Jan Dhan (28 Aug 2014): ₹2 lakh accident cover (accounts after 28 Aug 2018), overdraft ₹10,000
  • PMBJP (2008; PMBI): 25,000 Jan Aushadhi Kendras by March 2027; 20% margin; medicines 50–80% cheaper; National Vaccination Day 16 Mar (OPV first dose 1995); last polio case 13 Jan 2011 Howrah

19/29★ Must-learnIndia's Digital Public Infrastructure (DPI) snapshot

In plain words

India's Digital Public Infrastructure (UPI, Aadhaar, DigiLocker and others) is a set of public digital systems that other countries now want to copy.

  • DPI cooperation agreements with 24 countries; JAM trinity = Jan Dhan, Aadhaar, Mobile
  • UPI: ≈49% of global real-time payment volume (ACI Worldwide 2024); 81% of India's retail payment volume; 691 banks live; live in 8 countries (UAE, Singapore, Bhutan, Nepal, Sri Lanka, France, Mauritius, Qatar); IMF (Jun 2025) – world's largest retail fast payment system
  • Others: ONDC (2022); DigiLocker (2015); UMANG (2017); CoWIN (16 Jan 2021); eSanjeevani (Nov 2019); PM GatiShakti (13 Oct 2021); e-Courts Phase III ₹7,210 crore (2023–27); India Stack Global
Exam angle

In how many countries is UPI live and how many banks are on it?

20/29☆ Good to knowMaternal-child and women safety schemes (Mar 2026 PIB)

  • PMMVY 2.0 (1 Apr 2022, under Mission Shakti Samarthya): ₹5,000 in two instalments (first child); ₹6,000 after second child if girl
  • MMR 130 (2014-16) → 93 per lakh live births (2019-21); under-five mortality 48 → 28, neonatal 28 → 17 per 1,000 (2015–2023, IGME 25 Mar 2025); sex ratio at birth 918 → 929 (2024-25, HMIS)
  • Mission Shakti: Sambal (One Stop Centres, Women Helpline 181, Nari Adalat) and Samarthya (Shakti Sadan, Sakhi Niwas, Palna, SANKALP); Stand-Up India: women ₹10 lakh–₹1 crore, 7 years
  • Mission Indradhanush launched 25 Dec 2014 (immunisation of dropped-out children); UIP 1985, ≈2.67 crore newborns, 2.9 crore pregnant women yearly

21/29★ Must-learnMission for Aatmanirbharta in Pulses; farm finance schemes

In plain words

The government launched a ₹11,440 crore mission to make India self-sufficient in pulses (dal) by 2030-31, along with other farm credit and housing facts.

  • Pulses Mission (Dalhan Aatmanirbharta): ₹11,440 crore, 2025-26 to 2030-31; focus tur, urad, masoor; assured procurement via NAFED and NCCF under PM-AASHA; self-sufficiency by 2030-31
  • Agriculture Infrastructure Fund: 3% subvention on loans up to ₹2 crore for 7 years (rate cap 9%); PM Kisan Maandhan: ₹3,000/month pension at 60 (entry 18–40; 24.95 lakh enrolled); PM-KISAN women ₹1.01 lakh crore; NFSA 81.35 crore beneficiaries; 2026 = International Year of the Woman Farmer (UN)
  • PMAY-G (1 Apr 2016): 3.90 crore sanctioned, 2.99 crore completed (26 Mar 2026); min house 25 sq m; target 4.95 crore by 2029; FPO scheme ₹6,865 crore

22/29★ Must-learnRegional Connectivity Scheme – Modified UDAN

In plain words

Modified UDAN is the next 10-year version of the scheme that makes flying affordable to smaller cities, now also paying for helipads and new small airports.

  • Cabinet approved; FY2026-27 to FY2035-36 (10 years); outlay ₹28,840 crore (Ministry of Civil Aviation)
  • Components → 100 new airports from unserved airstrips (₹12,159 cr); O&M support 3 yrs (₹3.06 cr/yr per airport; 441 aerodromes, ₹2,577 cr); 200 helipads at ₹15 cr each (₹3,661 cr); VGF (Viability Gap Funding) ₹10,043 cr; 2 HAL Dhruv (Pawan Hans) + 2 Dornier (Alliance Air)
  • Original UDAN (Ude Desh ka Aam Nagrik) launched Oct 2016: 663 routes, 95 airports (28 Feb 2026), 3.41 lakh flights, 162.47 lakh passengers
Exam angle

Over what period and with what outlay was Modified UDAN approved?

23/29☆ Good to knowSchemes and portals (Mar 2026): PM-SETU, Smart Cities, RSETI, LaQshya, Gruh Sugam

  • PM-SETU (Skilling and Employability through Upgraded ITIs): ₹60,000 crore (Centre 30,000, State 20,000, Industry 10,000); 1,000 ITIs (200 hub + 800 spoke) + 5 NSTIs (Bhubaneswar, Chennai, Hyderabad, Kanpur, Ludhiana); ADB + World Bank co-finance
  • Smart Cities Mission (NITI Aayog evaluation): 93% of 8,063 projects complete; 100% ICCCs (Integrated Command and Control Centres)
  • RSETIs: 632 across 619 districts, 25 sponsor banks; age 18–50; 73 NSQF courses
  • LaQshya: 1,244 labour rooms and 917 maternity OTs certified (Feb 2026); needs ≥70% score; 8 areas of concern
  • NHB (National Housing Bank) Gruh Sugam Portal: home loans for defence/government personnel; Nasha Mukt Vidyalaya 3-year plan: 500 m drug-free zone around schools

24/29★ Must-learnJal Jeevan Mission extended to Dec 2028 (JJM 2.0)

In plain words

The Jal Jeevan Mission, which brings tap water to every rural home, was extended to December 2028 with a much larger budget.

  • Cabinet: extended to December 2028; total outlay ₹8.69 lakh crore; central assistance ₹2.08 lakh crore (2019-20) → ₹3.59 lakh crore (+₹1.51 lakh crore)
  • JJM – Har Ghar Jal launched Aug 2019; funds weightage: 30% difficult terrain (DDP, DPAP areas), 10% SC/ST-dominated areas
  • Reforms: Sujalam Bharat digital framework (unique Sujal Gaon/Service Area ID per village); Jal Arpan (handover with GP/VWSC); Jal Utsav (annual community maintenance event)
  • Coverage: 15.80 crore of 19.36 crore rural households (81.61%) with tap water; target all by Dec 2028
Exam angle

Till when has JJM been extended and with what total outlay?

Remember

₹8.69 lakh cr total · ₹3.59 lakh cr Centre · Dec 2028

26/29☆ Good to knowTribal, education and welfare schemes (Mar 2026)

  • DAJGUA (Dharti Aaba Janjatiya Gram Utkarsh Abhiyan): 2 Oct 2024; ₹79,156 crore; 63,843 villages; 549 districts; PM-JANMAN: 15 Nov 2023; ₹24,000 crore; 75 PVTGs in 18 States
  • NMMSS (National Means-cum-Merit Scholarship): ₹12,000/yr; 1 lakh fresh awards; parental income ≤ ₹3.5 lakh; 55% in Class VII; Pre-matric ST ₹2.5 lakh income cap
  • Sixteenth Finance Commission: ₹4,35,236 crore for rural local bodies 2026–31; Nirbhay Raho (Nirbhaya Fund, Panchayats); AMRUT Mitra 1,900 → 5,000 cities

  • Green Credit Programme (Green Credit Rules 12 Oct 2023; administrator ICFRE): credits after 5 years and 40% canopy density; ISA (launched 2015, HQ Gurugram, 125 members, "Towards 1000")

27/29★ Must-learnBHAVYA and Small Hydro Power scheme (Cabinet)

In plain words

Cabinet approved a scheme to build 100 ready-to-use industrial parks (BHAVYA) and another to support small hydropower plants, with rules on solar wafers.

  • BHAVYA (Bharat Audyogik Vikas Yojna): ₹33,660 crore; 100 plug-and-play industrial parks (100–1,000 acres); up to ₹1 crore/acre; external infra up to 25%; anchored by NICDC (National Industrial Corridor Development Corporation, DPIIT); challenge-mode selection
  • Small Hydro Power (SHP) scheme FY27–FY31: ₹2,584.60 crore; ~1,500 MW; plants 1–25 MW; CFA ₹3.6 crore/MW (max ₹30 cr/project, NE and border districts) or ₹2.4 crore/MW (max ₹20 cr, others)
  • ALMM List-III (solar ingots and wafers): effective 1 Jun 2028; initial list needs ≥3 independent makers with 15 GW combined

28/29☆ Good to knowAgriculture data and schemes (Mar 2026)

  • e-NAM (2016): 1,656 mandis (23 States + 4 UTs); 1.80 crore farmers; 4,724 FPOs; grant up to ₹75 lakh per mandi; Tamil Nadu most mandis
  • Horticulture 2024-25 (final): area 301.36 lakh ha; production 3,707.38 lakh tonnes (+4.51%); 2025-26 first advance 3,708.46 lakh tonnes; onion 307.67 lakh t (+26.79%)
  • Pulses Mission: 1,000 processing units; 489 focus districts; +35 lakh ha by 2030-31; PMFBY: claim not paid in 21 days → 12% interest; Coconut Promotion Scheme (part of ₹350 crore high-value agriculture); AIC Krishi Sakhi (2026 = International Year of the Woman Farmer)
  • FIDF (2018-19): ₹7,522.48 crore; 3% interest subvention; PMMSY ₹20,750 crore (2020-21 to 2024-25), accident cover ₹5 lakh
  • DPIIT-recognised startups: 2,12,283 (31 Jan 2026); 1,02,054 with a woman director/partner

29/29☆ Good to knowChild, nutrition and SDG items (Mar 2026)

  • Mission Vatsalya portal revamped (Khoya-Paya, TrackChild merged); Poshan Tracker; Poshan 2.0 six services; AWW internet ₹2,000/yr
  • MoSPI SDG Bulletins "Planet" and "Prosperity": RE share of capacity 16.02% (2015-16) → 22.13% (2024-25); waste recycling facilities 829 → 3,036; 97.2% schools with gender-segregated toilets; tourism 2.60% of GDP
  • Income Tax Department PRARAMBH 2026 awareness campaign: Income Tax Act, 2025 effective 1 Apr 2026; AI chatbot Kar Saathi

January–March 2026 · Section 10Revision75 questions

ESI, finance and business

  1. Base year of the new CPI series, and Jan / Feb 2026 inflation?
    Show answer

    Base 2024=100. Jan 2026 2.75%; Feb 2026 3.21%. Food weight 45.86% → 36.75%.

  2. New GDP series: base year and release date?
    Show answer

    Base 2022-23, published 27 Feb 2026; fiscal deficit 2024-25 revised to 4.9%.

  3. Which committee chair advised MoSPI on the GDP base revision?
    Show answer

    Prof B N Goldar (ACNAS).

  4. EPF interest rate for FY 2025-26?
    Show answer

    8.25% (239th CBT, chair Mansukh Mandaviya).

  5. Economic Stabilisation Fund corpus and purpose?
    Show answer

    ₹1 lakh crore; buffer for shocks from the West Asia war (₹57,000 cr fresh cash, ₹43,000 cr savings).

  6. Which Act was invoked to ration natural gas in Mar 2026?
    Show answer

    Essential Commodities Act 1955 (four-tier priority).

  7. Income-tax Act 2025: sections reduced from how many to how many?
    Show answer

    819 → 536 (chapters 47 → 23); rules effective 1 Apr 2026.

  8. HRA 50% exemption: how many cities now?
    Show answer

    8 (earlier 4): Mumbai, Kolkata, Delhi, Chennai, Hyderabad, Pune, Ahmedabad, Bengaluru.

  9. Minimum public shareholding: new categories and IPO offer?
    Show answer

    Six categories; 25% within 3–10 years; minimum offer 5% → 2.5%.

  10. IBC Amendment Bill 2025: what is CIIRP?
    Show answer

    Creditor-Initiated Insolvency Resolution Process (Chapter IV-A); ≥51% financial creditors; 150 + 45 days.

  11. India–France DTAC: new dividend tax rates?
    Show answer

    5% (holding ≥10%) and 15% (others); MFN clause deleted.

  12. India–GCC FTA: trade value FY25?
    Show answer

    $178.56 bn (15.42% of India's trade); Terms of Reference signed Feb 2026.

  13. Budget 2026-27: Khelo India Mission, Ministry outlay?
    Show answer

    ₹4,479.88 crore (Khelo India Scheme ₹924.35 crore).

  14. DPIIT start-up definition revised: deep-tech limits?
    Show answer

    20 years; turnover ₹300 crore (others ₹200 crore, 10 years).

  15. Gross market borrowing H1 FY2026-27 and share of the year?
    Show answer

    ₹8.20 lakh crore (51.0%) of ₹16.09 lakh crore; 26 weekly auctions; WMA ₹2.50 lakh crore.

  16. PLFS 2025: LFPR, UR and youth UR?
    Show answer

    LFPR 59.3%; UR 3.1%; youth UR 9.9% (10.3% in 2024).

  17. Modified UDAN: period and outlay?
    Show answer

    FY2026-27 to FY2035-36; ₹28,840 crore; 100 airports, 200 helipads.

  18. India–US interim framework: tariff and purchase pledge?
    Show answer

    18% tariff; $500 billion purchases (see FEB-045).

  19. RELIEF scheme: nodal agency and outlay?
    Show answer

    ECGC; ₹497 crore; 100% / 95% / 50% components.

  20. JJM extension: till when, total outlay?
    Show answer

    Dec 2028; ₹8.69 lakh crore (Centre ₹3.59 lakh crore); Sujalam Bharat.

  21. Exports Apr–Feb 2025-26; CPI and WPI for Feb 2026?
    Show answer

    $790.86 bn; CPI 3.21%; WPI 2.13%.

  22. BHAVYA: outlay and number of parks?
    Show answer

    ₹33,660 crore; 100 plug-and-play industrial parks.

Regulators

  1. RBI inflation target for 2026-31?
    Show answer

    4% with 2–6% band (1 Apr 2026 – 31 Mar 2031).

  2. Bank dividend payout cap?
    Show answer

    75% of PAT, linked to CET1; adjusted PAT = PAT − 50% of net NPAs; from FY27.

  3. RBI acquisition finance: key limits?
    Show answer

    Bank funding ≤75% of deal, debt:equity ≤3:1, net worth ≥₹500 cr, overseas syndicated cap 20%; now effective 1 Jul 2026.

  4. ECB limit after the 2026 amendment?
    Show answer

    Higher of $1 bn or 300% of net worth (earlier $750 mn); min maturity 3 years.

  5. When does 2FA become compulsory for digital payments?
    Show answer

    1 Apr 2026 (cross-border card-not-present: 1 Oct 2026).

  6. Draft rule on small digital fraud: compensation and reporting window?
    Show answer

    85% of loss or ₹25,000 (lower), once; report within 5 days; effective 1 Jul 2026.

  7. RBI Payments Vision 2028: how many initiatives?
    Show answer

    15 initiatives till Dec 2028; includes PaSS and shared liability framework.

  8. WMA limit for Apr–Sep 2026?
    Show answer

    ₹2.5 lakh crore; WMA at repo rate, overdraft repo +2%.

  9. Net open position cap for AD banks?
    Show answer

    $100 million (INR, onshore deliverable).

  10. RBI Digital Payments Index Sept 2025?
    Show answer

    516.76 (March 2025: 493.22); base Mar 2018 = 100.

  11. SEBI AI tool and what it tracks?
    Show answer

    Sudarshan: spots misleading finfluencer content (1.2 lakh posts removed).

  12. IRDAI: Ind AS 117 and cashless claim timelines?
    Show answer

    Ind AS from 1 Apr 2026; pre-authorisation 1 hour, final approval 3 hours.

  13. FDI in insurance: limit and Act?
    Show answer

    100% automatic route; Sabka Bima Sabki Raksha (Amendment of Insurance Laws) Act 2025 (in force 5 Feb 2026).

  14. PMFBY change: interest on delayed claims?
    Show answer

    12% if not settled in 21 days.

  15. CBDT APAs in FY2025-26 and total?
    Show answer

    219 (record); total 1,034; 84 BAPAs; safe-harbour margin 15.5%, threshold ₹2,000 crore.

  16. LBC investment: BO limit for automatic route?
    Show answer

    Non-controlling beneficial ownership up to 10%; 60-day decisions for specified manufacturing sectors.

  17. Duty Deferment (EMI): validity and turnover test?
    Show answer

    1 Apr 2026–31 Mar 2028; turnover above ₹5 crore; duty paid monthly.

  18. LTV for listed shares, equity MFs, debt MFs?
    Show answer

    60%, 75%, 85%; ₹1 crore cap; breach cured in 7 working days.

  19. CME ceilings for banks?
    Show answer

    40% aggregate; 20% direct; 20% acquisition finance; IPC CCF 100%, RW 125%.

  20. Dividend payout cap by bank type?
    Show answer

    75%: commercial, payments, SFB; 80%: LAB, RRB.

  21. e-mandate AFA-free limits?
    Show answer

    ₹15,000; ₹1 lakh for insurance, MF, credit card bills; 24-hr notice.

  22. KCC drawing limit formula (draft)?
    Show answer

    SoF × area + 10% + 20% + insurance; 6-year composite; CMPL.

  23. Multi Cap fund minimum allocation?
    Show answer

    75% equity; at least 25% each in large, mid, small cap.

  24. LEI and UTI length / UTI date?
    Show answer

    20 and 52 characters; UTI from 1 Jan 2027.

  25. Name of RBI strategy 2026-29?
    Show answer

    Utkarsh 2029: 6 pillars, 49 deliverables.
